# Checked and found closed

Every entry below was something an audit, plan or CLAUDE.md paragraph said was open, which a reader then checked against the code on 1-2 Oct 2026 and found done, answered or moot. It exists so a future session does not reopen them and so the audit documents can be deleted with their conclusions kept. Each entry names the evidence. The list is grouped by the source it came from; it is not exhaustive of everything ever fixed (CLAUDE.md is the record of that), only of what the sweep tested.

515 entries.

- POST-A-357 The register of people and companies (`entities` and its two relationship tables) is read by every member of the firm whatever clients the member is assigned to — Decided on 29-10-2026: the firm-wide read stays and the Entity Registry screen says so. Narrowing the read to the clients in reach would hide a person already known through another client from the entity picker and the cross-client match detection, which is what the register is for, and the signed-in role holds no write on any of the three (migration 562). `tests/test_the_register_says_it_is_shared_across_the_practice.py` holds the screen's sentence to the catalog category (`firm_wide`) the policies are checked against; narrowing the read later means changing both.
- POST-A-029 Decide if the unwritten GST, TDS and year-end E2E cycles (cycles 3, 4, 6 of the Phase 5.2 plan) matter — Decided on 29-10-2026: not written. The three cycles are held at the level where a defect shows: the GSTR-3B is tied out against the filed GSTR-1 (`test_the_gstr3b_is_tied_out_against_the_filed_gstr1`) and sets off against the credit ledgers it leaves (`test_a_gstr3b_sets_off_against_the_credit_ledgers_opening_balance`, `test_a_saved_gstr3b_records_the_credit_ledger_it_left`), the TDS deposit worksheet and the undeposited tax ask the challan register (`test_the_tds_deposit_worksheet_asks_the_challan_register`, `test_the_undeposited_tds_asks_the_challan_register*`, `test_a_recorded_tds_challan_closes_the_monthly_deposit_obligation_it_paid`) and the year-end finalise and reopen runs on real Postgres (`test_a_year_end_is_finalised_and_reopened_under_a_request_sign_in_pg`), beside the books-vs-ledger tie-outs that are product features and the 14 `test_e2e_*` modules; a fourth harness over the same cycles would restate them in a double that cannot see grants or CHECKs, which is the thing that cost this product its production incidents. Reopen it if a defect is found that none of these would have shown.
- POST-A-030 Decide whether the browser walk may run on every frontend pull request — Decided on 29-10-2026: no, as measured. A shared four-CPU runner takes 3 min 18 s to 3 min 30 s for the static export and 4 min 53 s (without the accessibility scan) to 7 min 51 s to 9 min 49 s (with it) for the walk, 14 to 16 minutes cold, over the 12-minute line, so the walk stays nightly, on dispatch and on the smoke-walk label: never a push trigger, never a `paths:` filter, never a required check. What would move it is a cheaper walk that scans only the screens a change touched, which needs a file-to-routes map nothing holds; that is a build with its own cost, not an open decision, and the ledger carries no line for it until a pull request is actually slowed or missed by the nightly.
- POST-A-149 Guard the remaining 'x && x.y.z.length' payload reads (about 21 sites) — Done on 30-10-2026, and not as the line expected: of the 28 reads found, 8 were already narrowed at their setter (BillsOfEntryTab, MonthlyReview, RegistrationsTab, CwipTab, ReconcileTab, the cash-flow forecast and the time-entry rate, twice) and one is component state set from a click (CsvImportModal), so the fix is at the NINE places the objects come from. The six document detail loaders and the six duplicate seeds the editors take return a detail only if it has its `lines`; the TDS deduction preview refuses an answer without `explain`; the TDS deposit state needs its `warnings` and two maps; the capital-gains exemption working needs its three lists; a fixed-asset category with no classes keeps its place with an empty list and `j.data ?? []` became `arrayOrEmpty`; the statement preview needs its `balance_check`. `scripts/a-nested-read-off-a-loaded-object-is-narrowed-where-it-is-loaded.test.ts` pins each source. NOT a general rule: a value a helper returns or a prop a page hands down is outside what `an-object-payload-is-not-its-fields-until-it-is-checked` can follow (it follows object state set straight from `.data`), and following it needs a data-flow walk this repository does not have.
- POST-A-097 Show how many balances sit on an 'Other' line by default — Done on 30-10-2026: `schedule_iii.residual_balances` counts, over the lines the statements present, the accounts whose caption `classify` reached by default (basis `residual`, on Other Current Assets, Other Current Liabilities or Other Expenses), the sum of their ABSOLUTE balances (a debit and a credit balance on one line do not cancel) and the split by caption; `build_schedule_iii` carries it as `residual_balances` for the reporting period, and the Schedule III mapping screen shows the server's count beside its "N of M accounts mapped". A nil that was read is said ("No balance sits on an Other line by default"); a count that could not be read is said to be unread and never drawn as a nil; lines `classify` could place on no caption at all or that carry no basis are named as `unjudged_lines` and are not in the count. It is of the reporting period, for one client when the screen's chooser names one and otherwise for the clients the caller can see (the mapping itself is firm-wide). Deliberately not done: the comparative column's own count. Not driven in a browser.
- POST-A-145 Convert the last 44 raw hex Tailwind classes to tokens — Done on 30-10-2026: all 44 sites in 16 files are token classes and `HEX_BUDGET` in `scripts/a-colour-and-a-type-size-come-from-the-token-file.test.ts` is 0, so a new `bg-[#...]` fails the guard. Mapped by role and not by nearest value: `#EFF6FF` (a highlighted option, a role chip, a quiet panel) is `brand-surface`; the three navies that were the hover step of a `bg-brand` button are `brand-dark` and the one that was a dark menu panel on the navy rails is `brand`; the amber and red ink of a note (`#92400E`, `#78350F`, `#991B1B`, each a darker private shade) is `state-attention` / `state-problem`, which stay above 4.5:1 on the surfaces they sit on (computed by hand, not measured in a browser); the executive dashboard's pale green, red and amber fills and borders are the `state-*-surface` / `-border` steps; the GSTR-3B page's near-white fills are `ps-bg` / `ps-surface`; the leftover indigo (the HSN library's bulk bar, a code chip, an invoice-editor note) is the selection affordance DataTable already uses. Two visible costs, named: the dashboard's two stat tiles are the lighter `-surface` fills where they were the `-hover` steps, and the global search's highlighted result is lighter (`#DBEAFE` to `#EFF6FF`, the combobox's own). Not driven in a browser; the nightly walk's accessibility scan covers the screens it visits.

Measured with the real SDK, a PAN in a logged line and a PAN in the query string of a call the server made both reached the event before; `tests/test_the_posting_failure_alert_has_its_tags.py` plants both and finds neither (it fails against the old setting). Recorded in `docs/design-record/deployment-and-operations.md`.
- POST-A-359 A section 197 lower-deduction certificate is written by the API only — Done on 29-10-2026 (#932, migration 564, applied to production by the deploy job after the web half of #930 was live): INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on `tds_lower_deduction_certificates` are revoked from PUBLIC, `authenticated` and `anon`, the migration closes with a check of the effective privilege and that `service_role` kept its writes, and the tab says a recorded certificate cannot be changed or removed. - POST-A-304 A row's client is not checked against its firm at the database — Done on 30-10-2026 (migration 565): a trigger on every table that carries a uuid `client_id` and `firm_id` (209 in a migration-built database) refuses a row that names a client of another firm, on insert (statement level, one join whatever the row count) and on an update that changes the client or the firm (row level, only when one of them changes); and on the 11 tables with a foreign key to `customers` and the 12 with one to `vendors`, a customer or vendor of another client. It refuses only what provably crosses a tenant (a NULL firm, a client that does not exist and a partitioned table are left alone), counts the rows that already break the rules in the apply report and changes none, and gives a table created later one call, `guard_client_firm`, which a migration-text guard requires. The member and portal-contact columns are the new line POST-A-361. Test: `tests/test_565_a_row_names_a_client_of_its_own_firm_and_a_party_of_its_own_client_pg.py` (24 cases, with the review's own case as a signed-in Partner and the same statement accepted with the trigger off). Recorded in `docs/design-record/tenancy-and-security.md`.
- POST-A-170 Tally importer's customer and vendor inserts are invisible to the schema-column test — Not so, checked on 30-10-2026: `domain/tally/migration_service._import_single_item` writes both through a literal table name and a literal dict, exactly as its comment says it must, and the column scanner (`tests/_backend_query_parser.scan_file`) reads all fourteen references (`firm_id`, `client_id`, `name`, `gstin`, `pan`, `email`, `address`, for each of `customers` and `vendors`) against the real schema. The line described what would have happened had the two branches been collapsed or the payload bound to a name, which is why they were not; the scanner's blind spots are counted by the budget in `tests/test_backend_columns_exist_pg.py` and are not a defect of this writer. Teaching the scanner to follow a payload name is a general improvement with no finding behind it and was dropped with the line.
- POST-A-356 Two columns default in the migrations and are NULL in production when a caller omits them (`client_timeline_events.actor_type`, `form_26as_uploads.status`) — Done on 30-10-2026, no migration, by naming the column at the writer and not by giving production a default. `client_timeline_events.actor_type`: every writer goes through `services/timeline_service`, whose `_row` always carries it (default "user") and whose `_stored` lists it among the keys it never omits, so nothing leaves it out and nothing stores NULL; no change needed. `form_26as_uploads.status`: `form26as_service.create_upload` left it out (the other writer, the TDS workspace's, names `reconciled`), so the same upload stored `uploaded` in a migration-built database (migration 052's default) and NULL in production; it names `uploaded` now, in both the real and the mock branch. Nothing reads the column on the pipeline's path (the 26AS page reads `parse_status`), so no reader treated NULL differently, and no existing row is changed.
- POST-A-161 Wire the engagement generate-obligations endpoint to a compliance screen — Decided on 30-10-2026, no code change: the compliance screens already generate obligations through the two doors that exist for them, `POST /api/compliance/obligations/generate` for the whole firm (the Practice → Compliance button) and for one client (`lib/data/compliance` passes a client id), and both run `generate_for_engagement` once per active engagement, which is what the per-engagement door runs. A second button for one engagement would be a second way to do one thing, and the per-engagement door is kept for the demo-firm seeder, which presses it, and for direct API use; it keeps `compliance:write` and the engagement's assignment scope. If a screen ever needs to generate for one engagement alone, wire it then.
- POST-A-012 Add a two-firm concurrent-traffic tenant-isolation test — Done on 30-10-2026: `tests/test_two_firms_calling_at_once_each_see_only_their_own.py` drives 120 requests from two firms, interleaved, through the ASGI app at once: the REAL `get_current_user` (only the token decode is replaced) over the real user and firm lookup on the in-memory database double, behind the real request-context middleware, with synchronous handlers that sleep in the thread pool so they overlap (the test measures the overlap and fails if there was none). It holds that every request's user, the firm bound to its context and the verified person bound to it are its own; that a customer list read through a real router holds only the caller's firm's rows; that naming the other firm's client gets an empty list or a refusal and never that firm's data; and that the 30-second user-lookup cache keeps two users apart. Negative control: with a process-wide "current user" variable simulated, four of the five fail. The ledger's open question, a load script outside tests/, was searched for and none exists. Not proved: the database's row security (the real-Postgres matrix does that) and a deployment with several workers (the caches are per process).
- POST-A-363 One test writes a temporary file into `tests/` while another lists that folder, so the parallel run fails now and then for no reason — Done on 31-10-2026: `tests/test_ci_runs_the_guards_that_read_the_browser.py::test_finding_nothing_is_a_failure_not_an_empty_run` now writes its copy of the selector script into a temporary tree at the same depth (`tmp_path/apps/api/scripts/ci/`, with an empty `tests/` beside it) and never into `tests/`, so `test_a_file_in_tests_is_a_test_or_a_named_helper` can no longer list a file that is deleted a moment later. Found while writing it: in `tests/` the script's own lookups (`parents[2] / "tests"`) resolved to a folder that did not exist, so the test passed by accident of the path, and patching the web-path pattern alone no longer empties the result (the script has a second way to recognise a web read); the empty tree is the honest version of "finds nothing". Negative control: making the script exit 0 on an empty result fails the test. No product code, no migration.
- POST-A-135 Triage the remaining ~60 unpaged, unbounded browser PostgREST reads — Done on 31-10-2026: every one was read. A scanner (`scripts/unboundedReads.ts`, quote-aware, a read is paged only inside an unclosed `selectAll(`/`selectAllKeyset(` call) found 57 reads in 42 files; 26 were paged (the client list every screen uses and four GST, payroll and report pick-lists that read it directly; the health dashboard's scores, the at-risk and critical lists and the alert lists; the MCA filings, companies and directors; the firm's income-tax notices; loans and fixed deposits, which embed the client and are walked by keyset; the two allocation modals, which list a party's documents OLDEST FIRST so the cut dropped the newest; a client's compliance calendar, oldest first for the same reason; a client's vendors, a vendor's bills, unresolved alerts and unreviewed matches), and the 31 left, in 27 (file, table) entries, are each named with the reason they cannot outgrow the ceiling in `scripts/an-unpaged-browser-read-is-named-with-its-bound.test.ts`, which fails a new unbounded read, a paged or removed one still listed, and a count that changed. The existing paging guard gained 20 (screen, table) pairs. Found on the way: that guard (and 173 others) removed comments with a regex that treats a `/*` inside a line comment as the start of a block, so a comment saying "not the /api/health/* routes" hid 200 lines of HealthDetailClient, and 50,000 characters of the API client, from every rule that reads them; the paging guard now uses the shared quote-aware stripper, and the rest is the next change. Not done: the same triage for the offset pager's ordering (every paged read carries a unique id tiebreaker, held by the existing guard). No product behaviour changes for a firm under the cap.
- POST-A-364 Guards that strip comments with a regex lose the code after a line comment that contains a block-comment opener — Found and done on 31-10-2026 while closing POST-A-135: about 170 guards remove comments with `.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, "")` and a per-line `//` strip, and that regex cannot tell a block comment from the two characters inside a LINE comment, so a comment reading "not the /api/health/* routes" opened a "block" that ended at the next real close and every code line between vanished from every guard that reads the file (200 lines of HealthDetailClient and of EntityDetailClient, 187 of the client tax page, parts of the API client, the portal dashboard, the onboarding checklist and the GST data layer). Nothing failed: a guard that cannot see the code it is asked about passes. Ten comments in nine files were reworded to say the path in words, and `scripts/no-comment-or-string-hides-code-from-a-guard-by-opening-a-block.test.ts` now holds the hazard itself (no line comment and no string in a web source file contains the opener; one known string, the image file input's accept attribute, is listed with its reason and must stay the only one), with the scanner tested on snippets and the hazard shown to be real. Making the hidden code visible found two things, both fixed: an unchecked payload assigned straight to a list in EntityDetailClient (`setMatches(matchesJson.data)`, now `arrayOrEmpty`), and 18 named palette colours (5 and 13 in the two screens) that the colour ratchet had never counted, so its budget moves 3,585 → 3,603 as a CORRECTION of the measurement with the reason written beside it; it may only come down from there. Not done: moving the other guards onto the shared quote-aware stripper (a mechanical sweep of about 170 files; the rule above stops the cause from coming back, and a guard that wants the stronger reading can import it). No product behaviour changes.
- **POST-A-365** (closed 31-10-2026) — The guard that every mounted route has a way in from a screen counted a route whose path ends in a parameter as reached by any longer URL beneath it, so `GET /api/tds-workspace/challans/{challan_id}` read as reached because the retract call's URL starts the same way (found when the new retract URL moved that prefix's budget from 1 to 0 with no screen reading one challan). `_pattern` now makes the last parameter one path segment (a template expression, a run with no slash, or an id concatenated on after a closing quote) and a test pins both directions; sixteen routes that no screen calls came out, and the budgets of twelve prefixes are RAISED to their true counts with the routes named beside them (approvals 1 to 2, engagements 1 to 4, health 2 to 3, mca-workspace 2 to 3, payments 0 to 1, purchase-cycle 3 to 4, purchase-payments 0 to 1, receipts 0 to 1, sales-cycle 2 to 3, tds-workspace 0 to 1, workflows 10 to 13, year-end 10 to 11). Not done: wiring a screen to, or deleting, any of the sixteen; a URL built by concatenation that adds a suffix after the id (`"/x/" + id + "/y"`) still reads as reaching `/x/{id}`, because a concatenation cannot be told apart from a plain one.
- **POST-A-123** (closed 2-11-2026) — Add a browser test tier with double-click tests for money screens. The tier exists as `apps/web/scripts/drive` (stubbed server, Chromium, run nightly after the walk; the run's own summary line is the count of scenarios) and every flow the finding named is driven with a same-tick double dispatch that must write ONE record: a journal post, an invoice created and issued, a receipt and its allocation, an asset added, an account added, a payroll run, and the purchase-bill editor's Save Draft (`doubleDispatch.mjs`, added 2-11-2026 after a review found it missing, with the typed rate read from the request and compared whole to 118000 paise, so a tenfold error fails); typed amounts are asserted as exact paise (`amounts.mjs`). GROUPED TEXT IS REFUSED AND NOT PARSED, and that is the decision: the finding's assertion that `1,25,000` is sent as 12500000 paise is obsolete. `paiseFromRupeeInput` returns null for it and `lib/money/rupeeInput.test.ts` holds that; the drive cannot reach the refusal in the two Record Payment boxes, which are `type=number` fields where Chromium drops a typed comma. A comma read wrongly in money entry is a hundredfold error, so refusing with a sentence is the safe side; accepting validly grouped Indian commas can be reopened if a CA asks for it. The scenarios for the asset, account, allocation and payroll flows each fail when the guard under them is broken. What stays is practice, not a gap: a new money-writing screen gets its scenario in the commit that adds it, and the drive is not a required check (PRE-A-015 owns that).
- **POST-A-361** (closed 3-11-2026) — Eight writers of a person column with no key, and three columns no writer is seen for, were not traced to the id they write. All traced. The guard (`tests/test_a_bare_person_column_takes_the_id_it_holds.py`) now follows a value through every call back to where it is made (up to twelve hops; a nested helper's outer function; a call made on a declared factory's result; a call made on something it cannot name when the function's name is defined once; rows collected with `.append`), so `UNREAD` is empty and the posting kernel's `posted_by` is proven through its whole caller graph (58 origins, every one the internal id, one deliberate empty); the three writer-less columns are proven through the functions that build their rows (`BUILT_ELSEWHERE`) and the two member routes through the lookup that checks their path parameter (`CHECKED_AS_A_MEMBER`). Three independent read-only investigations agreed with the mechanical result for every column. Three real defects were found on the way and fixed: the copilot chat attributed its model call to the SIGN-IN id although `ai_usage_events.user_id` keys `users.id` (in production the two ids differ for both users, so the row would be refused and silently lost, and a firm's monthly AI cap would not count the chat); the trusted-rule sweep posted with the rule owner's INTERNAL id in `audit_log.actor_id`, which takes the sign-in id; and four audit writes fell back from the sign-in id to the internal one when the sign-in id was missing. DECIDED: the nine `users.id` columns do NOT become keys now. Every writer is proven and held by a test that fails before a wrong one merges, a runtime key would turn a wrong id into a refused posting in production instead, and a key on `journal_entries.posted_by` (13,073 live posted rows) is a validation scan on the most-written table in the product for a defect the guard already prevents; revisit if a writer appears that the scan cannot read (a payload built elsewhere). What this found and did not fix: POST-A-376 (postings made by a person with no poster) and POST-A-377 (fifty-nine columns with no rows).
- POST-A-092 (first half, 3-11-2026) — Closed in code: a sales invoice's issue posts its entry and marks the invoice issued in one database transaction (migration 577, `issue_sales_invoice_atomic`, locking the draft and refusing a second simultaneous issue), so a failure between the two can no longer leave an entry behind a draft; a draft an earlier issue stranded is finished by issuing it again (the posting reuses the entry only if it carries the very figures); the Sales Invoices tab lists drafts already in the ledger and issued invoices with no entry, and posts the entry of the second kind on a click. Proved on a real Postgres with a forced failure after the posting, two simultaneous issues, a foreign entry and a closed year. The stock side of the same gap stays open under POST-A-092.
- POST-A-092 (stock side, 3-11-2026) — Closed in code except the three limits named on its ledger line: a document issued with its stock effect incomplete is found and completed. `resume=True` on the four stock functions writes only the goods lines with no stock row and journals the rest in one entry that must carry the figures the rows hold; migration 579 adds the finder; the client's Inventory tab lists the documents and offers Complete the stock after a question; the half-recorded multi-line document and the edit of an opening balance that was not applied now say so. An independent review of the first version found seven problems (a closed year was not asked, a journal with other figures was stamped onto the rows, a bill's freight was marked spent by a receipt that did not post, rows were matched to lines by item alone, a hand adjustment collided with the button, the refusal sentence was swallowed by the panel, a non-id was a server error); each is fixed and has a test that fails without it.
- POST-A-272 (3-11-2026) — Closed in code: the last open part, the bank-queue proposal, is built. A bank debit equal to the total of exactly one recorded, untied TDS challan paid within a week of the line is proposed as its payment (a fifth draft source, `challan`, migration 578), never ready, on the ledger the challan's section clears; passing it posts through the ordinary bank posting and then ties the challan through the challan's own door; a tie that cannot be made never undoes the pass. The challan's journal link (migration 568, 573), the take-back, the stale-link reading and the firm-wide list of challans tied to nothing (30 Indian days, a product default) were closed earlier. Proved on a real Postgres for the source check; the pass is held against the in-memory database, not a live bank.
- POST-A-265 (3-11-2026) — Closed in code: an amendment build no longer reads the same things once per earlier filed period. The rebuild of a filed period leaves out the ledger reconciliation nobody reads (the heaviest read of the build), and a read memo shares the client-wide reads and reads the documents of the whole span once. On a counting double over a seeded client with six filed months the build made 19 reads instead of 105, and the answer is identical with and without the memo. The periods already ran four at a time (1-11-2026). NOT measured on the live deployment, which this container cannot reach: the saving is in reads, not in seconds.
- POST-A-208 (parts 2 and 3, 3-11-2026) — Closed in code: the risk register lists an unfiled advance-tax instalment once (the Advance Tax Default; the generic Overdue Filing row for the same client and date is held out), and the capacity forecast takes its settled pair from the register's rule instead of a second copy. Part 1 (moving the trackers and the register's read onto the obligation store) stays open on the ledger with the facts read this day.
- **POST-A-128** (closed 3-11-2026) — Finish spreadsheet-style keyboard entry in journal, bill and bank editors. The journal and the bill editor were done on 31-10 and 2-11; the bank's entry dialog (hand-built, with three more dialogs opened from inside itself) is the shared Modal now, which stacks (one Escape closes only the top dialog) and takes a footer, driven in Chromium (`bank-dialogs`). The last check the line asked for, a five-line balanced journal entered with typed values, Tab and Enter alone, was driven and FOUND a hole: a ledger picker closed by Enter or Escape dropped focus to the page, so a hand that tabs and types lost its place after every account. The picker now returns focus to its button when a choice is made, on Escape and on Tab (not on a click away), and the scenario (`journal-keys`) posts one entry whose debits equal its credits; the build before the picker fix fails it.
- **POST-A-372** (closed 4-11-2026) — The sale and purchase ledger entries written before entries named their document are stamped by migration 580. Read-only on production on 4-11-2026 before it was written: 5,658 Sales and 759 Purchase entries with no source, each named by exactly one invoice or bill of the same firm and client (one Purchase entry is named by none), 1 vendor-payment entry naming its kind and not which payment, and 6 reversals (4 Purchase and 1 Sales of an entry the file stamps, 1 Purchase of the entry no bill names). The file stamps an entry only where one document of its own firm and client names it and the type matches and no other live entry of that type already carries the document (a document's cost-of-goods journal shares its source and is the ordinary shape, 5,645 of the 5,658, so the test is on the entry type); gives the payment entry its payment; hands each reversal its original's source; changes only the two source columns; writes one edit-log row per entry itself; and ships a rollback that undoes only the entries those rows name. 15 real-Postgres tests; 22 mutants of the migration and the rollback (a join that ignores the client, an ambiguous document accepted, no guard against a second entry of the type, a rewrite of another column, a reversal across clients or of a different kind, a rollback that undoes what it did not name, a file that never clears the allowance) were each caught. An independent read of the files, with a 98,000-entry trial on a copy (apply 10 seconds, rollback 9), found no defect; its observations led to a probe that proves the file itself clears the edit allowance (the old test could not fail on that), tests for the purchase side, the payment guard and the reversal edge cases, and a corrected header. Read on production before writing: step 3 reaches exactly five reversals (four Purchase, one Sales). NOT verified: the run on production itself (read the warning line of the apply job and the counts again after the merge).
- **POST-A-376** (closed 4-11-2026) — A posting a person makes is stored with that person as poster and creator. Sixteen helpers that build a document's entry (plus the bank one) take an optional `created_by` and forward it to the kernel; 26 callers pass the internal id (`current_user.get('id')`, `(actor or {}).get('id')` in the receipt and payment services, the caller's `actor_id` in the capital work in progress service); the depreciation helper takes it from both routes, and its compensating reversal posts as that person instead of nobody. The one caller left without a person is the bank service's `post_transaction`, which nothing calls, and it says so with `created_by=None`. A new rule derives the helpers from the service and fails a missing parameter, a dropped forward and an omitted or literal-None caller; the existing person-column trace follows every origin to a name that is the internal id (90 of 91, the other being the dead bank method; it reads an `(actor or {}).get('id')` by the name `actor` and not by where that dict was built, which is its limit; it needed the payroll router's local `svc` renamed, because the same name stands for another module elsewhere in that file). Negative controls: a caller without the person, a helper that drops it and a caller handing the sign-in id each turn a test red. No migration. NOT verified: a real posting through the API with a real database; the 6,420 older entries are unchanged on purpose.
- **POST-A-371** (closed 4-11-2026) — A credit or debit note that corrects a price or a discount no longer moves stock. The note says whether its goods came back (`goods_returned`, migration 581): yes moves stock as before, no moves nothing and the response says so, and issuing a note that names goods without saying is refused in a sentence before anything posts. The stock functions and the retry obey it, the finder of unrecorded stock (migration 579's function, replaced with one more condition) never lists a note that says no, and both editors ask the question when a line names a good and show the stored answer. Read-only on production first: 2 credit notes and 2 debit notes, none naming an item. 15 mock-mode tests and 6 real-Postgres tests; negative controls (the function ignoring the answer, the router not refusing, the column written when unstated) each caught; 7 web tests. NOT verified: a note issued end to end in a browser against a real stack.
- **POST-A-131** (partly closed 4-11-2026; the line stays for what is left) — Bill photo, icons, offline page. The purchase bill's upload offers Take photo on a touch device; the manifest names PNG and maskable icons; the iOS icon is linked; an offline page (`public/offline.html`, one file that loads nothing) is shown by the service worker (v5) for a page navigation whose fetch fails. Verified in Chromium: Page.getInstallabilityErrors empty and the manifest read without errors; with the network cut, a navigation to an uncached page shows the page with its inline icon; a worker that served the redirected copy as it came (Cloudflare Pages redirects /offline.html to /offline) failed that navigation with ERR_FAILED, which is why the page is stored and served as a plain response; the old SVG-only manifest raised no installability error in desktop Chromium either (the ledger had it unconfirmed; Android is not tested). An independent read found the photo row overflowing at 390 px (Extract pushed off the box, the filename invisible) and the offline page's icon never cached (a broken image when shown): both fixed and re-measured (the row now fits at 390, 360, 320 and 844 px), and three regressions that survived the first tests (an online navigation never trying the network, a 404 stored as the page, a refused cache write failing the install) are now each caught. 17 node tests run the real worker in a sandbox; eight mutants of it were each caught. NOT verified: the camera on Android Chrome, Lighthouse on the live site, a real phone.
- **POST-A-322** (closed 4-11-2026) — Drop compliance "System B"'s table `compliance_tasks` and what hung from it. Migration 582 refuses to run if the table holds a row, drops the view `compliance_entries` and the constraint `filings_compliance_task_id_fkey` if they exist (the `filings` column stays), and drops the table with no CASCADE; its real-Postgres test rebuilds the before state and runs it over an empty table, a table with a row, a table with an unlisted dependent view and a database with none of it. Production read the same day: 0 rows, one view, one constraint, nothing else depending on it. The view was over the table in a migration-built database too (016), which the earlier ledger line had wrong. Refreshing the 3-9-2026 production fixtures is POST-A-379.
- **PRE-C-007** (raised and withdrawn 4-11-2026) — Read why the marketing site's Cloudflare build fails. The Cloudflare Pages check for the marketing site (project practicesync) reported a failed build on two pushes (about 21:18 and 22:13 IST) and passed on the next (23:10 IST) with no change to apps/marketing, which also built clean in this container; it was transient on Cloudflare's side. The id is retired and not reused.
- **PRE-A-015** (closed 6-11-2026) — Drive the money editors in a browser: double-click guard, keyboard, drafts, dates. Done in eighteen slices between 9 October and 6 November 2026, in Chromium against the smoke export (`apps/web/scripts/driveMoneyEditors.mjs`, its scenarios in `scripts/drive/scenarios/`, run nightly on a GitHub runner after the walk): a same-tick double click on every control that writes, the ledger-row keyboard, drafts, deletes and prompts, amounts as paise, and every date box of a money, tax or payroll write form, which is 142 of the 182 date boxes the guard `the-money-editors-are-driven-in-a-browser.test.ts` counts, in 65 files (it names every date box of each driven file and fails a new one with no row; the other 40, in 23 files, are POST-A-386), each under en-US/Los Angeles, en-IN/Kolkata and en-GB/Kiritimati at a pinned clock, and the days that have happened also at an hour when the browser is a day ahead of India and one when India is a day ahead of UTC. The eighteenth slice drove the last ten: a recurring invoice template's start and stop days, an export's shipping bill date, a foreign supplier's no-PE declaration date, a product's opening-stock date and the catalogue importer's, the section 234A working's furnished-on day, the stock register's as-at day and an item's ledger window. The ledger line had said that three of them were the last money write forms; the guard's own count of what was left found two more (the catalogue's), so the line was not closed until all five were driven. Driving them found a shipping bill date and a no-PE declaration date after today kept, and text that is not a day for either reaching the database and coming back as "Unable to … Please try again."; a recurring template that took text that is not a day as a 500, could be edited into a stop day before its first, and had its manual run's day read by the same parser; a stock ledger window in the wrong order answered "No stock movements… Held 40 throughout"; and a stock register heading that printed its as-at day as ISO text. Independent reads of the first commit then found a shipping bill date left in a hidden box that would have been sent and refused, the last press's answer left on a supplier form opened again for the same vendor, a template whose window-order sentence stayed after the window was put right, and a drive scenario that failed on correct code.  The rules the slices established are in `docs/design-record/screens-and-site.md`. **Everything the line's chronicle still held as found and not changed was read out of it before it was deleted (an independent read of the whole line; 34 findings had no other home) and is now on the post-demo list: POST-A-386 to POST-A-397** (the remaining date boxes, other browsers, dates cleared on an edit, Section 197 (S27), the import dialog, refusals that change nothing on screen, refusal wording, dates accepted without a check, three tables a signed-in session can write, dialog hygiene, drive coverage and the nightly drive's length); the statutory point is S27 on the owner's list. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026 by the guard's own enumeration of `DateInput` elements._
- **POST-A-397** (closed 6-11-2026) — The nightly browser drive did not fit its 45-minute allowance and now does, with the allowance unchanged, and it now stops itself before the allowance. The run that started at 06:31 IST on 6 November 2026 was cancelled at the limit, 28 minutes into the drive, with the drive step still running and no drive report. The drive had grown one slice of the date group at a time (216 runs on the night of the 4th, 342 on the 5th and 840 on the 6th, the last counted from the tree that night built) and nothing measured it; that night the browser install alone took 8 min 46 s against 19 s on the night before. The runner's own step times give what a run costs, 4.92 s for a date run and 1.71 s for any other (216 runs took 9 min 18 s and 342 took 19 min 38 s), so the whole drive, 1,048 runs on this tree (891 of them the date group, which runs under three zones), is about 77 minutes. **A night now drives ONE zone and ONE half of the date group, taken in turn by the INDIAN day** (`--zone rotate` in `driveMoneyEditors.mjs`, the rule in `drive/kit.mjs`): the zone changes every day and the half every third day, so over any six consecutive nights every zone sees every date scenario once and none is left out; the other groups run in full. That is 315 or 320 runs and about 18 minutes of drive. **The allowance was not raised**; it is the owner's money. **And the drive stops itself (`--stop-after`).** The first idea, a report written as the drive goes, would not have saved the report: GitHub's own record of the cancelled run shows the drive step `in_progress` and the step that uploads the report `pending` when the job ended, 55 minutes after it began against an allowance of 45, because a job that times out is cancelled and its later steps never run. So the runner is given a deadline (24 minutes for a night, 98 for the whole matrix), after which it starts no more scenarios, writes its report with the runs it did not drive named, and exits 1: the job goes red, uploads the report and ends inside its allowance (the part before the drive took 16 min 26 s on the 6th and 8 min 8 s on the 5th, a scenario in flight may take 90 seconds more, the steps after the drive took 4 seconds: 17 + 24 + 1.5 + 1 = 43.5 of 45). The whole matrix is an on-demand choice (Run workflow, zones: all) with a cap of 120 minutes that only a person asking for it by name is given; the other choice is the night's part, which is the default and what a pull request with the label runs, and a single zone is not offered because it could not fit 45 minutes (about 31 minutes of drive after the longest setup seen). A run in progress is no longer cancelled by a newer one unless it is a pull request's: the nightly arrives hours after its schedule and would have cancelled an on-demand run of the whole matrix in the middle. **The key is the Indian day, not the UTC day**, because GitHub runs the job hours after its 02:00 IST schedule (its drive step began between 05:10 and 08:08 IST in the eight nights before this), across UTC midnight (05:30 IST): read as UTC days, the same eight start times drove America/Los_Angeles on two nights running and left Pacific/Kiritimati undriven for five. The report is also written after each scenario and names the zone, the part, the deadline and the instant the part was chosen at. The runner takes `--list` (print the runs it would drive, with no build or browser) and `--now` (read the rotation at an instant), so that its own use of the rotation is tried by spawning it; `--zone=rotate` is read as well as `--zone rotate`, and a flag it does not know, a value on a flag that takes none, a missing or empty value, a flag given twice and a bare word are each refused with exit 2, because a run that ignores what it was given drives something other than what was asked. `the-nightly-drive-fits-its-allowance-and-visits-each-zone-in-turn.test.ts` (23 tests) holds the rotation over three years (month ends, year ends and 29 February 2028) and over those eight real start times, the runner's lists night by night against the rule (six nights list every run of the drive), that choosing a zone or a part never removes a scenario, the arguments, the report, the deadline arithmetic (setup 17 + deadline + a scenario in flight + the steps after, inside the allowance, for a night and for the whole matrix), the workflow's wiring (one schedule a day, 45 minutes, 120 for the whole matrix only, `rotate` as the default, cancellation for a pull request only) and the estimate of a night as a BUDGET of 21 minutes (the deadline less a margin of 3): it fails ONE run when the drive has grown past that and says what to do (cut it further, or ask the owner for a longer allowance); 56 mutations of the product are each caught by the assertion named for them. An independent read of the first version found the report-upload gap above, that deleting the runner's own use of the rotation left every test green, a single-zone choice that could not fit the allowance, the cancellation of an on-demand run by the nightly, and statements that were not true (the slowest setup seen, the run count of the 6th); each is fixed here. **The ledger line's own proposal, one fixed zone nightly (Asia/Kolkata) and all three on Sundays, was not taken**: one zone with the whole date group is about 31 minutes of drive, which with the 16 minutes before the drive on the cancelled night is 47 minutes, over the same allowance, and the Sunday run of all three (77 minutes) needs a longer one, which is the owner's money. Not verified: a real nightly with the change (the workflow's expressions were read against GitHub's tables, parsed as YAML and the runner was driven against a real export with a deadline, but GitHub has not run them; what GitHub's record of the red night of 30 October does show is that the steps after a drive step that FAILED run and upload the report, so a stop that exits 1 is the case that works). By the rule the nights of 7 to 12 November drive Asia/Kolkata (part 2 of 2), Pacific/Kiritimati (part 2), America/Los_Angeles (part 1), Asia/Kolkata (part 1), Pacific/Kiritimati (part 1) and America/Los_Angeles (part 2); the drive's report (`mode.zone`, `mode.shard`) and the run's summary page name what a night drove. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._ **Changed 7-11-2026 (POST-A-399): the date group is cut in THREE parts, not two** (`SHARDS` in `drive/kit.mjs`). The ten browser scenarios that change added run every night and took the worst night to an estimated 21.1 minutes, over the 21 that its 24-minute deadline leaves; the guard's own remedy is to cut the group finer, so a night is one zone and ONE THIRD, the rotation takes nine nights, and the worst night is about 16.5 minutes. Each date box is now visited every nine nights instead of every six. The allowance was not raised.
- **POST-A-390** (closed 6-11-2026) — The shared import dialog's "Import N rows" could import a file twice. `CsvImportModal` awaits the screen's own import call and moves to its "importing" step only after the call has started, so two clicks in one tick started two imports of the same file, on the ten screens that share the dialog (customers, vendors, products, employees, bills, notes, journal and opening-balance files). A master is protected by the server's name, GSTIN and PAN checks (the second copy is reported as skipped); a bill, a note or a journal is not obviously idempotent. The button now takes the repeat-click flight the money screens use (`useSingleFlight`, held until the import settles), the one raw button of its kind that the repeat-click scan had named "NOT harmless" is off that scan's table, and the browser drive presses it twice in the same tick on the vendors screen (`csv-import/two-clicks-on-import-in-one-tick-import-the-file-once`) and once (the file's row is what the request carries). Controls: on the build of main before this change the two-click scenario fails (1 of 2 runs of the group fail, each on its own check: two bulk requests) and the one-click scenario passes; on the final build all 2 pass. Not verified: the other nine screens (they share the component, and the vendors screen stands for them), and Firefox or Safari.
- **POST-A-383** (closed 6-11-2026; the draft case it left is POST-A-398) — The challan amendment door could switch off the section 143 job-work clock with no date, stamp a goods-back day on a cancelled challan, leave a back day beside a status that says the goods have not come back, and take a whole-challan back day earlier than goods already returned. `update_challan` wrote `status: "received_back"` as it was sent, and `itc_04.balances` skips a challan in that status, so one amendment stopped the clock for goods that never came back and named no day; it also stamped a day on a cancelled challan, which the FORM GST ITC-04 lot door refuses (409), and accepted a whole-challan day before a lot already recorded against the same challan. Now the status "received back" is refused without a day, in the amendment or already on the row (422, one sentence in `domain/gst/delivery_challan.py`, which names the lot-by-lot route only for a job-work challan, because the lot door refuses every other reason); a patch that stops the clock on a cancelled challan, or leaves one cancelled with a back day, is refused (409) in the sentence the lot door says (now one function the two doors share), judged on the status the patch LEAVES and asked before the missing day, so cancelling and giving a day in one request, and the two-step route to the same end state, are closed; a back day is never left beside a status that says the goods have not come back (422); a whole-challan day earlier than the latest lot of goods RETURNED against this challan (not another challan's, another client's, or a row that records only scrap, which migration 462 says this product takes no judgement on) is refused (422), the same day being allowed; and an amended day the goods went out is compared with the days already recorded as back whether or not the patch also gives a back day. A draft is deliberately NOT refused, on a premise that is a test over the whole API tree, the migrations and the front end: no screen issues a challan, so a draft is the state every challan made in the product is in and the whole-challan "Record goods back" is the one path that ends its clock (the gap is POST-A-398). Held by `tests/test_a_challan_amendment_cannot_stop_the_clock_without_a_day_or_before_a_lot.py` (25 cases; mutations of the new behaviour each turn one red). An independent read found the cancelled-in-one-request bypass, the scrap row, the sent-day pair, a premise test that missed a write through a handle variable, a single-quoted table name, a database function and the front end's other call shapes, and advice that pointed a sale-on-approval challan at a door that refuses it; each is fixed here. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-388** (closed 6-11-2026; the same shape on every other optional box is POST-A-399) — A date cleared on an edit was kept, and the screen said the edit was saved: the shipping bill date, the Form 15CA date, a supplier's no-PE declaration date and a bank account's opening-balance date. The four edit screens sent an emptied box as `date || undefined` (the bank account's as `null`) and the PATCH routes read their body with `exclude_none`, so nothing was sent for it, the stored date stayed and the screen said saved. A shipping bill, a Form 15CA acknowledgement and a signed declaration are evidence, so the screens now REFUSE the Save rather than offer to remove one: pressing Save over a box that held a date when the form opened (or when it was last saved) and is blank now says "The shipping bill date on file can be corrected but not removed: enter the corrected date." (the Form 15CA date and the opening balance date likewise, and the no-PE declaration date with "or untick the declaration if there is none") and sends nothing. The sentence is `lib/dates/removedDate.removedDateProblem`, derived from the date on file and the box and said through each form's `useDateRefusal` latch, never copied into the form's error; text that is not a day keeps the box's own sentence; a box that is not drawn (the Form 15CA block only for a payment previewed under section 195, the shipping bill block only for an export) is not one that can be emptied; the two long editors bring the sentence into view, because their Save is in the toolbar and the sentence is drawn at the foot; the invoice editor's record of what is on file is what was SENT (the shipping bill fields go only while their block is drawn), so a date typed into a block that was not drawn at a save is never reported as on file; and the bank modal says its own, older sentence in preference to this one when a balance is typed. The server's contract is unchanged: an omitted date is unchanged and a blank one is refused on an update. Driven in a browser in all three locale and zone pairs (emptied and refused with no request and the sentence inside the window, a second press, text that is not a day, a corrected date sent as ISO; a date left alone sent as it was on all four screens; the supplier form opened again; unticking the declaration; the bank modal with a balance typed; 33 runs), 18 of which fail on the previous product; the source guard `an-edit-screen-does-not-say-saved-over-a-date-it-was-asked-to-remove.test.ts` states the rule per screen and 19 mutations of it are each caught by the assertion named for them. An independent read found the snapshot recording a date the server never received, the two sentences at once in the bank modal, the sentence drawn out of sight in the long editors and a wording that implied a place where the date could be removed; each is fixed here. NOT done: the same shape on every other optional box (POST-A-399), which includes the Form 15CA acknowledgement number and UDIN and the shipping bill number and port code beside the dates, being texts; and the declaration's own `no_pe_declaration_by` is never written, so the register's gap about it cannot be cleared (POST-A-401). _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-385** (closed 6-11-2026; the same shape in other screens is POST-A-400) — After a lot was recorded on FORM GST ITC-04 the panel was unmounted by the reload, and its success line was never seen. `Itc04Panel.record` says "Recorded. That was the last lot — the challan is now marked received back and its s.143 clock has stopped." and calls `onChanged`, which is the sales cycle tab's `load()`; that set `loading` on every read and the tab returned "Loading…" in place of itself, so the panel, the line it had just said, the year and window the person had chosen and any other open lot form were lost, and the person saw the list change without being told it had worked. The tab now shows "Loading…" for the first read of a client's lists only: a ref records which client the lists belong to and is set only when a read came back, a failed read (first or later) resets it so "Try again" blocks again, and a tab reused for another client does not show the last client's lists while the new ones load; a reload asked for while an earlier one is in flight is answered by the last question, not the last answer. **Keeping the tab drawn also removed the one thing that had been re-reading the panel after the tab's own writes (its remount), which a review found: the panel would have shown a statement that no longer matched the challans after "Record goods back" on a whole challan or a new job-work challan.** The panel now takes a `refreshToken` prop that the tab bumps when a reload has landed, and reads again when it changes (a prop and not a key, which would unmount it again). Driven in a browser in all three locale and zone pairs (`itc04-the-last-lot-recorded-keeps-the-panel-drawn-and-its-success-line-is-seen`, where the line is on the screen after the reload, and `itc04-marking-a-whole-challan-back-reads-the-panel-again`, where the goods of a challan marked back stop being listed; 6 runs, all passing). On the build of the tree before this change the first fails in all three pairs (the line is not on the screen) and the second passes (the remount was what re-read the panel). Held in source by `the-sales-cycle-tab-stays-drawn-when-it-reloads.test.ts`, 19 mutations of which are each caught by the assertion named for them. Not verified: that the second scenario FAILS on a tab that is kept drawn and does not tell the panel (no such build was made; the source guard states that rule). NOT done: the other screens with the same shape are POST-A-400. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-391** (closed 6-11-2026) — Nine writes on a client's MCA, TDS and GST compliance pages dropped the server's answer, and the app had a tenth. Each was `await apiFetch(…)` with nothing reading what came back: a director's KYC change and a filing's status change (MCA); the creation of a TDS return, a TDS return's status change and the save of a statement computed from the books (TDS); the creation of a GSTR-1 and of a GSTR-3B and the save of each computed from the books (GST); and the client payroll page's removal of a professional tax registration. These routers answer a refusal as an HTTP 200 with success false or as a 4xx, so a refused row action changed nothing on screen and looked like a button that does nothing, and a refused create or save closed the form or the dialog as though it had saved: a CA believed a return existed that did not. They read their answers now. A row action says the server's sentence in an alert above the table, leaves the row as it was and does not read the list again, and the sentence goes when the next press begins and when the list is read again; a create or a save says it inside the form or the dialog, beside the button that raised it, which stays open with what was typed or computed in it, holds its Cancel while the save is out, and starts clean when it is opened again; a request that fails outright says so in the same place and leaves the screen usable. (The GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B creates used to put a thrown error in the list's load-error row, behind the dialog; that row is the list's again. The payroll removal accepts only a success: its helper hands back nothing for a body that is not JSON, and `success === false` read that as accepted.) The guard `the-forms-on-a-clients-compliance-tabs-read-the-answer-and-take-their-dates-from-a-date-box.test.ts` had counted the dropped writes (nine, a count that could only fall); it is now a RULE over the whole app, no `await apiFetch(…)` write anywhere (with or without a type argument) drops its answer, and each of the ten has its checks by name: the answer read straight after the call, the refusal branch saying the sentence and returning with nothing before it that closes the form or reads the list, a failed request said and closing nothing, the last sentence cleared when a press begins and when the list is read again, the flag held across the request reset in a `finally`, Cancel held while a save is out, the sentence drawn as an alert inside the control that raised it; 40 mutations of it are each caught (the independent read of the first version found mutations the guard then let through, among them a form closing above the refusal check). Driven in a browser (`compliance-answers`, 17 scenarios: each of the ten gone wrong three ways, success false, a 404 whose body is a detail and an answer that is not JSON, then accepted, then Cancel and the form opened again): all 17 pass on the final build and 11 fail on the previous product, each on its own check (the refusal is not said), the accepted ones passing on both; the existing client compliance scenarios (`dates/client-`, 153 runs) pass on the final build. The sentences the stubs speak are the routers' own, pinned in `tests/test_the_drives_filing_sentences_are_the_servers.py` (4 new tests). One defect the independent read found beside the diff is fixed here: on the TDS compute dialog, changing the return type after a statement was computed left the old figures on the screen and the save sent the new type, so a 26Q's figures could be stored as a 24Q statement; the three boxes that choose the statement now empty the result and are held while a compute or a save is out. Not done and written down: POST-A-402 (the TDS approval's refusal names a flag the screen cannot send) and POST-A-403 (the compute dialogs have no catch). Not verified: Firefox, Safari and a phone width; the real server (the stubs were compared with the routers by reading them and by calling `_could_not` and the sentences' sources, not by running the routes). NOT done: writes made through a helper that is not named `apiFetch` (the rule reads that name, so it says nothing about them). _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-443** (closed 15-11-2026) — A signed-in session could write the TDS register, the TDS returns, the TDS challans and the TDS certificates over PostgREST, past every check the routes make: migration 345 left `authenticated` INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE behind policies that ask the firm, the client, the role tier and the internal practice, and production also granted TRUNCATE (`anon` too). Found while designing POST-A-078. Done in two parts, an hour or more apart because the order matters: part 1 moved every writer (15 sites, 12 of them moved) to the privileged client, naming the firm and the client, with the person's permission, the client and (on the three doors that name a row) the internal practice asked by the route, and a source guard that counts the write sites, holds that every update and delete names both, and holds the browser to a single read; part 2, migration 593, revokes INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE from PUBLIC, `authenticated` and `anon` on the four tables and checks the effective privilege and that the service role kept its writes, with its rollback, the four names on the closed-tables list and an entry in `docs/design-record/tenancy-and-security.md`. Tested: the migration against a real database, 45 cases (583's shape, the rollback as the negative control); the guard fails against the code before part 1. Not verified: the production database was not touched from the session (the migration applies when it merges), and the Render deploy of part 1 cannot be observed from here, so the order rests on the time between the merges.
- **POST-A-394** (closed 6-11-2026) — Three tables a signed-in session could still write over PostgREST: the e-invoice records, the e-way bill records and the section 140A challans. `authenticated` held INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE on all three (`anon` held TRUNCATE), behind a firm policy and the assignment scope, so a hand-made request wrote an IRN record, an e-way bill record or a challan that no route would have written (the routes ask the day a portal printed, whose invoice a record names and the duplicate). Done in two parts, an hour or more apart because the order matters: part 1 (merged 6-11-2026, 16:10 IST) moved the seven writers of the two record tables to the privileged client, naming the firm and labelled with the person of the request, with a source guard that counts the write sites; part 2, migration 583, revokes the four verbs from PUBLIC, `authenticated` and `anon` on the three tables and checks the effective privilege (table level, column level, through PUBLIC) and that the service role kept its writes, with its rollback, the three names on the closed-tables list the general writer rule and the migration-built-database check read, and an entry in `docs/design-record/tenancy-and-security.md`. Tested: the migration against a real database, 35 cases (the privileges for each table, an assigned Manager refused by the privilege and still reading, the service role running the same statements, the worst case of everything granted to everybody, a grant that cannot be revoked failing the migration, idempotence, and the rollback bringing the writes back as the negative control). Not verified: the production database was not touched from the session (the migration applies when it merges), and the Render deploy of part 1 could not be observed from here (the API host is refused at the egress proxy), so the order rests on the gap in time. The browser-reachable tables that remain are decided one by one in POST-B-014. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-393** (closed 6-11-2026) — Six dates were accepted with no check at any layer, or started from the browser's day instead of India's. (1) A tax audit recorded as completed or filed could carry an audit date, and one recorded as filed a filing date, that had not come: the tracker writes the table from the browser and nothing checked the two dates, which the ITR acknowledgement's door refuses for the same kind of fact. The decision taken: a status that says the act is done makes its day a statement of fact, and an audit not yet done may carry a PLANNED day (the boxes are labelled only "Audit Date" and "Filing Date"). It is enforced in the database, by migration 584 (a trigger on `tax_audits`, India's day, asked on insert, on a status change and when the day itself changes, so a row that already carries such a day is not locked by an unrelated edit), because the browser writes the table directly and a trigger is the one layer every writer meets and can word its refusal. The migration takes a lock timeout, revokes the trigger function from the signed-in roles (a trigger does not ask its caller for it) and reports, as a warning, how many rows already carry such a day, changing none. The screen asks the same question first, bounding the audit date for completed and filed and the filing date for filed, and shows the trigger's own sentence for a writer it did not see. The screen is deliberately STRICTER than the trigger on a row that already carries such a day: it judges what the form holds, so a person who opens a legacy row to correct the UDIN is asked to put the day right first, while a hand-made request that edits the UDIN alone goes through. (2) The stock transfer's date started from the browser's day and starts from India's, and so do the three as-at days the inventory page hands its stock panels and the dates of a stock adjustment and of an NRV write-down; a transfer dated after today is still accepted, as the line said (a movement planned for tomorrow is not obviously wrong). (3) The ITC-04 job worker's challan date was a hint on the box, with nothing behind it: the lot door now refuses a challan dated after the day the goods came back (422, one sentence in `domain/gst/itc_04`, the plain order of events with no statutory reading), which also bounds it by today, since that day cannot be after today; the box makes a later day a problem the form is held for and is judged again when the came-back day changes, whichever of the two was typed first. (4) The Schedule III ageing's As at started from the browser's day, and a blank box or text that is not a date was READ: the server answers a missing date as at today, so today's figures stood under a box that said nothing; a blank or unreadable box is now not read, nothing from the last date stays on the screen (a slower answer for a date the box no longer holds is dropped), and a note says why. (5) The Form 15CA / 15CB fields of a purchase bill are sent only when the block that asks for them is drawn (the server says the bill withholds under section 195). (6) The overdue-interest panel remembers the date its table was worked out for and holds "Prepare draft invoice" while the table is not the box's date (the read for the new date is out, or failed), says why in words once a read has failed, and drops an older date's answer that arrives last. Tested: the source guard 8 tests, 64 mutations of the screens each caught; the lot door's rule 6 tests, 3 of which fail on the previous code; migration 584 against a real database 23 tests, with the clock FAKED at 01:30 IST on 9 October (the 8th in UTC and in Los Angeles) and at 22:30 IST on the 8th under three session zones, so a function reading UTC's date, the session's zone or `CURRENT_DATE` fails (four such mutations of the migration are each caught by the database proofs alone), and the unlocked legacy row, the warning, the revoke, the idempotence and the rollback putting the gap back; the error mapper 5 tests; driven in a browser (12 runs of eight scenarios, two of them under three locale and zone pairs): all pass on the final build, 10 of the 12 fail on the previous product (the ageing ones because its box did not yet have a name to find) and the five that guard behaviour fail on a build with the four defects put back (the ageing and interest answers drawn in arrival order, the bill's block following the last reading, the date box not noticing a moved bound), each on its own check; the whole drive was run on the final build as three pieces, one per zone. Three independent reads, each of the version before: the first found two existing guards gone red, a source pin the migration's own header satisfied, the late-answer races, the held reason shown only in a button's title and the challan box that did not hold the form; the second found the remount dropping unreadable text (fixed at the root, in `DateInput`), a banner that outlived its cause, a stale closure reloading the old date's table, the 15CA entry dropped when the reading failed and tests that could not fail; the third found that the 15CA rule still hid the block on the next edit after a failed reading and when its last character was deleted (now the block follows the server's settled answers), a guard that let a mutation of the new date-box effect through and some overclaiming comments; all are fixed. Not done and written down: the class the screens belong to (POST-A-404, 94 call sites in 52 files); the job worker's challan date is not compared with the day the goods went out, and a tax audit's filing date before its audit date is a hint in the box and not a refusal (neither was asked); the Tax Audit tracker is still written from the browser (POST-B-014 decides per table whether a browser-written table gets an API door). Not verified: Firefox, Safari and a phone width; migration 584 on production data (nothing is changed or backfilled; the trigger is asked only on a write that touches the status or the day). _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-401** (closed 6-11-2026) — The register's "declaration nobody dated or attributed" gap could never be cleared: nothing wrote `vendors.no_pe_declaration_by` (migration 311's column), so a CA who ticked a supplier's no-PE declaration and recorded its date and reference, which is what the gap's sentence asks, was still told on every section 195 bill that nobody had recorded who obtained it (POST-A-388's account above says the column was never written; it was true then). The decision taken: the column holds the signed-in member whose save RECORDED the declaration in this product (the internal user id, which is what the column references), stamped by the server and never sent by a caller (a forged key in a create, a bulk item or an edit is dropped by the models, and a test sends one through each). `domain/tds/no_pe_declaration.declarant_write` decides it for the three doors that write a supplier: a request that leaves the declaration ticked and dated and either changed it or found it unattributed names the member; one that carries none of the three fields never writes the column; one that un-ticks the declaration or leaves it with no date empties the name (a name on a declaration that is not on file attributes nothing); and a save that leaves an attributed declaration exactly as it was keeps its declarant, because the supplier form sends all three fields on every save and a person who edits a phone number must not take the declaration over. No row is changed by a migration or a sweep (migration 311: backfilling would be inventing evidence): a supplier recorded before, with a ticked and dated declaration and no member, is named by the next save of its form that carries the declaration, by whoever makes it and for whatever reason, which is the first moment a person puts a name on it and is not evidence that they obtained the paper (said in the module, and POST-A-407 is the open item to show who is named). The register's gap sentence now says what is recorded ("no member has recorded it here"), not "who obtained it". Tested: 20 tests (the rule, the three doors in mock mode, a forged key through each, and the end of it: a declaration recorded through a door clears the gap on the next section 195 bill), 6 of which fail on the previous router. Two independent reads: the server read found the wording of two claims stronger than the code, the gap sentence naming something the column does not hold, the consequence for an old supplier above, and test gaps (the forged key, a role below Manager); all are fixed. Not verified: against a real database (the column and its foreign key are migration 311's and were not touched). _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-402** (closed 6-11-2026) — A TDS statement the product holds back (no deductees recorded, or its quarter not yet over) could not be recorded as CA approved or filed from any screen. The status door refused it unless the request carried `acknowledge_incomplete`, and its sentence ended "Resubmit with acknowledge_incomplete=true to record it anyway", an instruction nothing in the web app could follow: a CA who had filed a statement on the portal that this product held no deductees for (a shell made with "New Return", or a quarter whose books are kept elsewhere, which is the commonest shape for a firm running beside Tally) could not record that it was filed. The decision taken: a second confirmation the CA gives, never automatic and never on a first press. The door says the sentence for a person ("This return cannot be recorded as CA approved because it has no deductees recorded.") and answers that one refusal with a marker (`data: {can_record_anyway: true, incomplete: [...]}`; every other refusal still answers no data); the client's TDS tab and the firm-level returns page (the approval, and the filing inside its dialog) draw the server's sentence as a question, announced to a screen reader, with "Record it anyway" and "Leave it as it is" (`components/tds/RecordAnywayAsk.tsx`), and only the first sends the flag; and the audit row of a statement recorded over what was incomplete says so (`recorded_over`, with what it was) so "approved" is never read as "approved complete" (`log_event` never raises, so a failed insert is logged and not reported to the person, as for every audited change). Held from the side that reads the screens: the flag is sent from two files only and only inside `recordAnyway ? { acknowledge_incomplete: true } : {}`; the handlers wired to a button take no parameter (the shared Button hands its click to a handler, and a click is truthy: a handler that took "record it anyway" first would have sent the flag on the first press); the refusal becomes a question only on the server's marker and with its sentence. The independent reads of the first version found, and this version fixes: the date rule ("cannot have been filed before the quarter ended") was waived by the flag, so two presses on a statement with no deductees recorded a filing dated before its quarter ended, which cannot be moved back (it is now asked of every filing whose quarter has ended, ahead of the question, and the flag waives it only for a quarter that has not ended); the approval gate asked the role template and not the person, so a Manager whose approval of TDS the Team grid had switched off still approved, filed and recorded over a refusal (it asks the person now, as the MCA door does); the firm page's approval question outlived its statement (it names the statement it was asked about, is drawn only while that one is on screen and goes with a changed client, form, year or quarter, a changed PRN, acknowledgement or date, and a list read again), was not announced to a screen reader, sat at the top of the page far from its button (it is above the actions now), and the filing question gave no sign that its request was out and left Confirm Filed and Cancel live (the dialog is held while the request is out); the dialog was 424 px tall and 602 with the question, so on a screen under about 650 px its bottom buttons left the page (it scrolls now); a stale error stood over a new question; and the same defect as POST-A-403 sat in the GSTR-1 tab's mark-filed (below). Tested: the door, its audit row and the person it asks 19 tests (the file imports what the previous router does not have; the seven added after the reads each fail on the first version where they can), and the drive's copy of the sentences and marker is compared with the server's (1 test); the source guard 8 tests; 52 mutations of the screens, the helper and the guards' own files, 52 caught; driven in a browser (4 scenarios: the client's tab; its refusals that offer no way on and a confirmation the server refuses; the firm-level page's approval and filing, on a 440 px screen; and a refusal that arrives after its statement has gone): all pass on the final build, all 7 of the 7 runs of this item and of POST-A-403 fail on the previous product, the first version fails three of the 7 on their first new check (the question not announced, on both pages, and the late refusal drawn as a question; a separate probe at 440 px showed its Cancel unreachable with the question showing), and a build with two defects put back (the first press of CA Approve sending the confirmation; the firm page's approve handler taking the click as the confirmation) fails the three scenarios of the question and the late-refusal one, each on its own check. Looked for and not found: another refusal that tells a person to send a flag (the only other override in the API, `acknowledge_stale` on a GSTR-3B, is not named in its sentence, which says to recompute). Not done: the 26Q built from the books still carries no typed deduction, so a statement recorded over this refusal is the CA's own record and the product's figures stay what they were (POST-A-078); the dialogs were driven at 1280 px wide and 440 and 900 high, not on a phone. _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-403** (closed 6-11-2026) — The compute dialogs of the client's TDS tab and its GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B tabs had no catch: a request that fails outright (a dropped connection, or a proxy's error page that arrives as a 200 and is not JSON, which `res.json()` rejects) skipped the line that cleared the flag and left the dialog on "Computing…" with Compute held until the page was loaded again. They are reads (nothing is saved), which is why POST-A-391, about the writes, did not take them. Each has the shape the saves have: the request in a try, the sentence the thrown error carries said in the dialog as an alert, and the hold released in a `finally`. The source rule is extended twice: from the three to the app (a handler that holds `computing` releases it in a `finally`, or a promise chain's `.finally`), and, on the independent read, from the name `computing` to the nine flags a hold is taken with (`computing`, `marking…`, `saving`, `busy`, `posting`, `submitting`, `running`, `generating`, `approving`, `filing`): the widened rule found the same defect under another name in the same file, the GSTR-1 tab's mark-filed (its status change did not catch a thrown request, so the dialog stayed on "Recording…" with Record as filed and Cancel held until the page was loaded again), which is fixed here, and no other. Tested: guard 2 new tests; driven in a browser (3 scenarios, one per dialog: an answer that is not JSON is said as an alert, Compute is live again and not stuck, a second press computes and the sentence goes): all pass on the final build and all three fail on the previous product (the click threw and the dialog stayed on "Computing…"). A hold released after the try instead of in a `finally` is not something a browser can see while the catch stands, so that placement is held by the source rule alone, with its mutations. Not done and written down: the GSTR-3B tab's row actions release their hold but do not say a thrown request (POST-A-405), and a thrown request is said in the browser's own words at about thirty sites (POST-A-406). _Counted from the code on 6-11-2026._
- **POST-A-382** (closed 7-11-2026) — Fourteen functions of `lib/data/income-tax.ts` read a refusal as `json.error ?? "Failed to …: <status text>"`, so a request model's own refusal (a BSR code of six digits, a date after today sent by another caller) or any 4xx showed the CA "Unprocessable Entity" over the sentence the server wrote; `lib/data/tds.ts`'s `authedFetch` passed on a string `detail` and left a validation list as it was, and `lib/data/gst.ts` threw the JSON of the `detail` (a sentence inside quotation marks, a list as brackets). The income-tax module has ONE reader of an answer now, `answerOf`, under two names (`readAnswer` for a call that changes nothing, a stateless estimator included, and `writeAnswer`): a failed status is read by `lib/api`'s `errorMessage`, the one reader of it, and the body by `jsonPlain`. All twenty-three requests of the module, the worksheet reader and the two AIS readers go through it; the GST helpers go through `gstAnswer`, and `authedFetch` reads a validation list, or a page under a failed status, through `errorMessage` too. `errorMessage` also drops pydantic's label ("Value error, ") in front of a model's sentence, which is the first of POST-A-392's seven, and says a gateway's error page under a failed status as an error page and not as its markup.
