Tested: the guard `a-failed-request-is-said-in-the-products-words` (the three data modules call no `fetch`, read no `Response.json()` and no status line; a function that makes a request reads it through the module's reader; a change is never called a read; `errorSentence` and `request()` use the helpers), the helper's unit tests, and the browser drive (a 422 whose detail is a sentence, and one whose detail is a list with the label, are said as the sentence on the advance-tax screen; the previous product fails it). Not changed: the screens that still read their own answer are POST-A-410.
- **POST-A-405** (closed 7-11-2026) — The GSTR-3B tab's row actions, Validate and CA Approve, Mark as filed, Recheck, Recompute from books and Delete, held their row in a `finally` and caught nothing, so a request that threw (a dropped connection, a proxy's page that is not JSON) left a click that ended in silence: the button came back and nothing said what had happened, which on Record as filed is the act that locks the period. Each has the catch the GSTR-1 tab's status change has, said through `failureSentence` (a change says that it is not known whether it went through; Recheck, which never writes, says that no result came back), and the filing dialog says it inside itself and stays open with what was typed. Driven: six scenarios, one per action, against a page that is not JSON: the sentence is on the screen, the button is live again, the row is where it was, and a second press sends a second request and takes the sentence away; the previous product fails all six. Guard: the four functions that hold a row each catch into `failureSentence`, and no `try` that makes a request on the four compliance pages lacks a `catch`. Found, not changed: the box that carries the sentence on the GST tabs is not an alert region (POST-A-411).
- **POST-A-406** (closed 7-11-2026) — A request that failed outright was said in the browser's own words: `Failed to fetch` for a dropped connection (Firefox says `NetworkError when attempting to fetch resource.`, Safari `Load failed`) and `Unexpected token '<', "<html>…" is not valid JSON` for a proxy's page that arrives with a 200, at the saves and loads of the client compliance pages, the three compute dialogs, the income-tax screens, the report downloads and the public page a client opens to sign an engagement letter. One module, `lib/api/failureSentence`, says it, and what the request WAS decides the sentence: a read says that no result came back, a change says that it is not known whether it went through and to reload the page to see whether it was recorded, and never that nothing was saved, because a connection that dropped may have dropped after the server had written. `fetchPlain` and `jsonPlain` throw it at the source, so every screen that prints `e.message` is right without being edited, and `failureSentence` says it where a screen catches something it did not read itself (the auth client's token fetch among them). It is used by `request()` and its uploads and downloads, the income-tax, TDS and GST data layers, the four compliance pages' readers and thirty-nine of their catches (the nine others are bare by design: a read that falls back to "unknown" or to a "could not load" note, a best-effort timeline note, a paste and a file read, and none of them says a fixed sentence over a change), thirty other files that read their own answer, among them the public signing page; a failed report download and a gateway's error page under a failed status are said the same way. The guard fails a file that calls `fetch` itself unless it is one of three that must or is on the list that only shrinks (six files, POST-A-410), a compliance page that prints a caught value's message, and a change called a read. The drive's `/JSON/i` expectations (the compliance answers and the held-back statement's compute dialogs) now expect the product's sentence, pinned word for word to the module's by its unit test. A dropped connection cannot be driven (the runner does not allow the console error it leaves), so the words each engine uses for it are held by the unit test, and the page that is not JSON stands for it in the browser. Found, not changed: POST-A-411 (the GST row sentences are not announced), POST-A-412 (`request()` sends a write a second time after a dropped connection, and POST-B-279's facts said it never retries).
- **POST-A-413** (closed 7-11-2026) — A screen that reads or writes through the Supabase client was told `TypeError: Failed to fetch` when the connection dropped (Firefox: `TypeError: NetworkError when attempting to fetch resource.`, Safari: `TypeError: Load failed`): the client does not throw, it wraps what `fetch` rejected with as "<error name>: <error message>" and hands it back as `error.message`, and 63 lines in 35 files throw such an error or print its message (the lines that match `error.message`, `throw error` or `throw x.error` under `app/`, `components/` and `lib/`). The browser's client is made in one place, `lib/supabase/client.ts`, and is now given a `fetch` (`lib/supabase/plainRestFetch`): when the browser reports that a request to PostgREST got no answer, it answers in the server's place with a 503 whose body is the error PostgREST itself sends and whose `message` is the product's sentence (a read says that no result came back, a change that it is not known whether it went through and to reload the page to see whether it was recorded). So `error.message` is the sentence at every one of those places without editing them. Three things the independent read of this change found and this change did: the client returns a PLAIN OBJECT, not an Error, so a screen that holds it and says it through `failureSentence` lost its sentence (it fell to its fixed fallback); the error carries a code now (`PS_NO_ANSWER`) that `failureSentence` recognises, and says it for what the SCREEN was doing; and a connection that dies after the headers arrived is worded by the client as `TypeError: network error` with a status of 0, so the body is read inside the wrapper and the client is handed the answer rebuilt or, if the body never came, the same sentence. It answers with a response and not a throw because the client puts whatever is thrown behind its prefix, and because a 503 keeps the client's own policy for a dropped connection: it sends a GET or a HEAD again up to three times, one, two and four seconds apart, and never sends a change twice (a unit test runs the real client with fake timers: four attempts for a read, one for a change). Left alone: sign-in (`/auth/v1/`, whose screens read their own errors), a request somebody cancelled or that ran out of time, and a rejection that is not the browser's report of a dropped connection. Tested: eleven unit tests of the wrapper, most of them against the real client (postgrest-js 2.118.0), one more of the code's recognition in the sentence module, and the guard (the client is made once, with this fetch, and no second place makes one). Not tested: a dropped connection in a browser (the runner cannot drive one). Found, not changed: POST-A-414.
- **POST-A-412** (closed 7-11-2026) — `request()` sent any request a second time, five seconds after a dropped connection, a write included, on the comment's reasoning that nothing had been processed; a rejected `fetch` does not show that, because a connection can drop after the server has written and before the answer arrives, and then the second request recorded an invoice, a receipt or a challan again (nothing keys a create, and the posting kernel dedupes only on client, reference number and entry date). Now only a read is sent again; a change that got no answer says that it is not known whether it went through and to reload the page to see whether it was recorded. The cost is the one the line named: the first change of a session after the server has slept can fail where it used to succeed once the instance woke; the keep-awake ping keeps the instance up in a signed-in tab, and an always-on server tier (the owner's money decision) removes it. A POST that only asks (a preview, a compute) is now not sent again either, which is POST-A-414's second half. POST-B-279's facts are corrected again (the code no longer sends a write twice; a CA's second press still can, which the idempotency-key decision is about). Tested: the guard reads the source (`request()` cannot be imported by a unit test); a dropped connection cannot be driven.
- **POST-A-411** (closed 7-11-2026) — What a refused or failed row action says on the GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B tabs of a client's compliance page was drawn in a plain paragraph and a plain box, so a screen reader was not told that Validate, CA Approve, Recheck, Recompute or Delete had been refused; both now carry `role="alert"`, as the MCA and TDS tabs' row sentences do. Kept as it was: the look (the GSTR-3B box is amber, the GSTR-1 one red; choosing one tone for a refusal is a design decision this did not take). The six GSTR-3B scenarios of the failed-requests drive find the sentence by its role now, and the guard reads both elements' role from the source. Not tested with a screen reader.
- **POST-A-410** (closed 7-11-2026) — The last six files that called `fetch` themselves are converted and the guard's list of them is empty. `lib/invoices/shared.ts` (the invoice screens' request helpers) reads through `fetchPlain` and `jsonPlain`, and the purchases page, which held a copy of both closures that read a refusal its own way (a validation list with its field names, a body that is not JSON printed whole), uses them: one refusal reads the same on every screen that was once one, and a refusal with no sentence of its own says that the server did not accept the request when it is a 4xx and is told to try again only when it is a 5xx. The invoice drawer's PDF, the two attachment uploads (debit and purchase credit notes) and the AI reading of a bill say what they caught in the product's words (the uploads as changes, the reading as a proposal that records no bill, so as one whose result did not come back; the reading's try also holds the mapping onto the form, so only a request that got no answer is told that way and a defect there says its fixed sentence). Found while converting the payroll page, and fixed with it: its four run actions, Compute & Draft, Finalise, Recompute and Delete, swallowed every failure into nothing and said "Could not … the payroll run — the request failed.", a claim that the run was not made or not released over a request that may have been the one that posted its journal; they say what was thrown now (a change that got no answer: not known whether it went through, reload to see), and the page's reader throws for a body that is not JSON, and for an empty one under a failed status, where it read both as nothing, so the same sentence is said for a gateway's page and a load that met one says it could not load. Driven: four scenarios, one per run action, against a page that is not JSON; the previous product fails all four. Guard: the converted files call no `fetch`, read no body themselves and keep no copy of the shared helpers; a change whose failure is swallowed into `null` is flagged on the payroll and compliance pages. Known difference: a validation refusal on the purchases screens no longer prefixes the field path ("items.0.quantity: …"), it reads like the invoice screens' sentence.
- **POST-A-373** (closed 7-11-2026) — A bank rule that tags a customer or a supplier now names one of its own client's, asked when the rule is SAVED. The create and update models checked the pair's shape and the routes the pairing, so a rule could be saved naming a party of the firm's other client (or of nobody) and failed later, once for every line it was applied to, in a step whose failure is swallowed as a soft one (a tag is a label and must not stop a line being posted): the CA saw a rule that saved and lines that never got the tag. Saving a rule, or editing one to name a party, asks `customer_is_the_clients` / `vendor_is_the_clients` and answers 404 "That customer was not found." for a sibling client's party, another firm's, an invented id and an id that is not an id at all, in the same words; an edit that names no party is not asked (a rule whose party has since been deleted can still be renamed or switched off). The party's own check on the set-by-hand door told its refusals apart ("does not belong to this firm" for a party that is not there or is another firm's, "belongs to a different client" for a sibling's), which let a caller learn that an id was a real party of the firm's other client; it is one sentence and one status now, and a malformed id there, which was a 500, is the same missing party (`named_row_ownership.rows_of`). The status of that door's refusal is 404 (it was 422), so the two doors say it alike; the independent reads found nothing that depended on the old status or sentences. Tests: `tests/test_a_bank_rule_names_a_party_of_its_own_client.py` (10) and four in `tests/test_bank_payee_service.py` (two that were there now say the one sentence and 404, two are new); against the previous code 5 of the 10 and all four fail, and each of 11 mutations of the code is caught. Not done: `payee_id` is not in the named-row rule's list of kinds (the field is polymorphic: which table it names depends on a sibling field), so a NEW door that takes one is not asked about by that rule.
- **POST-A-374** (closed 7-11-2026) — The two routes that no screen opens are deleted, not wired: `GET /api/gst-workspace/gstr1/{return_id}` (read one return; the GST workspace lists returns whole and acts on one by `PATCH /gstr1/{id}/status`) and `GET /api/workload/{user_id}` (read one member's workload, which returned that member's full task rows under overdue_tasks and due_today; the team screens read `GET /api/workload` whole). The way-in guard's budget for both prefixes is gone, the reachable-routes fixture lost both lines, the ruff baseline lost the handler's one entry, and the tests of the deleted handlers' client scope went with them (one read in the list of `test_gst_client_scope.py`, two tests in `test_workload_team_client_scope.py`, two in `test_audit_remediation_5_1a.py`): a scope rule over a route that does not exist is not a rule, and a route that returns full task rows can only come back by being written again, which the router-scope rules and the way-in guard are there to catch. Recoverable from git (`git show 8969e176:apps/api/routers/workload.py`). Done under the owner's standing permission to delete dead endpoints; the independent reads found nothing that still refers to either route. Found on the way and fixed with it: the way-in guard's TOTAL budget stood at 218 while the per-prefix budgets add up to 197 (195 after this), so the total, which bounds net growth, was 21 of slack that could not bind: 21 more routes that reach no screen could have been added, in any prefixes, before it said anything. It is 195 now, and a test keeps it equal to the sum, so lowering a prefix lowers it in the same commit and growth is an edit of two numbers and not of one (a shuffle, one prefix allowed one more and another one fewer, still passes the total: refusing it is the per-prefix budgets' job). Against the previous tree 2 of the guard's 8 tests fail (the deleted routes are still counted), and each of 3 mutations (either route written again, the total drifting above the sum) is caught.
- **POST-A-375** (closed 7-11-2026) — The settings screens presented settings no document prints as if they took effect. The invoice PDF (on the practice's own fee invoice and on no other document) reads three of the saved look settings: the logo, the tagline (not on a Compact header) and the PRIMARY colour (the colour of the title). Nothing reads the secondary colour, the accent colour, the font, the secondary logo or the social links, and the PDF is set in Helvetica whatever is chosen, yet the preview drew a band in the secondary colour and set its text in the chosen font, the subtitle said the settings apply "across all documents and client communications" and the caption "on invoices and client documents". The preview now is a sketch of the top of the PDF in its own words (the logo, the title in the primary colour, the tagline if one is set, the firm, "Bill To" and "Invoice No", and amounts in "Rs." because the PDF's font has no rupee sign) and draws neither the font, the secondary colour, nor the full-width primary band and primary-coloured invoice number it also had that the PDF does not; the secondary colour and the accent are swatches and the font is a name, each with the plain fact that the PDF does not print it; the subtitle, the caption and the sentences at the colours, the font and the social links say what is printed (none says "yet": nothing is planned, and the word would need a row in the coming-soon register). The independent reads found the same defect one screen over and in the details, and it is fixed here: the Invoice Templates screen sold five layout styles ("bold typography", "ICAI branding guidelines and compliance fields", "numbers and key details only") that nothing draws, because `template_type` is carried and no property of the layout reads it (only where the logo and the signature sit, and the header and footer spacing, change the PDF), so a style is a name with no description (the label says "Style name") and the screen says so, and its empty state no longer says a template sets "what the footer says" (the footer's words are on another screen); the settings hub said the branding is "applied to all client documents" and listed the five styles; the payment-details screen said its details "appear on every invoice" while a client's own invoice carries none of the practice's, and it now says when the block is printed (a bank name, an account number or a UPI ID is filled in; the account holder and the IFSC code go with the bank details, and the QR picture with either); and the logo upload accepts PNG, JPEG, SVG or WebP up to 5 MB while the PDF draws a PNG, JPEG or WebP of up to 2,000,000 bytes that the server fetches within three seconds and silently leaves off anything else, which the Branding screen says under the upload and the Invoice Settings screen says under the UPI QR picture (the PDF fetches it with the same limits; the first wording said "2 MB", which a computer that counts in units of 1,048,576 shows differently, so it says bytes). The settings stay: wiring a colour, a font or a style into a document (the PDF would need a font whose licence is decided), refusing an SVG on upload, or dropping the settings is a product choice nothing depends on, and it is on no list. A Python rule on the side that owns the fact (`tests/test_a_settings_screen_says_only_what_the_invoice_pdf_prints.py`, 16 tests) measures which `firm_branding` columns the PDF reads, that the PDF and the screen that edits the branding are its only readers in the API, which properties of the layout it reads (never the style name), the bytes and the seconds the PDF gives a picture, the formats it can draw, the conditions of the payment block (the QR picture is read only inside it, the account holder and the IFSC code only with the bank details), that a client's own invoice is rendered with no branding, and the sentences of four screens; it fails when a column or property starts being printed or a promise comes back. It measures what the PDF READS and not what it draws, so a read left in place as dead code and a new screen that makes the claim are not seen. Its own comment stripper had the hazard the web side already guards against (the logo input's `accept="image/*"` opened a fake comment and hid the code after it) and a hole the other way (a sentence left in a comment at the end of a line still counted as on the screen); it walks the source now and strips only what is a comment. Driven in a browser: 4 scenarios (the preview's title is the primary colour and no block is, the tagline appears only when set, the templates screen, and the hub with the payment-details screen). Against the previous screens 6 of the 16 tests fail (the other ten measure the PDF itself, its readers and the stripper, which did not change) and all 4 of the browser scenarios; each of 35 mutations of the PDF, the screens and the guard is caught.
- **POST-A-378** (closed 7-11-2026) — A credit note or a purchase debit note asks whether the goods came back wherever the browser makes one, and the sentence the server says when it left stock alone is printed. The credit note raised from the invoice drawer and the debit note raised from the bill drawer ask the question when a line's item is a good (a catalogue read per set of ids, `lib/inventory/useNamesGoods`, whose two decisions are `lib/inventory/namesGoods`: false until the read returns, false again when the items change, and a failed read is never taken as a good, so the question is then asked later, as before) and send the answer with the draft; a draft can still be saved without one, and the server still refuses to issue a note that names goods and has not said. The question is worded for the note's own kind wherever the control is drawn (`goodsReturnedQuestion`: a credit note's goods "come back", a purchase debit note's "go back to the supplier", the server's own two verbs), which also corrects the debit-note editor that asked the credit note's question. The credit-note and the purchase debit-note CSV imports have a `goods_returned` column worded for each, read strictly (yes or no, blank for not known yet; a word that is neither is refused and never read as no, because no would tell the server to leave stock alone; the rows of one note must agree, and the refusal says two notes on one document need a Note Reference each); the strict read runs only in the two imports that offer the column, so a file for another note kind that happens to carry a column of that name is not judged by it. The four places that issue such a note (one and a bulk run on the credit notes tab, one and a bulk run on the debit notes tab) print the server's `stock_not_moved` sentence, or how many notes said it in the note kind's own verb (a debit note's goods "went back to the supplier"), after the usual message; it is no problem and does not make a toast a warning. Rules: a web guard over the tree (`scripts/a-note-that-can-move-stock-asks-whether-the-goods-came-back-wherever-it-is-made.test.ts`, 7 tests: every POST that creates such a note carries the answer or hands over a builder's payload, named; the four places that ask; the columns and which builders read them; the four issue paths print the sentence in the right kind; the lookup forgets the last answer and reads a failed read as not a good; the question is worded for the kind), 7 mapper tests, 4 tests of the stock-effect helpers, 3 of the question's wording and 5 of the lookup's decisions; against the previous code 6 of the guard's 7 tests and 6 of the 7 mapper tests fail (the helper tests cannot load, the functions do not exist). Driven in a browser: 10 scenarios (the question is asked, in the words of its kind, and sent for a goods line, not asked for a service line, a draft is saved without an answer, the sentence is printed on both tabs in the server's own words and absent when there is none); against the previous build 7 of the 10 fail (the three that expect nothing to be asked or printed pass there too); each of 27 mutations of the screens, the importers and the helpers is caught. Not covered by the guard: `api.*` or `fetch` calls and variable URLs that create such a note (none exists), and a third place that issues one.
- **POST-A-398** (closed 7-11-2026) — A delivery challan can be issued, so a job-work challan made in the product reaches FORM GST ITC-04. `create_challan` wrote a draft and nothing ever set `issued` (no route, database function, trigger or button; a test read the whole API tree, the migrations and the front end for one), so a job-work challan made here was not listed as sent until its goods were marked back, the ITC-04 lot door refused it ("goods can only come back on a challan that has been issued"), and the whole-challan "Record goods back" was offered for a draft and took `received_back` on it, which was the one way left to end the s.143 clock of a challan made here; a draft also drew a clock, an overdue banner and a gap about the kind of goods as if goods were out on it, and an order counted the goods on a draft as delivered. There is now ONE door, `POST /api/sales-cycle/challans/{id}/issue` (`sales_cycle_service.issue_challan`, the permission of the other challan writes, the challan tied to the client named, asked by the route and again by the door, whose update names the client as well as the firm and the id; an audit event), and the challan's detail has an "Issue challan" button for a draft. It turns a draft into an issued challan and changes nothing else, and refuses, in a sentence and with nothing written: a challan that is not a draft (issued, received back, cancelled), one with no lines, one dated after India's today, one that lacks a Rule 55(1) particular (the consigner is the CLIENT, read as the detail view reads it; the sentence names the clause), a job-work challan that does not say what is sent (inputs, capital goods, or tools: the period differs by a factor of three and is none for tools, so a challan issued without the answer would be listed on FORM GST ITC-04 while running no clock) and a delivery of more than its order has undelivered (asked where the goods leave, not when the draft is made); the write is a compare-and-set on the status, so an issue and a cancel, or two issues, cannot both win. WHAT ISSUING MEANS IS SAID BEFORE THE PRESS: it records that the goods left on the challan's own date (a period counts from that date, not from the press, so a draft dated long ago is one press from a period already over), and the draft's panel shows the server's sentence (if the goods left on the challan's date, the period under the Act would end on a day it names, and whether that day is already past) and says nothing of whether the goods have left, which the product cannot know. Around it: the amendment door cannot issue a challan (a hand-made `status: issued` is refused in a sentence), cannot make one a draft again, cannot give goods back to a draft (the lot door's own sentence, `delivery_challan.not_issued_sentence`), cannot cancel one with ANY lot recorded against it (goods returned, or lost or wasted), and leaves a received-back or a cancelled challan at its end (`delivery_challan.status_change_problem`, pure, decided by `AMENDMENT_MAY_MAKE` and tested over every pair); a status it writes, and the goods-back stamp, are compare-and-sets on the status they judged; an ISSUED challan keeps what issuing asked of it (a date that has come, a consignee, a place of supply for an inter-State movement, what a job-work challan sends: asked again of the row an amendment would leave, for the fields it touches), and the amendment door now writes an audit event naming the fields it changed and the fields that decide a period as they were and as they are; a draft and a cancelled challan draw no period on the list or in the Rule 55 view (`deemed_supply_clock(status=…)`); a challan recorded as received back with no day (a row from before POST-A-383) is neither overdue nor clear, is said to be unable to be timed, and is listed in the box for the day the goods came back, which is its way to be given one; goods count as delivered against an order once they have left (issued or received back), so a draft moves neither the order's position nor its status, issuing and cancelling re-settle it (an order that is `delivered` follows its lines back), and a draft still holds the order's lines from being re-priced; a challan made by picking its customer (or job worker) carries the party's name, GSTIN and address when none were typed (a typed consignee is never mixed with them); the form asks for the place of supply of an inter-State movement (Rule 55(1)(viii); without it every such challan stayed a draft) and says what Rule 55(1) asks and that a draft is not edited on the screen; the list's rows open from the keyboard, the card they open sits above the ITC-04 panel, the message line is a live region, and a refusal to an earlier press is not shown again. The premise test that failed when a door that issues a challan appeared is now the rule that exactly one function writes `issued`. Tests: `tests/test_a_delivery_challan_is_issued_by_one_door_and_a_job_work_challan_is_then_on_itc_04.py` (the door and each refusal, the compare-and-sets, the door and its update refusing another client's challan, challans made as the form makes them and then issued, ITC-04 listing a challan when issued and not before, the lot door, the amendment door, what an issued challan keeps, the pure status rule over every pair, the clock for each status, the route and its audit events), the order tests of `test_the_sales_cycle_before_the_tax_invoice.py`, a web guard of 9 tests and 11 browser scenarios; six existing tests that amended a challan they never issued now issue it first, and the cycle-document guard lists the issue route beside the amendment route. Mutation check: eighty-five deliberate breakages of the code (the status rule's branches and table, the period for each status and reason, each refusal of the issue door with its compare-and-set and its client, what an issued challan keeps, the amendment door's compare-and-set and audit event, the goods-back stamp, the order's position and status, the consignee copy and the client it is read for, the way a status is said, and the screen's button, box, form, list and live region) were each run against the new tests on a copy of the final tree, and every one was caught. Not done, and written down as new findings: GSTR-1 Table 13 declares only invoices, credit notes and debit notes (POST-A-415), an issued challan can still change its number, date and consignee to other valid values (POST-A-416), a draft cannot be completed or cancelled from the screen (POST-A-417), a recorded lot cannot be corrected or removed (POST-A-418), a challan's writes are several statements with no moment of issue stored (POST-A-419), whether Rule 55(1)(iii) needs the name and the address both is the owner's question (POST-A-420, S29), the challan-to-order link is API-only with three small remainders (POST-A-421), a received-back row with no day is not on the ITC-04 statement (POST-A-422) and a job-work challan to a job worker with no GSTIN, within the client's State, cannot carry the State the statement asks for from any screen (POST-A-423).
- **POST-A-417** (closed 13-11-2026) — A draft delivery challan can be corrected and cancelled from its panel. The amendment door took the consignee, the kind of goods and the place of supply but dropped `lines`, and no screen called it for a draft, so a challan made without a particular Rule 55(1) asks for (the HSN code of a line above all) could not be issued or mended and was made again under a new number while the draft kept its own. The door now corrects a DRAFT's lines by id in their description and HSN code only, refuses anything else of a line, a line that is not this challan's, a value that is not text, `lines` beside a status and every non-draft in a sentence with nothing written, and holds the header write to a draft; the draft's panel has "Correct draft" (a dialog) and "Cancel draft" (asked in the shared dialog, through its own wrapper, since the amendment wrapper is never given a status). The quantity, unit, rate and tax of a line stay as made, so the totals cannot change and a challan with different ones is made again. Design record: `documents-sales-purchase.md`, the sales-cycle entry.
- **POST-B-321** (closed 12-11-2026) — The practice's profitability tells the truth. Revenue is the fee before GST on invoices that are issued, in the period of the invoice's own date (Issued, Sent, Paid, Overdue; a Draft and a Cancelled invoice count for nothing), one definition for profitability, concentration and realization (realization used the total with GST and only Issued and Paid); cost is each member's cost rate times the hours they recorded, floored once per entry, and a member with no cost rate is unknown and not zero, so a client's profit and margin are null and the screen draws a dash and names who has unpriced hours; a client with time and no invoice is listed with its loss, and a client billed with no time recorded has no known cost; every time-entry and invoice read is paged (1,500 entries and 1,500 invoices are all counted); the member view reads the caller's clients only; and a Partner sets each person's cost per hour in a panel on the screen (`users.cost_rate_paise` was captured and read by nothing). No migration. Not done and in the ledger: realization's worth side (POST-A-426), a financial-year window and the seeder's cost rate (POST-A-427), and the surviving fee-billing engine (POST-B-193). Design record: `docs/design-record/screens-and-site.md`.
- **POST-A-426** (closed 12-11-2026) — Realization prices each billable hour from the rate it was recorded with: `time_rate.entry_rate` (`billable_rate_paise` first, the legacy `hourly_rate_paise` second, a stated 0 a rate, the order the unbilled-work total reads), floored once per entry, so a firm that times its work with the rates stored since migration 451 gets a figure where it had a dash against every engagement and one legacy entry no longer prices the nine beside it; an hour nobody priced is named (`unpriced_minutes`) and never worth 0, and a row, a client or the firm with one has no realization (null, drawn as a dash with the hours named); an engagement with priced time and no invoice is a row with a realization of 0.0; revenue per hour is null with no billable time. The firm's tile read `overall_realization_rate` while the server sent `realization_rate`, so it was a dash whatever the books held: the type and the screen now read the key the server sends and a test holds the payload's keys from the server's side. `domain/practice/realization.py` is the rule; the old averaging read is replaced by a raw `get_worth_entries`. Held by `tests/test_realization_prices_each_hour_from_its_own_rate.py` and a property test that the worth does not depend on how entries are grouped. No migration.
- **POST-A-427, in part** (closed 12-11-2026; the custom range of days stays open on the same id) — The three analytics endpoints take `period=fy` with a `financial_year` (one resolver; the year that is running ends today and is labelled "to date"; an unknown window, a year that has not started and a year given with any other window are each a 422), the Profitability screen offers the running financial year and the two before it, derived from the clock, and the demo seeder sets the member whose hours it records to the fixture's own seed cost rate so the seeded FY 2025-26 firm shows a cost. Taken ahead of the backlog order because it shares its resolver and its picker with POST-A-426. No migration.
- **POST-A-427, the rest** (closed 13-11-2026; no migration) — The three practice analytics endpoints take `period=custom` with `date_from` and `date_to` (both required; each judged by the query-string rule, so a refusal names the parameter; the later no later than India's today; the earlier no later than the later; a day with any other window refused in words; the label the two days with the year in four digits; the endpoints read exactly them, hours included), and the Profitability screen offers "A range of days…" with two date boxes that start blank, bounded by India's today, the last anchored on the first. Nothing is asked until the two days are a window: a missing, unreadable, reversed or future day is said in a sentence in place of the figures, and the figures of the window before are not left under the new one's name; a slow answer to an earlier window is not shown over a newer one. Driven in Chromium in the three zones of the date matrix (two scenarios of the date drive), with a broken build failing all four runs; a source guard holds the rules. Server half: PR #1081; screen half: the batch after it.
- **POST-A-424** (closed 12-11-2026) — An edit of an invoice, a bill or a note records the value it replaced. `audit_service.log_document_edit` writes, for the six edit routes, the previous value of each column the edit wrote beside what it now holds and which differ (the row read before the write; nothing for an UPDATE that matched no row or for `updated_at` alone; the actor the auth id, and the actor guard now scans the new call), the one door that replaces a draft's lines records the lines it replaced and the lines that replaced them (read as the API, in pages, in the document's order; nothing for a save that re-sends the same lines), and a unit correction on an issued invoice or a received bill records the unit it replaced. Taken from the finding's own premise and corrected by the review of it: the privileged client's statements carry the signed-in member (migration 547), so the row-level capture may already record a whole row for the three older tables where the header reaches the database, which is not verified, and the three newer notes have no capture trigger at all; the record is the application's own and relied on for none. Left in the ledger: the lines are read before the function's lock (POST-A-429) and two other doors keep no old value (POST-A-430). No migration.
- **POST-A-425** (closed 12-11-2026) — An issued note and a received bill can have their note, reference, due date or attached copy saved though a line would not pass today's check for a new document. The two purchase note editors and the purchase bill editor ran the create-time check (a vendor, a date, a catalogue item on each line, a clause on a blocked line) on a document that is no longer a draft, and showed its frozen lines red after the press; the invoice editor and the two sales note editors did not. The bill editor is the same defect and is fixed with them (the item named only the two note editors). All five now read the flag and the check only behind the lock; a guard over every editor that declares the lock (found by its declarations) fails on the five as they were; the browser drive's issued-document rows use a line naming no item. Not checked: whether production holds such a note or bill. No migration.
- **POST-A-430** (closed 13-11-2026) — The two doors that set a user-chosen classification record what the column held before. `POST /api/accounting/schedule-iii/ageing/classify` (the line named it a PUT; it is a POST) writes `is_disputed` and `considered_doubtful` on a receivable, `is_disputed` on a payable, a supplier's MSME status, registration number and agreement period, and an account's unbilled-dues side, and logged only `new_data`; the landed-cost basis door logged only the new basis. Both now read the row first under the same firm and client scope the write uses (`ageing_schedule_service._was`: one literal read per target, and for an account the client's own row and then the firm's shared one; `landed_cost_service.set_basis`) and the route puts what each written column held in the edit log as `old_data` beside `new_data`. A row that is not this client's is a 404 (the landed-cost write used to match nothing and report success) and nothing is written; a read that fails is not swallowed. The response a caller gets does not change shape. Two things the line did not say: the same ageing door classifies a supplier's MSME status (which s.43B(h) turns on) and an account's unbilled-dues side, so all four targets are covered, and the client's own landed-cost basis (an accounting policy, AS 2) was in the same position as a bill's override. Design record: `tenancy-and-security.md`, the POST-A-424 entry.
- **POST-A-423** (closed 13-11-2026) — A job-work challan to a job worker with no GSTIN, within the client's own State, can be given the State FORM GST ITC-04 asks for. The new-challan form shows the place of supply only for an inter-State movement, the issue door asks for neither, and no screen changed such a challan's place of supply afterwards (the draft's correction form does, for an inter-State draft), so the statement named the challan as a gap with no way to close it. - **POST-A-419** (closed 13-11-2026; migration 585 shipped in the change before, with no code using it) — A delivery challan's writes are one transaction each, and the moment it was issued is stored. `create_challan`, `itc_04_service.record_return` and `update_challan` call the three functions of migration 585 through one door, `services/delivery_challan_writes`, and the step-by-step writes are deleted (`record_goods_back` with them): a challan and every line are one call (a line that fails leaves no header); a job-work lot and the stamp that the challan is received back are one call that takes the challan's lock (two lots of 6 against a line of 10 cannot both be recorded, and a cancel and a lot cannot both win; the table's trigger asks again that a challan with a lot is not cancelled, and `amend` words that refusal in the API's own sentence); a draft's corrections, header and lines, are one call held to a draft; a request that loses a race is a 409 that says nothing was written. `issue_challan` writes `issued_at` with the status, and first asks that the header says what its lines come to (`delivery_challan.header_does_not_add_up_sentence`, in rupees, each differing figure named). - **POST-A-380** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — A request model or a query string that takes a date as a bare string was a 500 for any caller that is not the screen, and a slash date was stored month first. - **POST-A-094** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — `core.ist_clock._as_ist_date` read a timestamp string by its first ten characters, the UTC day, which is a day behind India from 00:00 to 05:30 IST, so `ist_fy_label('2026-03-31T20:10:00+00:00')` was 2025-26 for a moment that is 1 April in India. A string with a time is now read as the moment it names: one with a zone (an offset or a trailing `Z`) is converted to India before its day is taken, one with none is the Indian day (as a naive datetime already was), and text that is neither a day nor a moment is a ValueError rather than its first ten characters. Its prerequisite, that the doors reaching `fy_code` take only a real day, was met by POST-A-380 in the same change. Every caller was read: the FY readers are handed a document's own day, a date object or nothing, and the day windows' bounds are query days judged at the door; no live caller was found to pass a stamp, so the change protects the next one. Held by four tests in `tests/test_ist_clock.py`, three of which fail against the previous reader.
- **POST-A-407** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — Nothing showed who a supplier's no-PE declaration is attributed to, and a supplier recorded before POST-A-401 was attributed to whoever next saved its form. - **POST-A-409** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — A register row left behind by a failed sync of a reversed vendor advance (or of a bill that left the books) had no door that removed it, and the deposit worksheet, the hub's "deducted and not yet deposited" and FORM 3CD clause 34 would still count it. - **POST-A-156** (closed 14-11-2026; migration 586) — The time screen's "Total Hours", "Billable Hours" and "Non-Billable" tiles were the sum of the entries it listed (at most 500, no period at all), and `GET /api/time-entries/summary/me` summed every entry the member ever made in Python, ignored the `period` it accepted and was called by nothing. `public.time_entry_summary` (migration 586, SECURITY INVOKER, service_role only) sums one member's FINISHED time in [from, to) where the rows are, with the timers still running counted apart and the minutes per client; `domain/time_summary.summarise` is its mock-mode twin, held identical on real Postgres by `tests/test_time_entry_summary_parity_pg.py` (five windows, the 18:30 UTC midnight on both sides, and EXECUTE refused to `authenticated` and `anon`). The entry is off the frozen list of answers nobody can open.
- **POST-A-349** (closed 14-11-2026; migration 587) — A depreciation reversal writes the register back first and reverses the month's journal second, and a post of the SAME month in between adopted the still-live journal through the kernel's duplicate check, wrote the register up and was then reversed under (register posted, ledger reversed). Migration 587 adds `fixed_assets.depreciation_reversal_month` and `depreciation_reversal_claimed_at` (both or neither, the month a month); the reversal writes them in the same compare-and-set as its claim and clears them, by compare-and-set on its own stamp, once the journal is reversed or the claim is put back. A post refuses while the marker is within a 15-minute lease, and its own register write is a compare-and-set on the marker it read, so a reversal that claims the row after the post read it makes the post take its journal back (409). A marker past the lease is a reversal that died: a post adopts the live journal and clears it, a repeated reversal of that month claims it afresh and completes it, another month is refused until it is. The Depreciation tab serves the unfinished reversal in words with its times in IST and offers Finish reversing; a lost race reverses its journal only when the register is still behind the month; the restore and the clear compare the claim's own stamp; a delete waits for the reversal to be finished. The API writes the marker only when the row it read carries the columns, so it can run ahead of the migration. Held by 28 mock-mode tests (ten mutations of the fix and five of the review's fixes, each caught), 8 real-Postgres tests of the interleavings with the ledger tied to the register to the paisa, and 3 browser scenarios. Left open on purpose: a post that read the row before a whole reversal and writes after it (POST-A-434).
- **POST-A-045** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — Five of the six frozen private pagers now read through `core.db_paging`, which learned what they needed: `retries` on `iter_pages`/`fetch_all` (a failed page asked again with a growing wait, the last failure raised; the reporting ledger source's `_fetch_page` is deleted and its retry, which no test had ever exercised, is tested now), a query built afresh for every page (the bank history's IN list of values not yet seen shrinks between pages and the keyset cursor stays true), and `fetch_all_ordered`, an OFFSET read in a stated order that always ends in `id` (the stock ledger, stock ageing and stock position readers, which page in (movement_date, created_at) order). Ending the order in `id` fixes a fault the move exposed: rows that tie on the date and the recorded-at instant (an opening-stock batch is ONE insert, so hundreds share both) could come back in a different order on each page request and be read twice or never at a page boundary, which a double that reorders ties per request reproduces against the old loop. The sixth, the stock register's latest row per item, asks one row per item and is POST-A-435. `page_size` is read when called, so a test that shrinks `PAGE` shrinks every reader. After the independent read: the reporting source reads strictly (a page cap or a keyless row raises), orders by its key once, and the bank history counts a pager's own early stop as incomplete.
- **POST-A-435** (closed 14-11-2026; migration 588) — The stock register found each item's latest stock-ledger row by a scan capped at eight pages; each page found the items among its rows, so it ran out when page after page was filled by one busy item each, and every item older than eight such items showed ZERO quantity and value with a log line only; the item list was one request answering at most a thousand goods. `public.stock_latest_rows` (migration 588, SECURITY INVOKER, service_role only, an index on (item, created_at DESC, id DESC)) returns ONE jsonb array of each asked item's latest row of the firm and client, one probe per item (a set of rows would be cut at 1,000 by PostgREST, the independent read's finding); the nightly cache-drift check asks the same function; the router asks it once on the privileged client, refuses the whole register in words when the answer is not a list, and reads the item list through the shared ordered reader. `domain/inventory/latest_rows` is the twin, held to it on real Postgres (the tie of two rows recorded in one instant goes to the larger id in both), and `_last_ledger_row` breaks the tie the same way, so the register shows the row the next movement costs from. The guard's frozen list of private pagers is empty.
- **POST-A-101** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — Late interest a client charges its own customer applied each invoice's balance AS IT STANDS TODAY to every late day. The ledger line and the caveat on every answer said that OVERSTATED the interest where a part-payment arrived late; it was the other way round: today's balance is lower than what was owed before the payment, so it UNDERSTATED what the client's terms allow. `domain/sales/late_interest` now follows the balance by date (`BalanceChange`, `balance_paise_days`): money the customer paid reduces it from the date it arrived (a receipt allocated to the invoice from the receipt's date, a posted bank line matched to it from the line's date, less what that posting turned into the customer's credit), the day a payment arrives is still a late day on what it pays, and the interest is rounded once on the paise-days. An amount credited by an issued credit note or applied from the customer's credit is treated as never owed (`FROM_THE_START`: the records do not say whether a note corrected the price or recorded a later return, and this reading cannot overstate), and what an issued debit note adds bears no interest here (`OUTSIDE_THE_WINDOW`: it is a separate demand on its own terms). The service reads all of them and uses them only when they account for exactly what the invoice shows as settled (`history_ties`); otherwise, or when they cannot be read, the row is on today's balance and says why and which way that errs, the answer counts such rows by reason, and an invoice with nothing settled needs no history. A covered row still says its basis. The screen shows the row's sentence, and the prepared draft's line says "on the balance outstanding on each of those days" when that is what it was computed on.
- **POST-A-115** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — The Depreciation tab showed one year's charge (the year containing today) and nothing about when an asset would be fully depreciated. `routers/fixed_assets.project_remaining_life` runs `_month_charge`, the rule a posting now charges by, month by month on a copy of the register row, carried forward as a posting carries the row, so the projection is the postings' own arithmetic: months posted through the route add up to it year by year. It starts at the month after the last one posted (with nothing posted: the purchase month for an asset acquired here, the current month for one brought over part-depreciated, which says the months before are foreclosed) and ends where a posting would, naming what stays on the register. The tab's Remaining life control asks `GET /api/fixed-assets/{id}/remaining-life` for one asset, and the row's year figure is the projection's for the current year. Writing it exposed POST-A-436 (twelve floored twelfths leave up to 11 paise a year, posted in a year after a straight-line asset's life).
- **POST-A-436** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — A year's depreciation was posted as twelve floored twelfths (`floor(annual / 12)` a month), so a year whose figure is 20,000.00 posted 19,999.92 while the tab showed 20,000.00, and a straight-line asset of 1,00,000.00 over five years was 40 paise short at the end of its life and posted 36 of them in a sixth year. `routers/fixed_assets._month_share` now charges month k of the year (April is 1) `annual x k // 12 - annual x (k - 1) // 12`: the twelve add up to the year's figure exactly, a part year to its fraction, each month within a paisa of a twelfth, the purchase month still pro-rated. A reversal takes back what the month's journal carries, so nothing already posted changes; the schedule's monthly figure is the share the month being posted takes; the projection follows by construction. A straight-line asset reaches nil exactly where cost less salvage divides by its life; the few paise otherwise left, and a year's figure under twelve paise whose nil months block the next, are POST-A-437.
- **POST-A-066** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — Nothing compared the e-invoice register with the period's GSTR-1: an IRN recorded as live for an invoice the return does not declare, or a record whose number or date is no longer its invoice's, went unseen. `domain/gst/irn_against_return.compare` reads the GSTR-1 build's own payload, the period's live IRN records and the invoices they name (by link, or by number for an unlinked record), asks each invoice's own state first (deleted, cancelled, draft, carried over, another period) and then whether the return declares it as B2B or an export, as B2CL, or only within a total, and lists records that differ from their invoice and invoices with more than one live record. `GET /api/gst/gstr1/irn-check` serves it; the GSTR-1 screen's summary has a Check. The missing-IRN list no longer lends a linked record's IRN to another invoice that shares its number. Whether an exempt supply to a registered person owes an IRN is owner-list S33.
- **POST-A-377** (closed 14-11-2026; no migration) — The person columns with no key and no rows were not decided, so nothing checked what their writers put in them. - **POST-A-104** (closed 14-11-2026; migration 589) — An order's invoiced figure was always zero because an invoice line carried no link to the order line it billed. `client_sales_invoice_lines.sales_order_line_id` now names it (nullable, no backfill, ON DELETE RESTRICT, and a trigger that refuses another client's order line); the order's position counts the issued invoices that name each line (never one matched by description); both invoice doors refuse billing more than is left, drafts counted; an order whose lines an invoice names keeps its lines; and the order panel raises an invoice for what is unbilled, prefilled in the ordinary editor, saying first what is not delivered and what is not carried. What it does not hold is POST-A-440 (two issues at the same moment) and POST-A-441 (the order doors do not check an item is the client's). The probe pass's follow-on (an e-way bill recorded against a Rule 55 challan) is still true and is POST-A-439.
- **POST-A-093** (closed 15-11-2026; migration 590) — There were no narration templates for manual journals. A client now keeps saved narrations (`journal_narration_templates`, one live row per wording ignoring case, the words held to one line of 1 to 300 characters); the journal editor offers them beside the narration box, picking one fills the box (the entry keeps the words it is saved with, never the template), "Save this narration" keeps the box's words, and a picked one can be taken out of the list. Read by the members assigned to the client, written by the API only, with the two firm checks of migrations 565 and 566. The companion item of the same roadmap line, per-voucher-type numbering series, is the accounting-11 item and stays owner-parked.
- **POST-A-216** (closed 15-11-2026; no migration) — The knowledge-base search read the title only, with `ilike`, although an article's words are its text (`knowledge_article_versions.content`, one row per version); it was also one unpaged request, and a typed `%` or `_` was a wildcard. `knowledge_service.search_articles` now finds an article when every word of the search is in its title or in its current text (one word may be in each): for each word it reads the articles whose title holds it and the versions whose text holds it, keeps a version only when it is its article's `current_version`, reads the articles found by their text under the same firm, archived, scope and client filters, applies the per-row visibility to all of them, and says on each row where the words were (`matched_in`); every read is paged, the pattern escapes a typed backslash, `%` and `_`, and more than eight words is a 422 in words. Both knowledge screens say when the words were in the text alone and, for an empty search, repeat the words and say no article in this list has every one of them; the firm screen now waits for a pause in typing, shows only the newest answer and refreshes the list after a restore, and the client screen's empty sentence follows the answer shown, not the box. No ranking or stemming (the design record says so, and the sentence expires), and the record names the version read's cost in a large knowledge base as a known limit. 18 mock tests, 11 real-Postgres cases, 2 more source rules and 7 browser scenarios; `docs/architecture/11-revenue-ops-and-knowledge.md` §7 and §9 rewritten.
- **POST-A-291** (closed 15-11-2026; no migration) — Neither MCA filing form let the CA say which company a filing was for, so with several companies a filing could never close the client's AOC-4 or MGT-7 obligation, and the firm-level form defaulted its period to the current financial year. `GET /api/mca-workspace/filing-choices` serves the client's ACTIVE companies in name order and the financial years the compliance calendar holds the form's obligation open in (not Filed or Completed, a financial year's own window, newest first, with due dates; none, with a sentence, for a form with no obligation, for MGT-7A, for a caller without Compliance access, or when none is open). Both forms ask it whenever the client or the form changes and show only the newest answer: a company choice when there is more than one (the chosen company's own CIN shown and written, with its id), the company named when there is one, the year as a list with Another year to type one, the only open year preselected and the placeholder refused, and the typed boxes as before when the answer cannot be read, said with Try again; the firm-level period starts empty instead of from the clock and is required for a form that stands for an obligation. `POST /api/mca-workspace/filings` refuses a filing that names no company when the client has several active ones, one that names an inactive company, and one of a form that stands for an obligation with no year, and stores the company's own id and CIN. 24 mock tests (13 of the first 16 fail on the previous code; thirteen mutations caught), 7 tests of the answer's reader, 10 browser scenarios (all 10 fail on the previous forms), and the 123 existing MCA date scenarios pass; the firm-level page still inserts from the browser (POST-A-442).
- **POST-A-112** (closed 15-11-2026; migrations 591 and 592) — A fixed asset's place was one free-text field a move overwrote, nothing recorded who keeps an asset, and the annual physical verification a CA performs for audit had no working paper in the product. An asset has a keeper and a dated movement history written with it in one step (`record_fixed_asset_movement`, 591; a form opened before somebody else's movement is refused, and a typo stays an edit); and a client's assets are counted in a dated session with a finding per asset held on the day (found, found elsewhere with the place, not found, damaged) beside what the register held on the day counted, closed only once each asset has one and never reopened (`record_fixed_asset_finding`, `close_fixed_asset_verification`, 592; the client's Physical Verification tab). A finding moves nothing in the register.
- **PRE-B-014** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — No successful live call to either AI provider had ever been recorded. On 16-11-2026 at 02:09 IST the owner, signed in as a Partner with an authenticator, pressed Check now on Settings > AI status: Groq answered with `openai/gpt-oss-120b` in 316 ms (106 tokens) and Gemini with `gemini-3.5-flash` in 1,723 ms (1,157 tokens), and Claude read the two `ai_usage_events` rows (feature `probe`, outcome `ok`) in the production database. The final brand and domain stay POST-B-053.
- **PRE-B-012** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — The Supabase dashboard was read with the owner on 15 and 16 Nov 2026: plan Free (also read through the Supabase connection), region ap-south-1, no backups and no point-in-time recovery (written into the incident runbook's section 6), leaked-password protection 'Only available on Pro plan', both Partner sign-ins holding a verified authenticator, the rate limits (30 emails an hour, 30 sign-ups and sign-ins per 5 minutes per address, 60 seconds between mails to one address) enough for the demo, Confirm email ON and anonymous sign-ins OFF, CAPTCHA kept off until the pages send an answer (POST-B-039). The owner decided to upgrade when real clients' books go in: POST-B-347.
- **PRE-B-013** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — The AI providers' tiers and terms were read from their own pages through the owner's screenshots on 16 Nov 2026: the Gemini key the API uses was free tier (Google may use content to improve products and human reviewers may read it); the owner moved its project to Google's paid terms (content not used to improve products, logged for a limited time for abuse checks) by PREPAY with Rs 500 and auto-reload off, a hard cap; Groq is on its Free plan and the owner switched on zero data retention for inference. Recorded in docs/compliance/06 the same day; the sub-processor wording waits for the privacy notice (POST-B-266, POST-B-259).
- **PRE-C-002** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — The Render variables were checked with the owner over 15 and 16 Nov 2026: EMAIL_FROM is the verified demo sender, FRONTEND_URL and ALLOWED_ORIGINS name the demo domain, RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET is set, TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS is unset (the default 1 is right while no proxy fronts the API), and the Security status screen reads row-level security, two-step sign-in for Manager and Partner and the scheduled jobs all On with no warning. Left for after the demo: the fallback models and the multi-currency switch, POST-C-213.
- **PRE-C-003** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — Resend delivers: the sending domain is verified, the bounce and complaint webhook is registered with its secret in Render, and a staff invitation, an invoice and an engagement letter each reached an outside inbox. The Book a demo form was accepted by the provider but its DEMO_REQUEST_TO address is not one the owner reads; the owner decided the form says bookings are not open until a business mailbox exists (PRE-A-026, POST-B-346).
- **PRE-C-004** (closed 16-11-2026; no code, no migration) — Render's API service follows `main` with Auto-Deploy 'On Commit', and on 15 Nov 2026 the live version was the head of `main`, so no manual deploy is owed; release-and-rollback.md section 1 says so. The day-before check that the live version matches `main` moved to PRE-C-005.
- **PRE-A-020** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A staff invite not yet accepted showed Active on the Team screen. `domain/firm/member_state.py` is now the one rule (inactive when switched off; active once linked to a sign-in; invited while the token is unused and its expiry ahead; invite expired otherwise; a row from before invitations stays active) and `GET /api/identity/users` serves `member_state` on each row, asked before the token is stripped. The Team screen shows Active, 'Invited · link valid until <day>', Invite expired or Inactive from that word, counts only active members as active, offers 'Withdraw invite' rather than Deactivate on an invitation, and shows a joined date only for someone who joined; a row without the word (an older backend) shows a dash rather than a guess. Verified: 10 server tests, including a route test that invites, lists (Invited), accepts and lists again (Active), and an expired invitation; 5 screen-helper tests.
- **PRE-A-021** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The Secure your account banner and the security page named Billing to a Manager, who has none. `domain/firm/mfa_locked_areas.py` lists the eight areas the authenticator guard locks, each with the permission its router asks, and `GET /api/security/mfa-policy` now serves `locked_sentence` for the caller's own permissions (overrides included); both screens print that sentence and, without it, claim nothing specific. A Manager now reads 'Team, client assignments, Payroll, firm settings and your own profile stay locked until you set up an authenticator app.' Verified: 15 server tests, including that the guarded routers parsed from the app equal the named list, that each area's permission is asked by its router, and that a Manager is not told Billing; 13 screen-helper tests.
- **PRE-A-022** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The dark layer behind a dialog started 24 px down and cut the navy bar in two. The shared Modal, Drawer and ConfirmDialog now draw their layer at the end of the document body (`lib/ui/inBody.ts`), so no parent's spacing, transform or filter can move it, and the client bar's 3 px navy top line is gone (owner's decision of 16-11-2026). Verified in Chromium: `scripts/drive/scenarios/overlayLayers.mjs` opens the Team invite dialog and the fixed-assets Add Asset panel and requires the layer to cover the whole window, in the body; both pass on the new build and both failed on the previous one (24 px and 16 px short). A layer drawn in the body is compared with every other layer by its own level, so a dialog opened from inside a higher one went behind it: the full browser run found the New Product/Service dialog (level 70) hidden behind the product and service manager that opened it (level 80), where nobody could click it. So a shared layer that opens while another full-window layer is open takes the level just above the highest of them, set before the first paint (`lib/ui/layerStack.ts`, 7 tests); with none open it keeps its own level. A third scenario opens that dialog over its panel and checks a click lands on the dialog. The 131 hand-built overlays are not changed here: each one moved onto the shared Modal under POST-A-130 inherits the fix, and that line says so.
- **PRE-A-025** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration, deliberately) — An engagement letter from an older stored template printed '₹₹4,000'. The merge now drops a rupee sign (₹, its two HTML entities, Rs. or INR) written immediately before `{{engagement_fee}}`, because the fee field already carries its sign; nothing else in the template is touched and a word merely ending in 'rs' or 'inr' is not a sign. The stored templates were NOT rewritten by a migration as the line proposed: the merge covers every letter made from now on whatever a template says, a firm's template is the firm's own text, and the migration numbers after 595 are held by the security stack until after the demo. Letters already issued are unchanged. Verified: 4 new tests (a sign beside the fee, nothing else touched, a word ending in rs or inr, and every default template merging to one sign), and the 58 engagement-letter tests.
- **PRE-A-026** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The website offered hello@practicesync.com and +91 80 4718 2200, neither of which existed, and the demo form mailed requests to an inbox nobody read. Reopening is one setting (POST-B-346), recorded as COMING-036. The API is unchanged: its refusal messages are only ever shown inside the form beside the address. - **PRE-A-023** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — Settings was one column of fourteen cards and the security status sat behind a card whose only link read 'Manage 2FA'. Settings is now six tabs across the top of every Settings page (Your profile; The firm; Security and access; Invoices and documents; Tax and statutory values; AI and connections), from one list in `lib/settings/groups.ts` that the tab row, the Settings home and the Settings panel in the workspace menu all read. Each tab shows its own forms (your name; the firm's profile and financial year) and a short list whose links say what they open; 'How this deployment is set up' is under Security and access by name and opens on that section. No page moved and no route was added: a tab is the Settings home with `?tab=`. A Partner-only link stays Partner-only, as it was, and a tab with nothing for the person (AI and connections, to a Manager) is not shown. On a phone the row scrolls sideways inside itself. Verified: 8 tests on the groups (every Settings screen on disk is reachable from a tab; each page lights its own tab; the Partner-only list; which tabs a Manager sees), the five guards that read Settings links moved to the shared list (with a helper that also asks both surfaces render it), and four Chromium scenarios (`settings-tabs`: six tabs across the top; the security status one tab and one click away; Branding lights Invoices and documents; the page does not widen at 375 px), all four failing on the previous build.
- **PRE-A-024** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — On the live AI status page the window scrolled past the end after Save allowance, the top bar went out of view and a screen of blank space showed. Cause: the usage tables' screen-reader captions are positioned absolutely, `<main>` was not positioned, so they were laid out against the window, escaped the scrolling area and made the document as tall as the page; a local build serves no usage figures, so no tables, which is why it did not reproduce there. Fix: `<main>` is `relative` in both shells. Verified in Chromium (`window-stays-put`, two scenarios: usage figures served, on load, after Save allowance, after choosing another month, after scrolling to the end, and again at 125% zoom): on the previous build the document was 1,398 px tall in a 900 px window; on the new build it is no taller than the window. The drive now measures this after every scenario on any page inside the shell, so the nightly drive is the list of screens that do it; the design record names it (frontend.md).
- **POST-A-269** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The seeded books were out of proportion: five of the eight demo companies made a loss before tax (Anand Textiles turned over Rs 26.2 lakh on a Rs 4.2 crore factory) and six bank ledgers were below nothing on 31 March, because a purchase line was priced at the same catalogue rate as a sale line, the assets were sized real against documents drawn small, and an asset bought before the books open was paid out of a bank whose opening balance is dated later. Each client now states its posted turnover, what its purchases cost as a share of it and, for the construction client, what its projects cost (`fixture.Proportions`), and the documents are resized after every random draw (a good by its quantity, a service by its rate; a good bought is brought to the units the year sells and costs that share of its drawn rate; opening stock is costed at the same share), so no document moves its date, party, count or lines. The assets were cut to fit each business, the opening bank balances raised to what each trade needs, and `fixture.capital_introductions` records the owners' capital (share capital for a company) the day before each asset bought before the year, for what the `paid` door takes out of the bank, and on the day each project starts, which the seeder posts as journals (Dr the bank, Cr Capital Account) and refuses to skip. `domain/demo/year_estimate` works each year out from the fixture (it reproduced the 19-10-2026 seed's figures before the change), and `tests/test_the_demo_companies_are_in_proportion.py` holds every company to a profit with and without the bank queue passed, a margin of 3% to 30%, assets and projects no larger than a year's turnover, a bank ledger and a printed statement never below nothing in the seeded year, and a turnover under the e-invoice, section 44AD and tax-audit figures the code holds; 32 of its 45 tests fail on the old fixture. An independent review found the LLP sized over the LLP audit figure a code comment cites (now kept under it) and two tests weaker than their names (strengthened). NOT confirmed on a real seed (PostgREST is refused in this environment, owner list section 3 row 11): PRE-A-004 and the live seeding (PRE-B-001) are where that happens. Another financial year draws a different settlement pattern and its bank is not held.
- **POST-A-448** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The AI status page led with the model codes (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`, `gemini-3.5-flash`), which help only the person diagnosing a failure and read as though content goes to OpenAI when Groq serves that model, and the usage table showed each feature by its internal key and its provider by an id. The AI is now presented as PracticeSync AI: the status page, the assistant and the copilot carry the name, and `domain/ai/budget.fold` serves each feature's name (`FEATURE_LABELS`, every one beginning PracticeSync AI) and each provider's name as the status page shows it (`PROVIDER_LABELS`); a feature with no name is shown by its key and never given a guessed one. Every model code (asked for, fallbacks, the one that answered, the one each history row used, the models per feature) moved under a closed `components/ai/TechnicalDetails` (a native `details`). The provider is NOT hidden: each card is headed by it, the probe answer names it, the usage table has its column, and the send-time notices (`lib/ai/disclosure.ts`) are unchanged and still held by `test_the_ai_disclosure_names_the_providers_the_code_calls.py`. Guards: `tests/test_every_ai_feature_carries_the_products_name_and_its_provider.py` (every feature the code records a call under has a name, none is kept for a feature no longer recorded), `scripts/the-ai-is-named-as-practicesync-and-the-model-codes-are-folded-away.test.ts` (4 of 5 fail on the old screens) and the `ai-naming` drive group. Not done: the side-panel links still read AI Assistant and AI Copilot, which the owner did not ask to change.
- **POST-A-445** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A staff invitation could not be sent again and its mail named neither the practice nor the Partner: the Team screen received the invitation's token and asked Supabase to mail an ordinary sign-in link from the Partner's browser, which lasted only as long as Supabase keeps a one-time code. The server now sends the invitation (the practice's name as the sender, the inviting Partner as Reply-To, the expiry in IST) and the dialog says what the mail service did with it. Resend invite on a row still waiting to join writes a fresh link, so the earlier one stops working, and keeps the earlier one when the new email does not go; a joined member and a withdrawn invitation are refused in words. The join page signs the invitee in from the bare link when they press Accept, for as long as the invitation lasts, and asks before signing somebody else out of the browser. An independent review found the first version made a leaked token a sign-in (the team list returned it to Managers); the token is now kept as a hash, returned on no response, opens a sign-in only for an invitation the server mailed and never for an address that already has a sign-in, and a sign-in that already belongs to a practice cannot accept a second. Withdraw invite already existed (PRE-A-020); the badge now changes without a reload.
- **POST-A-446** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The invitation page's blue-to-indigo background, which the owner asked to keep and use elsewhere, was three classes written on that one page. It is now one named style in the product's palette and one shared layout, on the sign-in, sign-up and password pages, the client and employee portals' sign-in and first-visit pages, the engagement letter a client signs, the invitation, an unknown address under a signed-out page, and the error pages of those areas; never behind a working screen.
