The sign-in page's closing line "Trusted by accounting firms across India" (no firm used the product) and its first feature line "All compliance in one place" (the product prepares returns for the CA to file) were removed, and a guard keeps any statement about who uses the product off these pages.
- **POST-A-441** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A quotation, a sales order, a delivery challan, a purchase order and a goods receipt wrote the catalogue item a line named without asking whose it was, while every invoice, bill and note door asks (the purchase side was found by the independent review). The eight doors that write those lines now ask the same rule before anything is written; a line naming another client's or another firm's item is refused, naming the line, and nothing is saved. The quotation, sales order and purchase order edits also refuse a number already taken before they replace the lines, so a refused edit no longer leaves the totals out of step with the lines.
- **POST-A-433** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The year-end screens' engagement checks asked the person's assignment and not the rule that the firm's own practice is the Partner's alone. The rule is now in the assignment check itself, so an assignment to the practice (which only a privileged write could make) grants a non-Partner nothing, on the year-end screens and on every other screen that looks a record up by its own number.
- **POST-A-400** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — Five screens showed "Loading…" in place of themselves on every read, so a dialog or panel that saved and asked the screen to read again was thrown away by that read: the payroll roster's portal invitation lost the one-time activation link it had just been given, the deadlines list's Refresh lost the search typed into it, and the practice page's tax identity card, a client's employee drawer and the stock locations' Move stock panel lost what they held. Each now blocks for its first read only, through one shared rule; the employee drawer is handed the employee as the reload read them, and keeping it on screen also stopped a recorded settlement from bringing its record button back. A source rule finds the shape (it found the deadlines list, which the finding had not named), and two browser checks (the invitation's link and the deadlines search) fail on the build before the change and pass on this one.
- **POST-A-392** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — Six places answered a refusal in words that did not say what to correct. The MCA screens' doors said "Unable to complete MCA operation. Please try again." for every failure and mostly logged nothing; each now logs the cause and names what it was doing, or gives the database's own sentence where it has one. The compliance record doors put "Validation error on filed_date:" in front of the sentence; they give the sentence alone. The filing-date refusal said "filed_date cannot be in the future"; it names the date in words, gives both days and says today is India's. A professional-tax band with a negative figure or an upper limit of 0 was refused naming no band; it is refused naming the band, on the screen before it is sent and at the server. A typed date past today now says "is after today's date in India". The one left as it was, on purpose: a portal's date sent with a time of day is kept as the Indian day it falls on, because the record holds a day and nothing reads a time; the request now says so in its own description.
- **POST-A-414** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A failed request was said in the wrong words in two kinds of place. Of the screens that write straight to the database from the browser, most already said a dropped connection correctly; four did not, and each is fixed: switching a customer on or off said a fixed failure although the change may have gone through; saving a tax audit said "Save failed" for a request that got no answer; the income-tax notices' status buttons ignored the answer altogether, so a refused change looked made; and the chart-of-accounts import, when its single insert was refused, said all but one account had gone in (it now says none, or "not known" when no answer came back). Separately, seven requests that only ask (previews and one check, each read on the server and found to write nothing) were described as changes when they failed and were never tried again after the server woke; they are now treated as questions. A rule over the whole screen code holds the first, a named list holds the second, and a browser check holds the wording.
- **POST-A-147** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A screen that drew its own loading placeholder (grey blocks, a spinner with "Loading…", a hand-made pulsing box) said nothing however long it waited, while the shared placeholders have said "The server is waking up. The first screen of the day can take up to a minute." after three seconds since frontend_ux-05. Before this change that was 222 placeholders in 79 files, among them the home dashboard's lists and hub, a client's overview, health and year-end pages, the document drawers and the report screens. Each now sits in one shared loading region that carries the same sentence, said once on a page and gone with the answer, and never with a Retry, because the region is drawn by a read and a Retry beside a save would send it twice. 32 marks in 20 files are left on a named list, each with what it waits on: a save in progress, a redirect in the browser, a busy mark beside a region that already speaks, a field's own options, or the sign-in service rather than this product's server (where the sentence would not be true). A loading line made of words alone and a read a button starts are not covered yet and are POST-A-449. A rule over every screen fails a new placeholder drawn outside a region, and a browser check holds the dashboard's hub and the ratios screen.
- **POST-A-381** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — When a person pressed Save over a date the box could not read (31/02/2026, a day the box refuses), the form copied the date's sentence into its own error line, and that line was cleared only by the next Save. So after the person put the date right, the red sentence stayed and said the date was wrong about a date that was right, and the person pressed Save again for nothing. That was 80 copies on 60 screens, among them the purchase bill and sales invoice editors and the GST, MCA, income-tax, payroll and DSC screens. The shared date helper now says the sentence through the form's own message and takes it back the moment the date is right; anything the form shows afterwards in the same place (the server's refusal) is never taken back. A rule over every screen fails a form that copies a date's sentence itself, with three named exceptions that recompute on every change and cannot linger, and the browser drive checks, on every form it opens, that the sentence goes when the date is corrected.
- **POST-A-454** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The client workspace offered a Reviewer six sections that refused them on arrival (Accounting, Bank, Inventory, Fixed Assets, Year End and Reports, whose first reads ask the books, the bank or the year end), offered a Manager and an Executive the Verify Books tab (its checks ask a Partner's approval permission) and offered a Reviewer the GSTR-9 tab (it computes GSTR-9 and GSTR-9C on opening, which asks the GST compute permission). Each section now carries the permission its first read asks, Reports is offered to someone who may read the books or the bank, the client menu lists only what the person is offered by the same rule as the firm menu's tiles, and the two tabs are shown only to someone who holds their permission; a person who reaches Verify Books by a link is told in a sentence why it is not theirs. A test reads each pair off the route it names and checks the page still makes that read; 5 of its 6 fail on the code before. The IFF panel needed nothing: it appears only after a GSTR-1 is computed, which asks the same permission.
- **POST-A-450** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The DSC register is read by every member of the firm, like the people register, and nothing on its screen said so; its recorded reason ("the practice's own tokens") was contradicted by the code, which refuses to delete a client while a certificate carries its PAN. The tracker now carries the people register's notice, the catalog reason is corrected, and Add, Renew and Correct are shown only to someone who may write certificates. Held by tests/test_the_register_says_it_is_shared_across_the_practice.py.
- **POST-A-451** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — Every door that names a person asked only whether they were a member of the firm, so an Executive not assigned to a client could be made the preparer of its return, mailed its name each night and handed a notification with no client on it. Now a member who cannot open the client is refused in words at the obligation assign route, a task's and a recurring task's create and update, a fee engagement's create and update and a notification's create; the nightly reminders and the task-escalation engine tell only people who can open the client and carry the client; the unread count counts what the list shows. - **POST-A-058** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 615) — A credit or debit note against an invoice or a bill that carried GST compensation cess (aerated waters, pan masala, tobacco, coal, motor vehicles) reduced or added to the value and the GST and left the cess untouched: the four note tables had no place to record it, so the customer's or supplier's balance, the two cess ledgers, GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B all kept the full cess of a supply the note had changed, and only the sales side was even named on the return. Each note now records the cess the way the invoice does (a percentage and a figure per unit, the amount worked out by the server), includes it in the note's total, posts it to the cess ledger and never to a GST one, and it reaches both returns. The return still names a note that carries no cess against an invoice that did, because only the CA can say whether that note should have. Not done: a supplier note against a bill whose credit was blocked moves the cess credit the way it already moves the GST credit.
- **POST-A-062** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 616) — The screen that lists GST input credit about to lapse under section 16(4) read every purchase bill of the months it covers, with every GSTR-2B line of those months, to find the few whose credit is held back, so it slowed as a client's purchases grew. And an invoice the supplier filed late, so that it appeared in a later month's GSTR-2B, was listed twice (once as a bill the supplier had not filed, once as a filed document with no bill) and its credit counted twice; it is now one row that says which month's GSTR-2B carries it and for how much, and anything that cannot be matched with certainty is still shown as two. A later document is matched only when it is dated in the same financial year as the bill, because an invoice number repeats from one year to the next. Nothing is claimed or moved between returns.
- **POST-A-057, in part** (16-11-2026; no migration; the job queue and the read-back stay open on the item) — The bulk GSTR-2B drop sends one file per request, so a download older than a reconciliation an earlier request had written replaced it, and the screen only said so afterwards. - **POST-A-078** (first of its open parts closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — The 26Q built from the books read posted purchase bills and advances only, so a deduction typed on the TDS screen was named on the statement and left to be keyed from the register by hand, although a typed deduction that names a supplier had already lowered that supplier's later bills' withholding. The statement now carries each typed deduction as a deductee row (payee, PAN, section, date, payment, rate, tax and the s.197 certificate it was made under, each as the door stored it), says on the row that it was typed, fills it from the quarter's challans in the same date order the register's own readers use (so the statement and the register agree about which deduction a challan paid), and compares only the purchase bills' and advances' tax with the TDS Payable account, reporting the typed tax beside it because no journal stands behind it. - **POST-A-092** (first left item closed 16-11-2026; migration 620 is new and applies to production on merge) — A document whose stock effect the CA had already put right by hand (a stock adjustment, a count correction) stayed on the Inventory tab's list of documents whose stock was not all recorded for ever, because the list shows what the stock rows show, and Complete the stock would have moved its goods a second time (the button asked first and warned, and that was all). The CA can now mark such a document as settled by hand, with a reason they type: the list stops offering it, Complete the stock refuses it in words until the mark is taken off, the marked documents are listed with the reason, who marked them and when, and each mark can be taken off after a question. - **POST-A-167** (part, 16-11-2026; no migration) — The AS 11 revaluation run recorded no audit event, and nor did the routes of capital work in progress, party credit apply, cost centres, income-tax worksheets, AIS, reconciliation findings and purchase orders and goods receipts, whose tables carry no row-level audit trigger (185 firm tables do not; the trigger also writes only for a signed-in person). - **POST-A-208** (part 4, 16-11-2026; no migration) — The firm-level GST and income-tax trackers read `compliance_calendar` (and the income-tax one inserted into it) from the browser, so `rbac()` never ran, the assignment scope was row security's alone and the insert wrote no audit event. `GET /api/compliance/calendar/tracker?family=gst|itr` reads every page, firm-scoped and narrowed to the caller's clients, and `POST /api/compliance/calendar` adds a pending row for the caller's own client with an audit event and a sentence for a duplicate; the Add buttons need `compliance_record` write. Each tracker still shows the calendar and says so. 13 server tests (a mock-mode double of the table), 3 web guard tests; moving the trackers onto the obligation store is decided against for now and stays on the ledger.
- **POST-A-437** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — A month whose share of a positive year's depreciation figure is nil (a year's figure under twelve paise, or a purchase month pro-rated to nothing) posted nothing and left posted-through where it was, so the next month was refused for ever and the range runner stopped there; and a straight-line year's floored figure left (cost - salvage) mod life paise for a later year of their own. Such a month is now PASSED: posted-through moves to it with no journal, by compare-and-set on what the request read; the range runner lists it under `months_passed` and goes on; a reversal of the month after it goes back to it where an earlier journal or the opening position shows the run reached it (a nil month a first posting foreclosed is not taken as passed), and a passed month can itself be reversed. The last year of a straight-line life (less than a year's figure and fewer paise than the years of life would be left after it) takes what is left, so the asset reaches its salvage value at the end of its life. The projection passes a nil month as a posting does, and its `rounds_to_nil` ending is gone. Held by 7 new mock-mode tests (all 7 fail on the previous code) and one rewritten pin of the old trailing paisa.

- **POST-A-434** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 635) — A depreciation post that read the asset before a whole reversal ran (claim, journal reversed, marker cleared) and wrote after it found the row as it had read it and wrote its month over the reversal, so the register counted a month the ledger did not. Migration 635 adds `fixed_assets.register_version` and a BEFORE UPDATE trigger that adds one whenever the accumulated depreciation, posted-through, the reversal marker, the disposal, the accumulated-depreciation ledger pointer or the soft delete changes (a name or note edit does not). The post's register write (and a nil month's pass) is a compare-and-set on the version it read and carries the new one forward for a run's next month; on a miss it re-reads and keeps the month when the register now holds it (the duplicate post) and takes its journal back with a 409 otherwise. The API reads the version only where the row carries it, so it runs ahead of the migration. Held by 3 real-Postgres tests (2 fail without the version condition). Left: the reversal's own claim and the disposal and correction writes are not conditioned on the version (the figure and pointer compare-and-sets they already carry stand).
- **POST-A-075** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — The offered unforeseen income took the s.112A exemption off every transfer and carried no surcharge. `domain/income_tax/unforeseen_offer.py` now uses the annual exemption once across the year's listed-equity gains in date order (a loss in the section nets in its place, and the offers add up to the tax on the year's net gain) and adds the surcharge from the FY-versioned registry's bracket and capital-gains cap where the caller gives the year's total income and the client is an individual, HUF, AOP or BOI (a firm, LLP or company is offered none and told why); a year the registry does not hold keeps the register's figure and is told so in the `fy_rate_gap` sentence, a held unverified year applies and says so. Held by 11 new tests and one corrected fixture. On 17-11-2026 the panel gained the box: an optional "Total income for the year" read by the one rupee parser, where blank sends nothing (so the server offers no surcharge and says so) and is never read as nil, text that is not an amount is refused in words, a changed figure clears the offer, and each offered gain shows the surcharge it includes. Held by 8 browser tests and one Python test that reads the parameter name off the route.
- **POST-A-069** (closed 16-11-2026; no migration) — Form 3CD clauses 33 and 40 were plain manual clauses. Clause 33 now reads the CA's own entries on the computation workspace (`tax_deduction_claims`) grouped by the section each names, with claimed, allowed and pending counts (a year with none recorded answers nothing and says that is not "none claimed"). Clause 40 composes the Profit & Loss for this year and the preceding year (through the reporting service, so the caller's scope applies) and the stock ledger's closing value: turnover is the income under "Revenue from Operations", gross profit is turnover less cost of sales, net profit is the P&L's own, ratios in basis points and absent where turnover is nil; material consumed and finished goods produced are named as a gap (the books record no consumption or production) and no figure is computed; a year with no ledger entry, and a stock ledger holding no item, is named, never nil; a CA's own answer for either clause is shown where nothing was derived. Held by 10 new tests tying to the real P&L builder to the paisa. The register is now 10 derivable clauses; clause 35 stays manual and the screen's own columns for these two clauses are the form's, not recorded here.
- **POST-A-083** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 640) — The payroll ledger accounts were found by name in six places that did not agree: the accrual, a leaver's settlement and the salary payment by a pattern, the 24Q tie-out and the salary challan register by the exact name, and the ESI and professional-tax register by another pattern, so a renamed account was found by one and missed by another, and a settlement used a looser pattern ("Salaries") than the accrual. Now there is one vocabulary and one resolver for the client (the client's own account, then the firm's; key first, then the old pattern; two that could each be it are refused with both names), all six read through it, a new firm's chart carries the keys, the missing Employee Loans account is on the standard chart, and migration 640 stamps the keys only where exactly one account matches (and takes the salary TDS account off the §194 TDS key). A guard fails any payroll account named outside the vocabulary.
- **POST-A-082** (narrowed 16-11-2026; migration 641) — Payroll opening positions are built (see its ledger line for what is left).

- **POST-A-440** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 650) — What the ISSUED invoices bill against a sales-order line is held by the database. The API's check (`check_invoice_lines_against_orders`, asked on save and again at issue) reads and then writes in another request, so two drafts issued at one moment could each read the other as unissued and together bill more than was ordered. Migration 650's `order_lines_billed_beyond_what_was_ordered` takes the named order lines' row locks (FOR NO KEY UPDATE, in id order) and then, in a new statement, sums what the billing invoices (not deleted, not draft, not cancelled) carry, refusing with 23514 and a sentence; triggers call it when an invoice starts to bill (the issue inside `issue_sales_invoice_atomic`, an un-cancel, an un-delete), when lines are written on an invoice that bills, and when an order line's quantity goes down. The issue route words the refusal (`InvoiceBillsMoreThanItsOrder`) and posts nothing. `order_cycle.over_billing` stays the rule; `tests/test_650_order_line_billing_parity_pg.py` runs twelve scenarios through both, proves the second of two issues in flight waits and is refused, and after the rollback the same race bills 12 against 10. Not held: two DRAFTS saved at one moment can still each claim a quantity (the issue then refuses the second), and an over-billing written before the migration is not re-judged until something on it changes.
- **POST-A-418** (closed 16-11-2026; migration 651, shipped with the code, whose readers read every lot until it is applied) — A lot recorded on FORM GST ITC-04 can be retracted. `POST /api/sales-cycle/challans/{id}/returns/{lot}/retract` (the invoice-write permission, the client named, an audit event, a reason that may not be blank) is a soft delete: the row stays with the day, the person and the reason (`retracted_at`, `retracted_by`, `retraction_reason`; a CHECK and a final-once trigger), written by `retract_challan_lot_atomic`, which takes the challan's lock and, when the lots had marked the challan received back and the live lots no longer bring every line back, puts it back to issued with no back day so the s.143 clock runs again (a challan marked back by hand, with no lot or after a part lot, is left alone with its day) and the order is derived again. Every reader leaves a retracted lot out: the statement and its Table 5A (which now carries each row's `lot_id`), the record door's room check, the database function's room and stamp, the challan's received-back day, the cancel guard (the trigger of 585 restated) and the order; a challan whose every lot is retracted can be cancelled, and the refusal now says to retract the lot first. The Table 5A rows carry a Retract button for a person who may write invoices, with a reason box and a sentence that the product does not know whether the period's ITC-04 was furnished. NOT DONE, by finding out: the product records no ITC-04 filing (no table, obligation, route or status; the statement is prepared here and furnished on the portal), so a retraction cannot be refused once a period is filed; recording that filing would be a new tracker, and what it should lock is for the owner. Tests: `test_651_a_lot_recorded_on_itc_04_can_be_retracted_pg.py` (10, real Postgres), `test_a_lot_recorded_on_itc_04_can_be_retracted.py` (9, all fail on the previous code) and a web guard of 2.

- **POST-A-462** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — Where a firm has a Partner approve every manual journal, the journal list's bulk Delete offered a member who may not approve the posted entries the server then refused one by one. It now reads the server's own answer for the person (`may_post` from `GET /api/accounting/journal-approval`, the function the delete door asks): when it is false the Delete acts on drafts only, counts posted entries as skipped, says why in its confirm, and is not offered for a selection of posted entries alone; while the answer is being read, or could not be read, every entry is offered and the server decides. The bulk Delete is the only door in the browser that deletes a journal.
- **POST-A-464** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — Every screen that writes a file in the browser (29 components calling `downloadCsv` or `XLSX.writeFile`) was read for a failed read written as figures. Two shapes did it: the client P&L, balance sheet and cash-flow CSVs wrote a period whose read failed (kept as a column tagged `error: true`) as 0.00 under that period's heading, and the payroll year-to-date export wrote twelve months of nil pay when its payslip read failed (the CTC export was saved only by its empty list). Each now refuses in a sentence naming what could not be read (`lib/export/unread`), and the two payroll exports are disabled on the read's own error. `scripts/an-export-does-not-write-an-unread-figure.test.ts` holds the rule over every such screen. After the review of 18-11-2026 a failed read also leaves nothing on screen to export: the receivables ageing, the trend sheet and Schedule III clear their result when a read starts, the GSTR-3B line drawer and the recurring list clear theirs on a failure, the audit log, HSN library, risks CSV and client compliance calendar withhold Export after a failed read, and the shared table withholds both its exports while it shows an error; the guard's rule C states it over every screen that writes a file (what it does not see is named in its header).
- **POST-A-465** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — The employee edit now says at the box, before anything is sent, that a PAN, UAN, ESI number, bank account number or IFSC on file is corrected and not removed: both doors that edit an employee's identifiers (the employee drawer's Profile and the Add/Edit Employee form) mark an emptied box red with `aria-invalid` and the server's sentence beneath it, and the Save says the same sentence without sending. The list and the reason are `lib/payroll/employeeIdentifiers`, held word for word to `models.payroll.EMPLOYEE_IDENTIFIERS` and `EMPLOYEE_IDENTIFIER_WHY` from the Python side; the server stays the authority and refuses the same edit whatever is sent.
- **POST-A-463** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — The Rule 37 report took a bill's whole tax as the credit availed on it and netted each purchase note's whole tax against it, so tax a bill's lines had marked blocked under CGST Act s.17(5), posted to the cost and never credit, was shown as due for reversal, and a note against blocked goods took credit off the bill that had never held it. The report now reads the creditable tax of the bill and of each note (the tax less its `ineligible_itc_*` heads, migrations 240 and 685), the way the journals post it, and shows each bill's blocked tax beside its reversal. 8 new tests, all failing on the code before. Cess is not in the report, as before.
- **POST-A-057, the read-back** (17-11-2026; migration 715; the job queue stays open on the item) — The bulk GSTR-2B drop's answer for each file lived only in the browser that sent it, so closing the tab lost what the drop did, and a refused or unmatched file left no trace. The first request of a drop now opens a drop on the server and every later request names it; each file the server answered is kept with its status, sentence, GSTIN, month, client, counts, problems, caveat and what it replaced (the client's name is read when it is read back). The GST page lists "Your recent drops" for the person who made them, newest first, a row for a client they can no longer see saying nothing about it, and says that a file the browser could not read never reached the server. A drop that is not the caller's is refused before any file is touched; a deployment without the tables reconciles as before and says the drop was not recorded. A file the server answered whose record could not be written, and the files of a request that failed after its drop was opened, are counted on the drop (`unrecorded_files`) and the read-back says how many; a drop with nothing answered and nothing counted is not listed. The page names the files it could not record, below the table, and invites a drop only from someone who may make one (review of 18-11-2026). Both tables are written and read by the API's privileged client only. 37 new tests (21 mock, 2 route, 7 real Postgres, 3 browser source, 4 browser unit).
- **PRE-A-004, slice 3f** (mock-mode part, 17-11-2026; the line stays for the walk on the real stack and what is left; no migration) — Four empty or wrong pictures on the demo firm. (1) The seeded payroll recorded only the TAN and sent no ESI fields, so every employee was flagged insured with no IP number (the ESIC return listed eleven people above the ceiling and the exceptions list named all twelve) and the ECR and ESIC panels named the establishment and employer codes missing: the fixture now decides ESI coverage from the ceiling `domain/payroll/statutory` holds for the year (none for a year it does not hold), held to `routers/payroll._compute_slip` by a test, gives the insured employee an IP number, and records the EPF code for both payroll clients and the ESIC code where somebody is insured, each marked SEED. (2) The salaried individual keeps a Schedule S salary worksheet before her return is computed, and a worksheet whose salary box differs from the computation's is a finding. (3) The partnership that 'demonstrates section 194T' had no partner: two partners are credited each posted quarter, each credit a section 194T deduction through the TDS door (the engine's tax) and a journal carrying that tax to TDS Payable, before the GST returns and the TDS stage. (4) The reverse-charge client's unregistered supplier was unrecorded, so no section 31(3)(f) self-invoice was ever due: it is recorded unregistered (other unregistered parties stay unrecorded) and its self-invoice is issued through the screen's door. Also fixed: `POST /api/accounting/accounts` answered 500 in mock mode (the store was handed the model's keys); after review it also refuses a blank code with production's 422 and keeps `is_active`, the group, sub-group and caption. The review's fixes: the 26Q note, the keying sheet and both return screens no longer say no journal stands behind a typed deduction (the seeded 194T journal contradicted them); they say nothing records which journal, if any, carries one. The salary-worksheet finding says when the answer carries no figure, and states figures in rupees; the 194T tax line keeps the partner's name as written. Tests: three new files and new tests in the body-shape, income-tax-stage and seeder-run tests. NOT done: the real-stack walk (owner list section 3 row 11), a composition or GSTR-8 client, the section 31(3)(g) payment voucher.
- **POST-A-386** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — Every date box no row of the date drive covered is a row now: the windows and filters of WINDOWS (a ledger's, Worth a look, a vendor statement, the Bank Book, the match finder, the shared period picker and table filter, the ageing as-at, the portal statement, the time export) and the practice's own records in EDITORS (the DSC tracker's three forms, a lead, a task three ways, a document's expiry, a health override on both screens, a renewal, the portal's needed-by), plus the NRV review's posting date that came after the count; Time Tracking's hours range and the Reports P&L have scenarios of their own, all under the three zones of the matrix. The guard now fails any file that renders a DateInput and is driven nowhere. The drive found one defect, fixed: the Reports P&L cut its rows in the browser, so an unreadable To drew a nil statement; its window is now the server's and an unreadable or blank date is refused on Generate.
- **POST-A-126** (closed 17-11-2026, corrected 18-11-2026; no migration) — The date field's known gaps. A day and month typed with no year beside another date they may fall either side of (a notice's response date, a settlement's payment date, an export's shipping bill, a Form 15CA) were read in the anchor's financial year, a year off near a year end; those four fields now say `near`, and the short form is read in the year nearest that date (the years tried are near's own, and only within half a year, so a far 29/2 keeps its refusal), without claiming a side. A document's expiry, a health override's expiry and a portal request's needed-by keep the financial-year reading: they are nearly always ahead, and nearest-to-today read 15/3 typed in June as the March gone. The rule that a Clear emptying a date box remounts it is a guard over every inline onClick, with three reviewed exceptions.
- **POST-A-158, the rest of the uploads** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — Every file the browser sends now goes through the one upload door and its screen says how much has been sent and, once the last byte has left, that the server is reading it (never that it is done): the portal's shared document, the supplier invoice read for a bill, the debit and purchase credit note attachments, the statement's column read and mapping check, a bank line's attachment, a tax notice's file, the firm logo and a statement workbook shared to the portal. The GST/2A, TDS, payroll and trial-balance imports send rows as JSON, not a file.
- **POST-A-266, the EMI calculator and the currency preview** (narrowed 17-11-2026; no migration) — The loans page's EMI calculator is the server's answer (`GET /api/accounting/emi`, exact decimal, accounting read), and a foreign sales invoice's or purchase bill's INR total on screen is the save's own head-by-head HALF_UP conversion, pinned to the Python function by shared vectors, so neither is a browser estimate any more.
