- **POST-A-516, the PF wage ceiling by wage month** (closed 19-11-2026, night; no migration) — The three Rs 15,000 ceilings (EPF wages, the employer's EPS wages, EDLI wages) are one notified figure now, read from a dated table of notifications by calendar month (`statutory.PF_WAGE_CEILINGS`, `pf_rates_for_month`): Rs 15,000 up to August 2026 and Rs 25,000 from October (S.O. 5109(E) of 17-09-2026, from the e-Gazette's own copy, graded read), taken by the slip, the Statutory Deductions projection and the ECR route alike, so a Rs 40,000 member's contributions are 3,000 / 2,083 / 917 with EDLI and the administrative charge at 125. September 2026, the month the change falls inside, keeps Rs 15,000 and is named in the run's statutory gaps at creation and at release (S43): no text read gives the rule. Verified: 35 new tests, an oracle written apart from the registry, a property over every wage and month, six mutants that each fail them; the whole backend suite passes (33,589 passed, 6,936 skipped without a database; two tests that need DNS fail in this sandbox and on main). NOT done, for want of a text: the administrative-charge percentage and the insurance rate (S49), the age-58 rule (S44).
- **POST-A-517, the ECR's contribution columns** (closed 19-11-2026, night; no migration) — Column 7 is the employee's contribution alone, column 8 the pension share and column 9 column 7 less column 8, as EPFO's own sample and return statement have them (1,800 / 1,250 / 550); the file used to carry the employee's share and the employer's PF half together in column 7 (2,350) and 1,100 in column 9. The build refuses, by member, a slip whose employer EPF half is not column 7 less column 8, `totals()` carries the employer column and account 1 as columns 7 and 9, the hand-off shows the three columns under EPFO's names, and a stored filing's meaning is said beside it (no real filing exists). Verified: EPFO's sample line is reproduced figure for figure; 11 tests fail on the previous code. NOT settled: whether the portal tolerates a one-rupee rounding difference on a line, and whether its file has changed to Form VII's fourteen columns (S48).
- **POST-A-518, the PF filing walk-through and the hand-off's two acts** (closed 19-11-2026, night; migration 769) — The walk-through follows EPFO's manual in its order (the upload page, the checks, the Return File ID and the employer's irreversible approval, the Due Deposit Balance Summary, the challan and only then the TRRN, then the payment), the specimen TRRN is 15 digits, it no longer says there is no OTP or DSC (the manual's two notices are not settled by it) or that a month with no members needs a NIL return (the FAQ's Direct Challan Entry), and the hand-off records the RETURN with its own Return File ID (`epfo_ecr_filings.return_file_id`, additive) and says the payment, with its TRRN, bank and receipt, is a second act this product does not record. **A defect found on the way and fixed:** the hand-off raised AttributeError for every run of a client once any return was recorded for it (`f.get` over dataclasses); it now shows the month's latest approved return when the month needs nothing further. Verified: 16 new tests and a real-Postgres proof of the migration and its rollback; the walk-through and hand-off tests were re-pinned and fail on the old code. NOT built: recording the EPF payment (owner list: the EPF deposit record), which waits on a statutory reading.
- **POST-A-519, the ECR download's name** (closed 19-11-2026, night; no migration) — `ECR202609.txt`: letters and digits only and a small-case extension, as EPFO's upload notice asks, from one function used by the ECR response, the hand-off and the browser's fallback. Whether the portal really refuses an underscore is not known from the manual. Verified: 5 tests and two mutants.
- **POST-A-525, citations of the replaced provident fund schemes and Acts** (closed 19-11-2026, night; no migration) — Every screen string and statutory comment that cited a paragraph of the 1952, 1995 or 1976 scheme as the authority now cites the 2026 scheme's paragraph or says the old one was replaced (the deposit and upload paras 24(2)(ix) and 28(3), the joint option para 9(4), the pension share paras 4(1) and 7(1)), the journal narrations and the payslip name the Code on Social Security, 2020, the payroll screens no longer name the EPF Act or the ESI Act, and a rule over the tree (`tests/test_no_screen_or_statutory_comment_cites_a_paragraph_of_a_replaced_pf_scheme.py`) fails a new one: it found 29 passages in 13 files and 9 web passages in 4 files on main before this. Sentences the new text contradicted were corrected, not re-pointed (the election notice no longer says the reading is unverified or asks for an allowance). **Left, on purpose:** the sentences `one_time_earnings` serves still cite EPF Act s.2(b) and ESI Act s.2(22) for which earnings count as wages, because the Code's s.2(88) is a legal reading that waits on a person (POST-A-524); the gratuity labels (POST-A-526); and the compliance notes keep the old paragraphs where they describe history.
- **POST-A-526, gratuity under the Code: (a) the name and what is carried over, (b) a fixed-term employee** (closed 19-11-2026, night; migration 770; the open remainder is POST-C-132 and S50) — (a) The settlement line, the module and the screens name section 53 of the Code on Social Security, 2020 in place of the repealed Payment of Gratuity Act, 1972 (repealed from 21-11-2025, section 164(1)), and every settlement that includes a gratuity says in words that two figures are carried over from the repealed Act because the Code leaves them to a notification or a definition: the ceiling of Rs 20,00,000 (section 53(3), "such amount as may be notified") and the base of basic pay plus dearness allowance (section 2(88)). **No figure moved.** (b) An employee can be recorded as on fixed-term employment (`payroll_employees.fixed_term_employment` and `fixed_term_end_date`, migration 770, additive and nullable; NULL for everybody who exists, NULL and FALSE compute alike, and a check allows an end date only beside a fixed-term employee), and where the term EXPIRES the five years are not needed: one completed year under the contract is enough, a part of a year over six months counts as a year, and the amount is pro rata (the second proviso to section 53(1), the third proviso to section 53(2), rule 33(1)(a) of the Social Security (Central) Rules, 2026). "Expired" is stated by whoever records the settlement (a box shown only for a recorded fixed-term employee) or read from the recorded end date having been reached; neither is inferred from a short service. **Not built, on purpose:** a fixed-term employee who resigns or is dismissed BEFORE the term ends stays on the five-year rule and the reason says why, because section 53(1)(b) needs five years for a resignation, the second proviso waives them on the "expiration of fixed term employment", and rule 33(1)(a) names one year under the contract without naming expiry; the text read does not say how the two fit (S50 question 3). **A first version was wrong and was changed before it left this machine:** it waived the five years for every recorded fixed-term employee whatever the reason for leaving, which built on the Rule's sentence alone; the Code's text ties the waiver to expiry. Verified: 43 new tests (the limbs, the open reading, the settlement, the Statutory Deductions screen, create, update and withdraw, and a real-Postgres proof of the migration, its check and its rollback), a web test of the form's rules, and fifteen deliberate breaks that each fail them. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-507 (the findings), the public site's accessibility, and a measurement that kept nothing** (closed in part 20-11-2026; no migration; the speed, the human checks and the unread Lighthouse audits stay on the ledger line) — The first two runs of the marketing quality pass measured the site and kept no report: the upload step named `apps/marketing/.qa/`, a hidden directory, and `upload-artifact` leaves hidden files out by default, so the run's own log said "No files were found with the provided path" and the artifact promised for 30 days never existed. The three screenshot uploads of the smoke walk and the other-browsers drive named `apps/web/.smoke/` the same way with `if-no-files-found: ignore`, so the pictures of a failed walk would have been lost without a word; all four now set `include-hidden-files: true`, and the first run on the branch kept a 7.6 MB artifact. The job's own log now prints one line per failing element (the rule, the element, and for contrast both colours and the ratio), because the artifact host is not reachable from every place the work is done. The findings fixed: the word-by-word headline put an `aria-label` on a span (17 `aria-prohibited-attr` on the home page; it says its text once in a screen-reader-only span now); the hero's figures put a paragraph inside a description-list group (now term and description only); white text on the accent button blue was 4.34:1 (the blue is #5272c2, 4.62:1, in the header, mobile menu, hero, demo form and shared button); the 12px uppercase labels in slate-400 on white were 2.5:1 (slate-500); the section eyebrows at 50% were 3.4:1 (60% on dark, 65% on light); the demo page's step numerals, the products page's dark label and the resources table's headings were between 2.2 and 4.4:1 (raised); and the resources table's scrolling box could not take keyboard focus (it does, and is named). Verified: the third run on the branch scans 0 rules broken on all sixteen page and window scans (home, access, demo, pricing, privacy, products, resources and support, at a desktop and a phone window), Lighthouse accessibility 95 on the home page and 98 to 100 elsewhere, layout shift 0.000 on all; 85 Python tests that read the site, type check and lint. NOT verified: keyboard focus visibility on every control, the phone layout beyond the scanned window, that the colour changes read as the same site, and the home page's speed (71, 88 and 91 on three runs of the same page). _Refs:_ POST-A-122, apps/marketing/components/motion.tsx, apps/marketing/components/cinematic.tsx, .github/workflows/marketing-quality.yml
- **POST-A-515 (c), a verified code no longer rebuilds the page under an admitted person, and the assurance's reads settle latest-first** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration; this closes the whole of POST-A-515 and, with it, the platform page's own gate, POST-A-528) — When a code was verified (a first factor enrolled on Settings > Security), the sign-in service announced MFA_CHALLENGE_VERIFIED and the page's assurance was reset to "not read", which the guard draws as nothing, so the page was unmounted and mounted again and what was typed was lost. The announcement now keeps the known value when it is "nothing owed" (the session is already admitted; a verified code only raises the level), for the same person, and still resets and re-reads where a challenge is owed (the login page's flow) or nothing was read. An independent read of the diff found no admission path, and two unheld premises, both held now: the handler always starts a fresh read after the keep-or-reset decision (a source guard), and `readAssurance` goes through the latest-wins gate (an older read that answered late wrote over a newer one; a late "nothing is owed" over a newer "a challenge is owed" admitted the person for the seconds until the newer read answered). It also found the claim too wide: the password proof has run on a throwaway client since POST-A-512 and emits nothing on the main one, and the platform page (the step-up) had its own gate that re-checked on every session change and drew a spinner in place of its body, so it was not cured by this; that gate now stays open while it is read again (POST-A-528: a re-check that says no takes the page away at once, one that fails shows the error state; three source guards, two of them held on the old page by a negative control). Verified: the decision's tests (the kept and not-kept cases, both tokens), the two new source guards, the full web suite (5,204 tests), type check. NOT verified in a browser: the stand-in sign-in service does not emit the event. _Refs:_ POST-A-493, POST-A-512, POST-A-528, apps/web/lib/auth/assuranceCarry.ts, apps/web/lib/auth/AuthContext.tsx
- **POST-A-527, sixteen browser list reads that dropped the shared pager's error** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration) — `selectAll` and `selectAllKeyset` hand a failure back as `{ error }` and do not throw, so a read that took `data` alone drew a refused read as an empty list and a partial one as the whole list. Found while reading the quiet catches (POST-A-515 (d)): four client pickers had a `catch` that never ran, and sixteen more sites had none. The vendor payment form said "This vendor has no open bills. The whole payment is recorded as an advance." for a failed read; the receipt form offered nothing to allocate against; the bank settlement modal listed no customers, vendors or open documents; the customer import took every row for a new customer (a failed read stops the import now, before anything is sent); the four note editors' "Against invoice" and "Against bill" pickers were empty; and the fixed-asset form's vendor bills, the year-end adjustments' accounts, the bills-of-entry accounts, the recurring journal's accounts and the knowledge base list each drew nothing. Each now says what could not be read and that it is not a party with none. `scripts/pagerReads.ts` with `scripts/a-pager-read-reads-its-error.test.ts` holds the rule over the tree (44 pager reads, none drops its error; negative controls and a floor; twelve pins). Verified: the full web suite (5,235 tests), type check, and 210 Python tests that read these files. NOT verified: in a browser, with any read failed in turn. _Refs:_ POST-A-515, apps/web/scripts/pagerReads.ts, apps/web/lib/supabase/selectAll.ts
- **POST-A-515 (d), the quiet catches around a server call, read one by one** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration; (c) stays on the ledger line) — 97 catches around a server call told nobody anything (counted on 19-11-2026, never read). Read on 20-11-2026: most were a prefill or a lookup that leaves a screen as it was, and thirteen places drew a failed read as a fact, each now fixed. The client accounting dashboard printed ₹0 for revenue, expenses, profit and cash when the profit-and-loss or cash-flow report did not answer (a dash and a sentence naming the report now; a genuine zero still prints as ₹0; the profit is unknown unless both revenue and expenses are known). The payroll Outputs tab said "No payroll runs yet" for a failed read, and drew nothing for an EPFO filing order it could not read. The Shared Reports list said "No reports shared yet" for a failed read. The payroll declarations and statutory screens, the Schedule III screen and the receivables ageing offered an empty client picker for a client list that could not be read (and the pager does not throw, so the catches meant to cover it never ran). The copilot kept a thumb lit for a rating never stored. The onboarding checklist stayed on the old state after a saved step whose re-read failed. The bank reconciliation statement, the payroll bank advice, the account deletability answer and the retainer services list vanished when their read failed. The firm calendar left out every MCA form when their read failed. The Team access grid drew a member whose answer did not come against their role's template and now names them. The client-portal page told a person whose invitation failed to be accepted only to ask for one. `scripts/quietCatches.ts` with `scripts/a-quiet-catch-around-a-server-call-says-why.test.ts` holds the rule over the tree: a catch around a server call tells, hands the failure on, records it, or says in a comment why it does not (830 examined; the 21 that told nobody anything and gave no reason were fixed or now carry one, none left); negative controls for each shape, a floor that fails if the scan stops recognising catches, and ten pins. Verified: the full web suite (5,223 tests), type check and lint, and 584 Python tests that read these files. NOT verified: in a browser, with any call failed in turn. Found and recorded, not fixed: twelve more list reads drop the pager's `error` (POST-A-527). _Refs:_ POST-A-515, POST-A-527, apps/web/scripts/quietCatches.ts, apps/web/lib/accounting/dashboardFigures.ts
- **POST-A-505 (the rule), a narrow handler that substitutes a constant for a failed read says so** (closed in part 20-11-2026; no migration; the reading of the frozen handlers stays on the ledger line) — The sweep that made a swallowed fallback say so was one file list wide, and its guard read `pass` and `continue` bodies in eleven named files; a narrow handler whose whole body was a constant (`except ValueError: return None`) sat outside both it and the tree-wide ratchet, and a new one went unseen. `tests/test_a_narrow_handler_that_substitutes_a_value_says_so.py` states the rule across `core`, `domain`, `jobs`, `repositories`, `routers` and `services`: such a handler carries a comment of ten characters inside it saying why, or reports through `capture_soft_failure`. The tree held 152 (in 147 functions), frozen by file and function in `tests/fixtures/narrow_handler_baseline.json`, which only shrinks. The 45 that substitute a number or a flag rather than `None` were then read: six moved a report or a deduction onto a fallback and now report through `capture_soft_failure` (the customer's receivables ageing, the vendor's payables ageing and the unapplied advances aged a document they could not date as zero days old, which is the current bucket, through one shared helper `days_old`; a stored TDS rate that could not be read became 0%; a stored bonus percentage became a bonus of nothing; a joining date kept the twelve-month projection), and the rest are coercions of a typed column or documented tri-states, left frozen; 146 remain in 141 functions. An independent read of the branch then found the rule blind to `return -1`, `return []`, `return {}`, `return ()` and `name = -1` and to `middleware/` and `models/`; it sees them now, and the eight handlers it newly saw were read (four report, four carry their reason), with the baseline unchanged. Verified: the rule fails when a new one is added (shown with a probe file), the five spellings and the comment, short-comment, reporting, computing and broad cases; the six reports, each driven with an unreadable value (the two statement ageings and the advances report through real rows, a garbage date still counted and reported). NOT done: reading the ~100 that return `None`. _Refs:_ POST-A-021, apps/api/tests/test_a_narrow_swallow_is_triaged.py
- **POST-A-501, the signing page misnamed a body the bound cut off** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration) — The public signing page read a response body with `.json().catch(() => null)`, so a request that ended while its body was still arriving (the 45-second bound aborts the stream too) was told as "not the product's own answer", the sentence for a gateway's page, where the cause is that the answer did not finish. Both reads (the letter's load and the acceptance) go through `jsonPlain` now: a page that is not JSON says that, a body the bound cut off says it did not answer in time, and a write still says it is not known whether the signature went through. Verified: a new test builds a response whose body fails midway with a timeout and reads it through `jsonPlain` (a timeout sentence for a read and for a write; the other sentence for a page), and a pin test holds the page to it (two of three fail on the old page). NOT verified: in a browser against a real cut-off body. _Refs:_ POST-A-470, apps/web/app/sign/page.tsx, apps/web/lib/api/failureSentence.ts
- **POST-A-520 (the three notes), what the portal checks and which rate** (closed in part 20-11-2026; no migration; the idea of reading the portal's list stays on the ledger line) — The EPFO panel built a correct ECR and said nothing of what the portal still depends on. It now says, in plain words and with no claim of what the portal will accept: mark on the portal the exit of everyone who has left before the upload (EPFO's FAQs, question 23, say unmarked exits leave members treated as active and may block future filings); compare the file with the portal's own Active Member List, which the product cannot see (FAQ question 9; manual page 6); and which contribution rate the file was built at, said from the rate the payslips deducted at, so the upload form's rate box can match it (manual page 8). No figure or date is stated beyond that rate, and none of the three is said for a run with no file (an independent read found the panel saying "built at 12%" beside "no ECR to build"). Also from that read: a stored bonus percentage such as 4.35 was read as 4.34% by floating-point arithmetic and gave a bonus base a basis point short (now exact), and the report of an unreadable percentage no longer names an employee's pay. Verified: the hand-off tests (289 with their neighbours), including that no sentence promises acceptance, that the rate is said in integer arithmetic and that a caller that passes no rate gets no sentence. NOT verified against the live portal; the sources were read on 19-11-2026. _Refs:_ POST-A-517, POST-A-525, apps/api/domain/payroll/handoff.py
- **POST-A-504, the employee portal of a deleted client** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration; a decision taken and recorded) — The employee door never asked the client, so an employee of a client the practice had deleted kept signing in, reading their payslips and leave and filing their tax declaration, while the client portal already refused a contact of a deleted client. Decided under the three tests: the employee is refused at the door, in the same words as every other refusal there; an archived client is untouched, as for the client portal. The cost is said: such an employee asks the employer for a payslip. Reversing it is one condition. Verified: 10 new tests with the door over a fake of the two tables (six fail against the old door); the neighbouring employee-portal tests (two seed the client row now). NOT verified: against a live sign-in. NOT done: the invitation of a deleted client's employee still binds the identity (the door refuses it afterwards). _Refs:_ POST-A-447, apps/api/core/portal_auth.py
- **POST-A-076, the Form 3CD register as a printable working paper** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration; the portal's JSON stays on POST-C-094) — The register had no print, PDF, spreadsheet or JSON output. It now leaves as a PDF or a spreadsheet from `GET /api/income-tax/form-3cd/export`, built by the register's own function: every clause with its number and heading, the answer and whether it came from the books, was recorded by the CA or is not answered here, and a table of the basis of each answer (or why there is none), headed by the practice and naming the client, with a note that it is a working paper and not the portal's form and that nothing is filed. The page has the two buttons and says the file shows what is saved. An independent read of the first version found five faults (an answer typed over several lines printed run together, a cleared box counted as an answer, one long answer made the page layout fail as a 500, a control character in an answer made the spreadsheet a 500, and two note sentences were wider than the code), all fixed with a failing test each; the spreadsheet fault was latent in every report and is fixed in the writer. The screen now shows a `_paise` figure as rupees and a `_bps` figure as a percentage, as the paper does. Verified: 42 Python tests over the real service on seeded books (the PDF text carries every clause number and heading and the clause 34 figure in Indian grouping; the spreadsheet reads back; a long answer is a row per line; an empty or over-long register is refused; five mutations, one per finding, each fail 1 to 4 of them), 3 web pins and the screen renderer's tests (run on their own), the whole web suite (5,223 tests, run before the pins were added) and the type check. NOT verified: in a browser against a live deployment; the paper's layout was read as page pictures of one seeded client, not of a real one. NOT done: the portal's JSON (needs the e-filing schema, POST-C-094), the 2026-27 successor form (POST-C-096), a CSV. _Refs:_ rm/tds_income_tax-26, IT-11, apps/api/domain/reporting/form_3cd_document.py
- **POST-A-515 (a) and (b), the buttons that said nothing, and the rule** (closed 19-11-2026, night; no migration; (c) and (d) stay on the ledger line) — Seven places now say what happened, in the server's own sentence: the GSTR-8 panel's four Remove buttons and the GSTR-4 annual panel's four (one helper that catches, reads the answer and shows it; a refused delete used to leave the row and say nothing), Detect Matches (a 403 for an Executive, and what a press found), Restore version in the knowledge base, the recurring-journal history (a read that failed is no longer drawn as an empty list), the onboarding task's status, and the bank's Undo and Book under (how many stayed and the first reason). `scripts/silentHandlers.ts` is the rule over every button, `scripts/a-button-that-saves-tells-the-person-when-it-fails.test.ts` holds the tree and nineteen controls, and `scripts/silent-handlers-baseline.json` lists the two places read and found quiet on purpose. Run over the old code of the seven, the scan flags each. NOT verified: in a browser. **The ledger said "Detect Matches" had been found already handled; it had not (the catch dropped the answer), and the scan found it again.**
- **POST-A-514, the health address names the build** (closed in part 19-11-2026, night; no migration; the owner's screenshot and the live look stay on the ledger line) — `/health` carries `build`: the commit Render built, or `SENTRY_RELEASE` when somebody set one, or `unknown`; read from the environment, so the route still touches no network. Four tests and four deliberate breaks that each fail them. The evidence read from outside is on the ledger line.
- **POST-A-510, the reset page's lost marker** (closed in part 19-11-2026, night; no migration; the live tries stay on the ledger line) — see the line: the page read the recovery marker after the library had cleaned it, and now asks a memory made before the library starts. Eight tests of the pure memory, six of its wiring and four deliberate breaks of that wiring. Driven in a real Chromium against the drive's stand-in sign-in service on the night of 19-11-2026: with the fix a recovery link with no earlier session reaches the new-password form and an ordinary signed-in visit with no link is refused; against the old code the same block fails (the form never appears), so the fault was real and the drive catches it. Not driven against the live sign-in service.
- **POST-A-485 and 497, a sign-up link the server keeps** (closed 19-11-2026, night; migration 771) — A sign-up link used to be the sign-in library's own emailed one-time link: the first opener won, so a mail scanner that opens links before the person does, a second click, or the link opened on a phone after the laptop each left the person on a page that said it had expired; and what the link MEANT (a new practice, not the one this address already belongs to) lived in a note in the browser that had sent the form, so a link opened in another browser entered an existing practice silently. The server keeps the link now, the way it keeps a staff invitation. `public.signup_links` holds one row per address (the HASH of the secret, never the secret; the firm name and the person's name typed on the form; when it ends; the counters of how often it was asked for), the form asks the server, which mails the link itself to the address typed in the product's own name (the subject is fixed, the only typed text in the body is the firm's name, cleaned and escaped between quotation marks, and the person's name is not in the mail), and the link opens a page of ours, `/signup/continue`, which asks what the link is for WITHOUT signing anybody in and signs in only at a press, so a mail scanner that opens the page spends nothing. The press asks for a sign-in for the link's OWN address (never one a request names), writes the firm name and the person's name from the SERVER's answer into the browser before the session lands (so the intent travels with the link: an address that already has a practice is told so by the wizard, POST-A-480, in whichever browser the link is opened), and carries on to the wizard. Nothing is used up by a press, so a second click, a reload or the link on a phone all work, until the link expires (24 hours) or the practice is created (the create-firm route removes the row). Asking again replaces the link and the earlier one stops working; it is limited per address by the row's own counters (30 seconds between mails, five in an hour, so they survive a restart), and the write is a compare-and-set, so two requests that overlap cannot both be let through (the first draft wrote unconditionally, and the independent review sent twenty overlapping requests and twenty mails went), and per network by a bucket of the form's own (`signup_link`, so a sign-up does not draw twice on the budget the create-firm route spends) and the page's two calls by `invite`. A mail that did not go puts the earlier link back, only while that link is still the one that request wrote, and says so in words. The two names a stranger types are cleaned (control characters and invisible characters out, no more than 100 characters, never shaped like a web or email address). An address that already has a sign-in is signed in as the library's link did, because this link goes only to that mailbox (a staff invitation refuses an existing account because its holder could be somebody it was forwarded to); asking reads no account, so it answers alike for any address. A browser signed in as somebody else is asked before it is signed out, and is signed out (this device only, not every device) only once the sign-in is in hand, so a link that has just expired costs nobody a session; every press asks for a fresh sign-in, in a browser signed in as the link's own address too (a session kept from before a practice was closed is refused by the server, and carrying on with it left the link doing nothing); the page shows the firm's name and the person's name before the press; an address with no secret in it says it is incomplete and not that the link expired; and the sign-in guard no longer bounces a signed-in member from that page. Links that have expired are removed by the next request for one and by the daily sweep, so a stranger's typed address and names do not wait for somebody to ask again. The question that kept POST-A-497 open (does the project's redirect list match a link carrying a query) falls away: the link points at our own page and the library's emailed link is no longer used to sign up. Verified: 105 tests of the rules, the service (the compare-and-set against a double that speaks the database client's calls and against two requests that really overlap), the routes, the mail, the names, the daily sweep and the agreement of the browser's pattern with the server's sentence, with twenty-six deliberate breaks that each fail one; 24 of migration 771 against a real PostgreSQL (the checks, the one row per address, the statements the repository really sends, two real sessions that overlap and the unique index that refuses the second first request, the privileges, the rollback); 30 web tests of the page, its decisions, the sign-out reasons and the guard; and the page driven in a real browser against the drive's stand-in sign-in service (a link opened in a browser that never held the form says the address already has a practice, from the server's answer alone; nothing is signed in before the press; a browser signed in as somebody else is asked, is signed out only after the sign-in is in hand and on this device alone, and stays signed in when the link is refused; a browser signed in as the link's own address still gets a fresh sign-in; an address with no secret says so; a dead link offers a new one; the form asks the server and Supabase mails nothing). An independent review of the first version found ten things: eight were closed in this change, and two are named and left (a stranger who types a person's address can replace the link that person was just mailed, which the page now makes visible by showing the names before the press; and a mail the mail service accepted but the server saw fail leaves a dead link and a page that says none was created). NOT verified: on the live site (the browser drive used the drive's stand-in for the sign-in service), or against the live sign-in service's administrator call for a NEW address (a staff invitation uses the same call live, and the existing-address fallback is the employee portal's), and no mail was sent through the mail service.
- **POST-A-499, the people of a permanently deleted firm are told their firm is closed** (closed 19-11-2026, night; migration 772) — The purge deletes the firm's `users` rows (migration 141) and leaves the sign-in accounts, on purpose, so a stamp on the row ended nothing: a person who held a session reached "User not found in firm" and the new-practice wizard, signed out of nothing and told nothing. `public.closed_account_markers` is the stamp that survives the row: before the purge the API writes one row per member of the firm (the sign-in account id, the instant and how; a platform administrator is left out, because their access to the platform page survives the purge of their own firm by design) and REFUSES the purge, in words and with nothing deleted, if it cannot; if the purge itself then fails the markers are taken back. `get_current_user` reads the marker only on the path where an account has no member row, and answers a session that BEGAN before the purge 403 "Firm unavailable" (the words the browser already turns into a sign-out and a sentence), judged by the oldest authentication stamp so a renewed token does not walk back in; a sign-in made after the purge is not refused, so the person may start a practice with the same login; a marker that cannot be read or compared leaves the ordinary refusal, so a person mid-sign-up is never signed out by a failed lookup. The refusal ends THIS device only (a person who has signed in again from a new sign-up link on another device keeps that session when a stale tab elsewhere meets the refusal), and the account's marker is on the person-column guard's list as the sign-in id. Verified: 18 tests (the markers, the order, the take-back, the door, and that a member is never asked) with ten deliberate breaks that each fail one, and 12 of migration 772 against a real PostgreSQL. NOT verified: in a browser or on the live site (the "Firm unavailable" sign-out path itself is the one POST-A-482 drove).
- **POST-A-511, the sign-up screen's "Send it again" button came back after 30 seconds and the sign-in service wanted 60** (closed as moot 20-11-2026, with POST-A-485 and 497; no migration) — The line was about the button waiting 30 seconds while the sign-in service's own minimum between mails to one address was read as 60 (PRE-B-012), so a person who pressed it at 31 to 59 seconds was refused. The sign-up mail is no longer the sign-in service's: the form asks the product's own server, which keeps the link and sends the mail itself, and the button waits the number of seconds the SERVER names (30, its own rule, said in the same answer), so there is no second clock to disagree with. The sign-in service's mail is still used by the other emailed-link screens, and PRE-B-012 stays open for them. NOT verified on the live site.
- **POST-A-396** (closed 18-11-2026; no migration) — (1) Drive group `held-clicks` presses one button of each single-flight family twice in one tick (GSTR-1 Build, CA Approve and Confirm Filed; GSTR-3B Confirm Filed; TDS Compute, Approve and Confirm Filed; a client's Archive and Delete Permanently; a task's delete; a capital-gains record's delete; Accept on an AIS line; the house property worksheet's Save): 8 scenarios, all passing, all 8 failing on a build whose `<Button>` runs its handler without the flight. (2) The GST openers answer the routes' whole shape from `apps/web/scripts/drive/gst-answers.json`, written from the builders by `apps/api/scripts/write_drive_gst_answers.py` and held by `tests/test_the_drives_gst_answers_have_the_routes_shape.py` (3 tests). (3) The whole drive was run at a window 720 pixels tall: no hand-built overlay was out of reach; the 8 failures were the shared combobox's drop-down under the purchase bill editor's line picker (POST-A-471). The date group is cut in four (`SHARDS`) so a night still fits its allowance.
- **POST-A-477, the password rules** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — Every screen that saves a password (the sign-up wizard, the reset page, both portal activation pages and Settings → Security) shows what a password must contain (at least ten characters, a lowercase letter, a capital, a digit, a symbol), ticked as the person types, and checks all of it before sending; the sign-in service's refusal, which arrives with no error code and as a developer's string of alphabets, is worded in plain English from the groups it names, and the three screens that rendered the service's raw text now use the plain wording. The symbol list is the live refusal's own. The rule is a guard over what a page does, so a sixth screen cannot skip it. Verified: 4,9xx web tests pass, the new tests fail on the old code. NOT verified: in a browser, and the project's own password setting (POST-C-215).
- **POST-A-478, the Create Password step that came back** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — Saving the password made the sign-in guard unmount the wizard for a round trip and it mounted again at step 1, empty, with the password already set (a tester saved it eight times in eight minutes). The wizard's memory (password set, firm created, a verification code pending) now lives outside the component, in a module and in session storage keyed on the person, written before the wizard moves on and restored by one pure rule (`restoredStep`): step 2 once the password is set and the name is known, step 3 once the firm exists, step 1 with a Continue button when the name is not known. A same-password answer, on the direct path and on the emailed-code path, means "already set". The guard's fail-closed rule is unchanged. The independent review found no blocker and three real problems (a name lost on restore, a blank step 2 after the firm exists, the emailed-code remount), all fixed; the same remount on the other password screens is POST-A-493. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-512, a password change on Settings → Security that the project refused and the screen did not say** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The project's setting "Require current password when updating" was on and no password screen sent the current password, so the owner's change was refused with `current_password_required` and the screen showed nothing (the page had been rebuilt in the middle of the save). Settings → Security now proves the current password on a throwaway sign-in client that is never stored and never replaces the page's own session (`makeEphemeralAuthClient`, `verifyCurrentPassword`), sends `current_password` with the update, and says in words why a change was refused, including the two refusals the service gives for the current password, and a refusal set on a page that has since been rebuilt is remembered and shown (`passwordNotice`). It no longer signs the person in again to check, which ended the rebuild at its source and a side effect (a fresh session of the first assurance level, which for a person with an authenticator would ask for the code again). Verified: the new tests fail on the old screen; the whole web suite passes. NOT verified in a browser against the live service, and the other five password screens are untouched: the live checks with the setting on, and the unconfirmed code for a wrong current password, are on POST-A-510.
- **POST-A-513, the eye on every password box** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The sign-in screen had no way to show the password being typed, and on Settings → Security one setting drove three boxes while only the new-password box carried the eye, so the current password could show in clear text with no eye beside it. One shared password box (`PasswordField`) now draws the eye, an accessible button with a name and a pressed state, and each box owns its own "shown" setting. It is used by the sign-in screen (which keeps the browser's saved-password fill: the type changes only when the person asks), the portal sign-in, the three boxes on Settings → Security and the locked-statement password on the bank screen; a rule over the tree fails a password box drawn another way. Verified: the new rule and tests fail on the old screens.
- **POST-A-493, a password saved or a tab refocused no longer unmounts the page** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The sign-in library announces a sign-in each time a tab regains focus (and refreshes the token hourly, and announces a change after a password update), and the sign-in guard drew nothing until it had read the sign-in assurance again, so the page under it was unmounted and mounted again: what was typed was lost and a message set by a request in flight was set on a page that had gone. `AuthContext` now keeps an assurance it has already read when the announcement changes nothing about who is signed in: a token refresh or an account update for the same person, or a sign-in announcement carrying the very token it already holds (`mayKeepAssurance`, one pure rule in `lib/auth/assuranceCarry.ts`); any other announcement (a different person, a different token, no token, an assurance never read) resets it to "not yet read", so the guard's fail-closed rule is unchanged and the assurance is still read again after every announcement. Settings → Security re-proves the current password before saving, which opens a new session, so that page is still remounted by design; it now carries its "Your password has been updated." notice across the remount (`lib/auth/passwordNotice.ts`). Checked in a browser, both ways: with the drive's sign-in stand-in slowed to a real service's pace, the new-password box typed into on Settings → Security stays the same element with its text after the tab is sent away and brought back, and the saved-password notice shows; both fail on the code before the change and pass after. The password-reset page and the two portal activation pages save with an account update and are covered by the same rule (its tests and a source guard), but were NOT driven in a browser, and the one attempt showed a "link invalid" page before any save, which is recorded as POST-A-510. Not tested against the real sign-in service or a phone.
- **POST-A-487, every client picker shows the PAN and the GSTIN under the name and searches them** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration, no API change) — The switcher inside a client showed the name and the entity type only; it shows the entity type, the PAN and the GSTIN now (`lib/clients/clientLine.ts`, the one rule) and its box searches the name, the PAN, the GSTIN and the entity type. The firm-level "All Clients" lookups already showed and searched both where the list they were given carried them (Reports, Income Tax Notices); Receivables and Schedule III read `id, client_name` alone and now read the PAN and the GSTIN too. An identifier a client does not have is left out, never drawn as a value, and the GSTIN is the client's primary registration. NOT established: what the tester meant (fewer clients than expected, or details missing); the list route serves every active client to a Partner or a Manager and only the assigned ones to an Executive or a Reviewer, and no cap was found, so if clients were MISSING that is a separate defect for his exact words (on the owner's list).
- **POST-A-488, a way to add a client is in the client dropdown and the sidebar's Clients panel, for a member who may add one** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — Both offer "Add a new client" only where `can("client", "write")` (a Manager or a Partner, the server's own rule), and both go to the Clients page, which opens its form when the address says `?add=1` and takes the request out of the address at once. Decisions made differently from the plan: the dropdown's entry is pinned FIRST in its list and not at the foot, so a long list never pushes it off the end; and the form is the Clients page's rather than a second copy beside the dropdown and the panel (it holds the list the new client joins). Read in the code: the page drew Add Client, and Import CSV, for every role, and the server accepts a client only from a Manager or a Partner, so for an Executive or a Reviewer the button ended in a refusal; both are drawn only for a member who may now.
- **POST-A-491, the two screens that draw "Loading workspace…" say when it is taking longer and offer Reload, and a page whose files fail to load reloads once** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration; the cause was found afterwards and is POST-A-495) — After eight seconds the client workspace's entry page and the not-found page's redirect branch say "This is taking longer than usual" with a Reload button; a page whose own files fail to load (a tab opened before a new version was published asks for files that no longer exist) reloads itself once a minute at most and the second time says what happened (`lib/async/stuckLoading.ts`; it recognises a failed chunk in each browser's words and never reloads away an error in the page's own logic). Not reproduced and not tried in a browser.
- **POST-A-479, a link that lands already used** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The sign-up page keeps the address beside the names and, once the link is sent, says it works once and offers "Send it again" with a thirty-second wait; the page the link opens, when nobody is signed in, now says the link was already used or has expired and offers "Email me a new link" to that address (with the same wait), instead of making the person type everything again. A person already signed in who opens a used link in the same browser carries on, as before (the session is kept). The sign-in service's throttle on a REPEAT request (seven seconds, which arrives with the hourly limit's code) is worded as a wait and no longer as "too many emails in the last hour". The link that survives a second click and a mail scanner is POST-A-485, still open. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-480, signing up with an address that already has a practice** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — When a new-firm sign-up was started in this browser and the link signs in a person who already belongs to a practice, the page no longer sends them into that practice's dashboard. It says "This address already has a practice", names who they are signed in as (name, role, firm), explains that one sign-in belongs to one practice, and offers "Continue to <firm>" and "Sign out and use another address"; the new-firm steps are never offered to a member. A member who only visited the page, with no sign-up started, is sent home as before, and the firm this wizard itself creates never turns it into the notice. The decision is one pure function (`signupLanding`, unit-tested) and the sign-up page now says the link signs in to an existing account. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-481, a staff invitation asks for a password and a repeat is answered** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — A person who joins by an invitation link now creates a password (with the same rules and memory as the sign-up wizard) before the dashboard opens, because the link works once and without a password they could not sign in again. A repeat of accept-invite by the person who already joined (a second click, a reload, the link on a phone) is answered by the server with their own membership (`already_joined`), the page says they have already joined and offers to set a password or go on, and a caller with no membership still gets the one refusal for an unknown token; a suspended member is not told they have joined. The employee portal's activation page was read and already asks for a password and sends a repeat to the employee portal, so it needed no change. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-482, a suspended or soft-deleted firm's people are signed out** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration; a PERMANENT delete is NOT covered: POST-A-499) — The platform page's suspend and soft delete now stamp `sessions_revoked_at` on every member of the firm FIRST (the service client, naming the firm; a stamp that fails leaves the firm open and the request refused), count them in the answer and the audit row, and unsuspending restores nothing: people sign in again. A permanent delete deletes the member rows the stamp lives on and leaves the sign-in accounts, so it ends nothing and no longer says it does; its people reach the sign-up wizard, as before, and that is POST-A-499. The Team area's "Sign out all users" now asks first and says it includes the person pressing it, because it now really does. Reading the code showed the stamp alone would not have held, and the same was true of the Team area's own force sign-out: the API compared the token's `iat`, a browser renews its token whenever a call answers 401, and the renewed token's `iat` is after the revocation. The API now also refuses a token whose SESSION began (the OLDEST `amr` stamp, which a renewed token carries unchanged) before the revocation (`core/auth_time`), so a renewal no longer walks back in, an authenticator code entered afterwards on the old session does not bring it back (the independent review's case: the newest stamp would have read it as new), and a fresh sign-in does. The browser signs the person out at once on 403 "Firm suspended", "Firm unavailable", "Account disabled" and on 401 "Session revoked" (and on nothing else), through one guarded function, and lands on a signed-out page that says why in words that blame nobody and name no address. The database's own revocation check still reads `iat` alone: POST-A-494. NOT verified: in a browser or on the live site, and no live token has been decoded to confirm that renewal keeps the stamps (POST-A-500).
- **POST-A-484, the platform admin's authenticator proof** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — Suspend, soft delete and permanent delete now need an authenticator code entered in the last five minutes (the token's `amr` stamp for the code), not merely a session that passed the second factor once; a stale or absent code is refused 403 with one sentence that the platform page recognises (pinned across the two languages), and the page asks for a code, proves it and tries the action once more. The permanent delete proves a code right before it asks, as before. The router's opening docstring no longer says there is no hard delete. The second approver for a permanent delete is the owner's decision and was not built. NOT verified: in a browser.
- **POST-A-483, the session ends when the browser closes, and after thirty idle minutes** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The sign-in page has a tickbox "Keep me signed in on this computer", OFF unless ticked (up to 14 days, never longer, and an edited flag that claims longer is refused). With it off, a page that starts asks whether this is still the browser session the person signed in during (this tab's session storage, a ping answered by another tab of the browser, or a heartbeat any tab wrote in the last two minutes) and, if none says so, ends the stored session on THIS device before it is used; no web page can detect a browser close, so this is the honest reading of it, and a browser that restores its tabs on reopening restores session storage with them and cannot be told from an unbroken session. A signed-in screen left alone is signed out after thirty minutes (a key, a pointer, a touch or a wheel in any tab counts, shared through local storage) with a warning banner in the last minute and a page that says why. Decisions made differently from the plan, with reasons: the browser's own sign-outs end this DEVICE only (scope local) and do not stamp `sessions_revoked_at`, because the revocation now also ends a person's sessions on every other device (POST-A-482) and an idle laptop should not sign out the phone in their hand; an API call does not count as activity, because the app makes some on its own and a timeout they keep alive never fires. An explicit Sign out still ends every device, as before. The portal's sign-ins are covered too and have no tickbox yet. The sign-in service's own time limit and inactivity timeout are paid settings and the owner's money (POST-B-347). Thirty minutes and the default of signing out on close are the owner's to confirm. An independent review of the first version ran the real sign-in library against it and found it ended NOTHING, for two reasons now closed: the library's announcement of a session recovered from storage marked the tab, and the heartbeat started, BEFORE the start decision read them. The decision now reads what the page found when its script first ran, no marker or heartbeat is written until it has been made, a page that arrives from a sign-in link (the address carries the sign-in) counts as a fresh sign-in, and another tab of the browser is told by the Web Locks API (a frozen or sleeping tab still holds its lock and a closed browser holds none) before the ping and the heartbeat; the tickbox now names the person who ticked it, so nobody who signs in later by another door inherits it; a sign-out no longer waits on the API to record the logout (at most 2.5 seconds) and records it once; and the idle clock still runs when storage is blocked. Known edge, not closed: a browser without Web Locks (an old one) falls back to the ping and the heartbeat, and a tab frozen for more than two minutes in such a browser can read as a closed browser when another tab of the app is opened. NOT verified: in a browser; the logic is driven here against fakes of storage, the channel and the locks.
- **POST-A-489, a person's bulk pass of bank lines states the GST of the lines they coded, once per kind of line** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — Read first, as the item asked: a line the CA codes to an expense ledger in "Book under…" can carry GST, and every bulk pass sent no rate, so the line was posted with the whole debit on the expense and the input credit (CGST Act s.16) lost without a word, where the single line's screen shows a control. Now a person's bulk pass (`Pass selected`, `Pass all ready`; not the trusted-rule sweep) carries the answer for a kind of line, a rate or 0 for "carries none", which the server applies only to lines the person coded that can carry GST (`posting_map.gst_split_allowed`, the rule the queue's flag and the posting engine's refusal both ask); with no answer such a line is HELD, never posted: the row keeps a standing error saying so (so no later chunk reads it again and no list of ids travels in an address), it stays coded, and the pass's last chunk names every held line with its category and direction. The screen asks ONCE PER KIND (a category and money in or out), never one answer for all, because the independent review showed one rate for an expense, a loan instalment and a salary line would put input credit on the two that are not supplies; no choice is preselected, "no GST" is a choice, a kind left alone stays held, each kind is passed with its own answer in requests of at most 100 ids, and the result says how many were passed with GST. The row's Pass button on such a line opens that line's own screen. Decisions made differently from the plan: the question is asked when the lines are PASSED and not when they are booked, because nothing stores a person's answer between the two (the only GST column on a bank line is what was POSTED, and the draft columns are the machine's proposal), and a column for it would be a migration reviewed apart; "Book under…" says so in its note and result. NOT covered: a line a rule or the history drafts keeps the draft's own treatment (POST-A-496), and a line the answer cannot apply to (a document settlement, a split, a control-account category) is never given one. Proven on the in-memory double and the router; not on a real database or in a browser.
- **POST-A-492, a new bank account's ledger is created for it and says so, a ledger a bank cannot sit on is refused, and a failed creation refuses the save** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The route already created a ledger named "<bank> — <last four digits>" when the form named none (proved on the in-memory double: the account is linked to a Bank ledger of that name); the form's box said "— Not linked —" and told the person to link an account, so the two newest production accounts were linked by hand, and the picker offered every asset and liability ledger, "Cash in Hand" among them. The form's default now reads "Create a new ledger for this account — “HDFC Bank — 7890”" (the name comes from the same function the route creates it with, `account_kind.new_ledger_name`), the choices are the server's (`GET /api/banking/accounts/ledger-choices`: this client's and the firm's, never another bank account's, never a cash ledger or an income or expense ledger; an edited account keeps its own current one), the route refuses such a ledger on create and when an edit CHANGES the link, and an account whose ledger could not be created is no longer saved unlinked: the form stays open with its fields and says what to do (the earlier choice to save it unlinked, recorded in `_ensure_bank_ledger`, is reversed on purpose: an unlinked account's postings fall back and its balance sits nowhere). NOT established: why the automatic path was not seen working in production (no database error was found in the log for the owner's add at 16:50 IST on 18-11-2026, so the likeliest reading is that a person chose a ledger by hand because the form told them to); not tried on a real database or in a browser.
- **POST-A-486, devices on a login** (closed 19-11-2026; migration 765) — Each password sign-in and sign-out now records the address our own proxy wrote (a session found by the tab's own report, such as an invitation's, is recorded in the sessions list and not in the history), the browser and the sign-in service's session id; a login's live sessions (heard from in the last 40 minutes, the browser's idle limit plus its report interval) are listed on the person's Security page ("Where you are signed in", with "This device" marked and a button "Sign out my other sessions" that ends the others and spares this one) and, for a Partner or a Manager, on the Team login history ("Signed in now", members on more than one device marked, a Partner may sign a member out everywhere); a second device under a login that already has a live session puts an in-app notice in each active Partner's bell, once; the privacy summary says the address and browser people sign in from are kept (and still says it does not cover for how long). No cap on sessions (the owner's decision). NOT built: a place from an address (it would send the address to a third party), a hard cap (the sign-in service's paid setting, POST-B-347), and any purge of the stored address and browser, whose retention period is on the owner's list (S3). NOT seen live: POST-A-506. Tests: 39 or more in `test_devices_on_a_login.py` (the rule, the record, the notice, the sign-out, the older-database tolerance), two sentences held in the privacy guard, a real-Postgres test of migration 765, six web tests of the report timing.
- **POST-A-470** (closed 18-11-2026; no migration) — Every request a screen or a data module makes for itself goes through `fetchBounded` (`lib/api/failureSentence`), which gives up at `REQUEST_BOUND_MS`, the same 45 seconds `lib/api`'s `request()` uses (it now reads the same constant); 65 calls in 39 files were converted. Uploads, a model reading a notice or a supplier's bill, the GSTR-2B and Form 26AS files, the statement PDFs and the two firm-wide recalculations take `LONG_REQUEST_BOUND_MS` (5 minutes); the three editors' own uploads pass it to `fetchUpload` as `timeoutMs`. A request given up on says it did not answer in time and, for a change, that it is not known whether it went through. `apiCall` keeps no default (bulk imports stay unbounded) and the Supabase client's transport is untouched: both are named exceptions in `scripts/a-request-a-screen-makes-ends.test.ts`, the rule over the tree.
- **POST-A-036, the first real-client run and its scan** (narrowed 18-11-2026; no migration) — The client-workspace pass was run with the pre-installed Chromium: 44 screens, none broken, 44 distinct bodies. Its first accessibility scan found five defects and they are fixed (the client top bar's entity-type chip at 4.39:1, four icon-only Reload buttons with no name, grey empty-list text on the client's knowledge). The pass now runs the scan with screens keyed `/clients/{client}/…`, leaves the six named screens to the full walk, and its baseline is empty (43 pages, 0 failures). What is left (screens with rows, the night's time margin) stays on the line.

The ledger line carries the other lists' sizes as measured that day.
- **POST-A-119, the extractor and the demo form's runner** (narrowed 18-11-2026; no migration) — `_marketing_copy` reads JSX text broken by `{value}` and a `+` join of literals and values once more as one sentence with `…` where the value goes, and the claims ledger names it like any other (three source shapes tested); code between tags and a value at a sentence end add nothing; test files are not copy. The demo form's fake-clock and mailto tests moved from pytest (where they skipped without a TypeScript-capable Node) to `apps/marketing/lib/demoRequestWait.test.ts`, and a Python test holds that they are still there. - **POST-A-395** (closed 17-11-2026; no migration) — The thirteen leftovers of the date drive. (1) The engagement template and detail dialogs, the two Add Filing forms, the bulk Mark Filed prompt and the supplier form hold Close and Cancel while their request is out, and the supplier list's row Edit and Add Vendor while the form's Save is. (2) The engagement letters page's JSON helper and its PDF download are bounded at `lib/api`'s 45 seconds, so a held dialog is freed when a request hangs. (3) The dialogs' icon-only closes, a statutory band's remove and a capital-gains record's delete are named. (4) The New lot card scrolls (measured at a window 320 pixels tall). (5) Both Add Filing forms keep Save and Cancel on a window 700 pixels tall (measured). (6) Not reproduced: the narrowed bulk prompt already kept the date tried with; a drive row holds it, with the Cancel now held. (7) The advance-tax register says a date's sentence once. (8) The date box takes a value its parent changes before the paint. (9) A recurring journal's history opens the generated entry. (10) A client's MCA page prints its dates through the one date module. (11) as (1). (12) Closed already by POST-A-381, whose drive rows hold it. (13) A list row's menu near the foot of the window opens upwards, on all eight sales and purchase lists. Evidence: drive group `dialog-leftovers` (12 scenarios, 11 of which fail on a build of the tree before), `lib/ui/rowMenuPosition.test.ts`, `scripts/a-dialog-that-holds-close-is-freed-by-a-bounded-request.test.ts` and `components/ui/date-input.test.ts`. Not done: the page-local JSON helpers of about thirty other screens are not bounded (POST-A-470). **Reopened in part on 18-11-2026 and closed again on 19-11-2026:** the sweep that held 153 ways out of 63 files (`scripts/dialogHolds.ts`) and the `timeoutMs` bound `apiCall` takes had not been described in this entry, and the review of them found ways out still live or trapped (POST-A-473) and the bound given to calls it should not reach (POST-A-474); both are closed below.
- **POST-A-473, the dialogs the sweep left live or trapped are held, freed and held only over a write** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — The stock adjustment and NRV write-down dialogs, drawn inside the stock ledger's click-to-close backdrop, and the account editor, inside the account ledger, were closed with it by a backdrop click or by the ledger's Escape while their write was out: a hand-built backdrop now stops its click always and closes only when no write is out, and the two ledgers leave Escape to an open dialog. The vendor and customer Deactivate dialogs reset their flag after the await and not in a `finally`, so a thrown write (the 45-second bound) left both buttons held until a reload; both use try, catch and finally now. The Add and Edit Account ×, the fixed asset drawer's × and the portal access dialog's Done are held and named (the rule knows an × glyph and a button whose click is `onClose`). `savingOverride` and `busyId` (the health override and recurring journal dialogs) are matched as write flags. The capital-gains exemption dialog, the client documents' upload and the relationships' link were held over a READ and are held on their own write flag only. A write's flag is let go when the write returns, before the re-read (the landed-cost panel, the payment link). Not done: the rule cannot see an Escape handler installed in an effect or a write flag under another name, and the upload dialogs stay held over an XHR with no abort timer, by design. An independent review of the rebased change then found and closed four more: a late exemption answer landing in another record's dialog, the rule's lost `filing` flag (and that dialog's backdrop not stopping its click), the override dialog held through its re-read (a failed re-read read as a failed save and a second press posted a second override) and a landed charge removable twice. Evidence: four new drive rows that fail on the earlier tree (the builder's run of the whole drive, 748 of 748, was NOT re-run after the rebase onto main and none of the review's four was driven in a browser), the extended rule and its self-tests, and `scripts/a-late-answer-never-lands-in-another-dialog-and-a-failed-reread-is-not-a-failed-save.test.ts`.
- **POST-A-474, a bulk loop no longer carries the bound meant for a held dialog** (closed 19-11-2026; no migration) — `timeoutMs: HELD_DIALOG_MS` stays on the single-document writes a dialog holds over and is removed from every `apiCall` inside a loop (the receipt and note CSV imports' rows, the bulk actions' `Promise.all` and `mapWithConcurrency`, the recurring bills' run-all-due), so one slow row no longer throws out of the loop, reports a run as failed or as nothing done, and invites a duplicate re-run. - **POST-A-469** (closed 18-11-2026; migration 745) — The firm Reports page's P&L Statement added the taxable value of the feed's sales and purchase rows by party in the browser. `GET /api/reports/party-totals` (accounting read, the caller's own clients, the page's date window) now answers it: `public.report_party_totals` sums in the database, with `domain/reporting/party_totals.py` as its twin for mock mode and the minutes before the migration, and the screen draws the lines and totals as served. It groups on the party's id, not its name, so two customers of one name are two lines; a party with no name on record is said in words. Verified: 45 real-Postgres tests run both halves over eight windows and five scopes and compare the totals with the old browser arithmetic on the same rows; 4 service and 6 route tests; the page's own test holds the P&L branch to the server's figures. Every new test fails on the previous code.

A journal line takes only manual journals because an entry posted by a document names the same control accounts every time. The name-based state selects in Settings and onboarding stay native on purpose (they hold a state NAME, so the code-based picker does not apply). - **POST-A-471** (closed 18-11-2026; no migration) — The shared picker (`components/ui/combobox.tsx`, every lookup in the product) always opened its list under its box, so near the foot of a 720-pixel window the purchase bill editor's line product picker ran off the window. It now places its list by the row menus' rule (`lib/ui/rowMenuPosition.listPosition`): under the box, or above it when there is less room below than the list's tallest height and more above; aligned to the box's left edge, kept inside the window's width, and capped in height to the room on the side it opens, so it scrolls inside itself. Verified: 5 node tests (below, above at 720 px with every row inside the window, a window too short for either, the width, and a rule that the combobox places its list through `listPosition` and never by hand), all failing on the previous code; the purchase bill editor scenarios driven in Chromium at 720 px pass.
- **POST-A-131, phone-width flows** (narrowed 18-11-2026; the line stays for what needs a real phone; no migration) — Viewing and approving deadlines and approvals, opening a client's draft invoice to Issue it, and reading a client's outstanding were driven in Chromium at 390 x 844 (`scripts/drive/scenarios/phoneFlows.mjs`, 2 scenarios). The check is that no page scrolls sideways (the document and the shell's own scrolling areas, which is where the client workspace hid a 43-pixel sideways scroll from a first, document-only measure) and that nothing outside a box made to scroll sideways (a table, a tab strip) crosses the window's edge or spills out of its own box, and that each action is on the screen once scrolled to. It found and this fixed: the shared table's date-range filter ran its second box past the window (the two boxes now share the row on a phone); the shared table's toolbar did not wrap, pushing Columns and Export off the window; the Columns menu opened right-aligned to a button near the left edge, past the window's left side (across the window on a phone); the Sales tab's three summary tiles left each figure wider than its tile (stacked on a phone); and an approval's title was squeezed to its first letters (its actions now wrap under it). All 4 checks failed on the build before and pass after. Decided: the other 16 file inputs carry no `capture` attribute, because a phone's file chooser already offers the camera and `capture` would force it for a statement, a workbook or a notice. Not verified here: a real phone, its camera, touch and keyboard, and Lighthouse on the live site.
- **POST-A-124, customers and vendors** (second slice closed 18-11-2026; the line stays for the other lists; no migration) — A client's Customers tab and Vendors tab read their whole list again behind a placeholder on every return. Both now read through the shared read cache (`lib/data/partyLists.ts`): the last answer is painted at once under a line saying when it was read, the real read always follows, a failed real read leaves the painted list up and says it may not be current, and a failed read is never stored. Every browser writer of a customer (the customer form, deactivate, reactivate, delete, bulk import, bulk deactivate and delete) and of a supplier (the vendor form, its deactivate, reactivate, deletes and bulk import, the Suppliers page's save and status change) forgets its family right after its request, whatever the answer, and the Tally import and its rollback forget both. A rule in `scripts/the-read-cache-is-opt-in-and-never-feeds-a-write.test.ts` finds every write by its request (a write method to the family's API, the typed client's create or update, a PostgREST write on the table, the Tally import) and fails one not followed by the forget; only the two tabs read through the cache, and every picker and editor stays uncached. Driven in Chromium with the server held 3 s: both lists come back within 1.5 s under the line, which goes when the real read lands; after a deactivation the next visit is not painted from before it. Negative controls: with one supplier forget removed the rule fails; on a copy of the build whose forget does nothing for customers, the deactivation scenario fails.
- **POST-A-447, the portals and the table** (narrowed 18-11-2026; no migration; the item stays open for the live walk) — A soft-deleted client's portal contacts no longer resolve as members (the multi-contact rows and the legacy link, mock and real), so they cannot read its invoices, documents or compliance list or post messages. The staff Client Portal screen offers New Request, Delete request and Send Message only with Portal write, Upload with Document write, a file's Delete with Document delete and a shared report's Remove at the reports' write tier, and marks a thread read only for someone who may write; the client workspace's Portal tab offers Invite only with Portal write, and its Payroll section asks payroll read. - **POST-A-205, slice 3** (narrowed 18-11-2026; no migration; the item stays open) — The client books export now also carries the records beside the books (cost centres, advances and credits, loans, deposits and related-party loans, FX revaluations and adjustments, price lists, recurring templates, quotations and orders with their lines, delivery and job-work challans with their lines and returned lots, goods receipts, bills of entry, post-dated cheques) and, under the GST permission, the client's GST registrations, GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B returns with their JSON, GST challans, GSTR-2B lines and reconciliations, the ITC reversal register, reverse-charge documents and e-invoice and e-way bill records, every read naming the firm and the client; a Partner blocked from GST is not handed those files and is told in the manifest; the manifest, README, card and coming-soon row say what is still not in it. - **POST-A-021, fifth slice** (narrowed 18-11-2026; no migration; the item stays open) — Fourteen narrow `except ...: pass` handlers no longer move a figure onto a fallback in silence: an unreadable stored due date (collections, overdue status), base date (a draft's credit terms), filed date (a client's late-filing history) or run month (the payslip, the TDS return's slip date) is reported through `capture_soft_failure`, and an impossible date in a notice's text, a trigger's missing due date and a mock invoice number without digits are asked as checks. The two screens say what an update kept. See `docs/design-record/payroll.md`.

See `docs/design-record/payroll.md`.
