- **POST-A-399, fourth slice** (18-11-2026; the finding stays open, narrowed; no migration) — The bank matching rule's edit clears an emptied narration phrase, amount bound, proposed ledger, category, narration or GST rate (the form always sent null and the route dropped all but the rate), and refuses a clear that would leave the rule with no condition, nothing proposed, a rate with no ledger, or a trusted rule with no ledger. See `docs/design-record/frontend.md`.
- **POST-A-502** (closed 19-11-2026; migration 766) — The firm Reports page's P&L Statement ignored the four CGST §34 notes, so a month with a sales credit note showed more revenue than the books hold and a month with a purchase return more expense; the browser's own arithmetic had always done the same, which is why migration 745 moved it faithfully. `public.report_party_totals` now nets them as the ledger posts them (a sales credit note and a purchase debit note reduce, a sales debit note and a purchase credit note increase), each joined to the line of the party it names, counted when issued or applied and not deleted, by its own date and the caller's clients; a line can be negative where a note falls in a window its invoice does not. The answer gains `revenue_notes_paise`, `expense_notes_paise` and `note_documents`, the screen says how much of each total is notes and says nothing when the keys are absent (an API ahead of the migration), and `domain/reporting/party_totals.py` is the twin for mock mode. Verified: 48 real-Postgres tests run both halves over eight windows and five scopes with all four notes seeded (issued, applied, draft, deleted, another firm, another client, a credit note in a window its invoice is not in) and compare explicit figures; 15 mock tests, one of which reads each note's sign off the ledger's own journal function so a change in how a note posts fails it; a screen test; and three deliberate breaks (a flipped sign, a dropped table, a draft counted) each fail a test. Not verified in a browser. Not decided and not done: the figure is by document, as the page always was, not read from the ledger, so a manual journal never appears in it. _Refs:_ POST-A-469, apps/api/migrations/766_the_reports_pages_pl_statement_nets_credit_and_debit_notes.sql, apps/api/domain/reporting/party_totals.py
- **POST-A-494** (closed 19-11-2026; migration 767) — The database's own revocation check compared the token's `iat` alone, so a signed-out person's browser, which renews its token hourly, passed the database's row-level check again for the tables a signed-in session can still read directly, while the API had refused it since POST-A-482. `public.staff_session_is_live` now applies the API's own rule (`core/auth_time.issued_before_revocation`): live only when the token's `iat` and the OLDEST stamp in its `amr` are both at or after the revocation; a token with no readable stamp is judged by `iat` alone, as it always was, and one with no readable `iat` still fails closed. The oldest stamp, not the newest, because an authenticator code entered against an old session adds a stamp after the revocation and must not read as a fresh sign-in. Function only, derived from migration 468, its only definer; the three helpers and every policy are untouched. Verified: 19 tokens (renewed, new sign-in, a code after the revocation, a stamp at the instant and one second before, malformed lists, objects, string and boolean stamps, fractional stamps) judged by the database and by the API's function and compared; the rows of a real table, not only the claim, dark to the renewed token and open to a new sign-in; with 468's body restored 10 of those tests fail. Not tried against a live token from the sign-in service. Not done and recorded: the narrower "sign out my other sessions" mark is still the API's alone (POST-A-509). _Refs:_ POST-A-482, POST-A-486, apps/api/migrations/767_a_renewed_token_of_a_signed_out_session_is_not_live_to_the_database.sql, apps/api/core/auth_time.py
- **POST-A-509, the database's own check did not know "sign out my other sessions"** (closed 20-11-2026; migration 773) — "Sign out my other sessions" writes two marks on the person's own row (the instant, and the session that asked to stay), and the API refused a token whose session was not the kept one and began before the instant, while the database's own question (`public.staff_session_is_live`, asked by the three helpers every row-security policy depends on) read only `is_active` and `sessions_revoked_at`. So a session signed out that way was refused by the API and still read, through the database's own door, what a signed-in session can reach there (narrow: the browser reads no payroll, bank or document table since POST-A-010). The function takes the two marks now and applies the API's own rule (`core/auth_time.ended_by_other_sessions_sign_out`): live when it is the kept session (judged by its id first, so whatever its `iat` shows) or began at or after the instant, and still subject to a revocation of every session; a token with no readable `iat` that is not the kept session is not live. The token side (the `iat` and oldest-`amr`-stamp rule of migration 767) is one function asked for both marks. The three helpers pass the two marks and were derived from migration 468, their last definer; the old two-argument function was dropped once they stopped calling it. Verified against a real PostgreSQL: 595 tests, among them 288 combinations of token, mark and session id judged by the database and by the API's function and compared (the answer called directly is always TRUE or FALSE, never NULL), the rows of a real table dark to another session and open to the kept one, the privileges, running the migration twice and its rollback; 667 with the neighbouring database tests, run four workers wide as CI runs them; ten deliberate breaks of the SQL each fail at least one (two were missed at first, a token issued exactly at the instant and an `iat` one second before it, and the cases were added). An independent read of the migration found no input a sign-in service issues on which the database and the API differ; it found that rolling back migration 765 while this one is live would fail every helper call (both rollback files now say to roll this one back first), one path that answered NULL instead of FALSE (the function is total now), a token function that answered NULL for no instant, and two comments that said more than the code did; all were fixed, and the one difference left (SQL trims spaces where the API trims every Unicode whitespace, for a kept id only a hand-written row could hold) is written in the migration's header. NOT verified: against a live token from the sign-in service or on the live database. _Refs:_ POST-A-486, POST-A-494, apps/api/migrations/773_the_database_knows_a_person_signed_out_their_other_sessions.sql, apps/api/core/auth_time.py
- **POST-A-498, the invitation's repeat-accept answered a member whose session had ended** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration) — A person who has joined and presses the invitation's link again is answered with their own membership (POST-A-481), by a route that authenticates with the token alone because the caller may have no member row yet. It read the membership by the token's own `sub` and asked none of the refusals every other route makes, so a member whose sessions had all been signed out, whose session had been signed out from another device, or whose firm was suspended or deleted, calling it with any token string, was handed their own firm id, role and name instead of a refusal. The four refusals are one function now (a disabled account, a deleted or suspended firm, a revocation of every session, a sign-out from elsewhere), `get_current_user` asks it for every route behind it, and the repeat-accept asks it too, with the token's session claims (the JWT-only door hands over `iat`, `amr` and `session_id` and nothing else of the token). Not asked, on purpose: the second-factor level, because the answer is the caller's own membership, which `/me` gives at the first level too. Verified: 31 tests, among them 13 cases (a good standing, every session signed out with an old, a renewed and a new token, an unreadable revocation, the other-sessions mark with another, the kept and a later session, both marks, a deleted firm, a suspended one, a disabled account) sent through both doors with the same rows and token and the answers held equal, status and words; a sign-in that is not a member is asked nothing and refused as before; and the test that read `get_current_user`'s source for the other-sessions check follows it one level into the shared function. Nine deliberate breaks each fail at least one (one was missed at first, a sign-in with no member row asked of the function itself, and the test was added). NOT verified on the live site. _Refs:_ POST-A-481, POST-A-482, POST-A-486, apps/api/core/auth.py, apps/api/routers/identity.py
- **POST-A-503** (closed 19-11-2026; migration 768) — A deleted client's portal contact was refused at the API door (POST-A-447) but not by the database's own row-security policies: ten policies on four tables read the contact's membership inline (the contact table with status active, or the legacy `clients.portal_user_id` link) without the client's `deleted_at`, and `my_portal_client_ids()`, which the portal branches of migrations 440 to 443 are all built on, did the same, so a contact of a deleted client who held a valid session could read the client's portal messages and fee invoices and what hangs from them, and write portal messages and payment links, with the public key and their own token. `my_portal_client_ids()` now answers only for a client that is not deleted, in both branches (derived from migration 443, the last that declares it); a new `my_portal_contact_client_ids()` is the contact-table branch alone and the five `portal_contact_*` policies ask it; the five `portal_client_*` (legacy link) policies keep their inline subquery and gain the deletion test, deliberately not pointed at the function, so the migration tightens and widens nothing (a legacy-link contact's portal-message read is blocked by row security on `clients` before and after). All ten altered in place. An archived client is untouched, as at the API door. Found while testing it: shared reports, documents and document requests were never reachable by a contact over the direct interface (shared_reports by its restrictive assignment-scope policy, which has no portal branch; the other two since migration 551), so the ledger line's list of what a contact could read was longer than the truth; their policies are brought in line anyway. Verified: 11 real-Postgres tests as a signed-in contact (a live client's contact reads their message and fee invoice and posts; a deleted client's, by either link, reads nothing, posts nothing and gets no id back; a contact of two clients gets only the live one; an archived client is untouched; restoring the client gives the rows back; one rule over the catalogue: no policy reads the contact table, every legacy-link read tests the deletion, every function that answers who a contact is tests it); with the old function and policies 7 fail; the rollback restores the policy text and the function body exactly and drops the new function; the existing portal, guard and drift tests pass. Not tried against a live portal sign-in. _Refs:_ POST-A-447, POST-A-482, apps/api/migrations/768_a_deleted_clients_portal_contact_is_nobody_to_the_database.sql

- **POST-A-495, moving to the Clients, Health or Relationships list from inside the app drew the client workspace's "Loading workspace…" screen until a reload, and the address rule that did it is fixed** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration) — The cause was an address rule, not a stale tab (the owner reproduced it in a private window). Moving between screens, the app asks for a page's data file, which for the Clients list is `/clients/index.txt`; the rule that serves a real client's page data, `/clients/:id.txt`, matches it with the id "index", and Cloudflare applies a rewrite even where the export holds a file at that address, so the list was answered with the client workspace's data and drew the workspace's loading screen at the list's address. A reload asks for the page itself, which no rule matches, so it worked. It began on 17-11-2026, when POST-A-148 listed the `.txt` rule before the plain one: until then the plain `/clients/:id` rule claimed the request and answered HTML, which the browser ignores and then loads the page the ordinary way. `/health` and `/relationships` had the same shape. Reproduced on a local copy served by Cloudflare's own Pages routing code, where the list's file on disk and the file served differed. Fixed: the generator gives each static list page's data file a literal rule to itself, ahead of the placeholder (three rules, none against the 100-dynamic cap: 142 rules, 99 dynamic, the first at position 44). A new test asks, for every static page in the route tree, which rule is first to match its address, its page data and their bare forms, and fails unless each goes to itself; on the file as it was it finds exactly those three. Confirmed on the live site by the owner on 20-11-2026 after the update went out ("it works now, no more loading problem"), moving from a client to the Clients list; this container cannot reach the live site, and whether the Health and Relationships lists were tried was not said. Design record: `docs/design-record/screens-and-site.md`.

- **POST-A-530, every screen was audited for where it sits and what it draws while it waits, and every one now sits in the page frame and waits with a shape** (closed 20-11-2026; no migration) — The owner found Billing Schedules against the left edge of a wide window, waiting as one line of text, while a client's workspace was laid out and waited with placeholders. The audit was made as a measurement in a real browser and not as a reading of source: the browser walk opens every screen at a wide window with the server's answers held back, records what is drawn while it waits and where the screen's own container sits once the answer lands. The first run found 13 of 116 firm-level screens against the left edge and about 46 waiting as words or a spinner; a client's 41 screens were laid out, and 14 of them waited as words or as nothing. In four pull requests (#1187, #1188, #1189 and this one) every such screen took the one page frame (a screen of data fills up to 1600 pixels and is centred; a form keeps a narrow measure and is centred) and a placeholder in the shape of what it will draw. Both lists of exceptions are empty, a test holds them empty on every pull request, and the measurement runs every week and on demand and fails on a screen that is newly wrong. Four things found on the way are recorded with it: a count beside a list is blank while unread, not a second "loading" region; a wait on the sign-in service draws placeholders and not the sentence about this product's server; Fee Billing's dashboard no longer shows Rs 0.00 while its figures are read; and the calendar no longer draws empty days while a month is read. Not done: error and empty states outside the Practice menu were not put in the frame; the weekly measurement means a new screen can be wrong for up to a week; nothing was tried on the live site. Design record: `docs/design-record/frontend.md`.

- **POST-A-531, guided tips: pop-ups that explain a screen, a switch per person in Settings, and the server remembers which tips each person has closed** (closed 21-11-2026; migration 774) — The owner asked for the product to show a new CA round, "the way a game does", with a switch to turn it off. A tip is a small pop-up beside the button it explains, shown once to each person until they press "Got it"; "Next" walks on to the next screen of the tour; "Turn off tips" is the switch on Settings, under Your profile, where "Show every tip again" is too. Tips are on by default for every member of staff and each person's choice is their own, on every device. The first six tips are on five screens: the menu and search on the dashboard, adding a client, the deadline list, the door to the firm's own books, and billing a client on a schedule. Each tip is held to its screen by a test: the button it points at must exist on that screen, every label it quotes must still be written there, and its words may carry no figure, date or rate. The pop-up dims and locks nothing, does not take the keyboard away from what a person is typing, can be reached and closed from the keyboard, and waits while a dialog is open. Driven in a real browser (four scenarios, run weekly with the screen measurement, because the nightly browser run has no time left in its allowance). Not done: nothing was tried on the live site; whether each sentence is true beyond the labels it quotes was read by Claude and not by a practising CA; more tips, and the tip for the firm's own books moving to My practice, come with POST-A-532.

- **POST-A-532, My practice: the firm's own books, billing, payroll and compliance in one workspace, with the frame a client's workspace has** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The owner could not find where a firm keeps its own books on the live Test Firm, and asked for one place with everything a client's workspace has and more. Built in three parts and merged as one. (1) The frame: the menu entry reads "My practice"; the practice's own workspace and the fixed practice screens wear the client workspace's bar and grid, in two groups ("Your firm's books" and "Running the practice"); no address is new, because the live site has 99 of its 100 address patterns in use. (2) The firm's own staff payroll: the old rule "no payroll for the practice" is lifted and replaced by "the practice's own payroll is the Partner's alone" at every payroll door; the firm-wide payroll lists leave the practice's own staff out, and the attendance sheet, the leave list and the practice's own Payroll page show them when a Partner opens them for the practice. (3) Fee billing: the old Fee Billing screen is "Earlier fee bills", takes no write, and it and "Earlier fee receivables" moved from the Accounting menu to My practice; fees are billed from Billing Schedules, on the engine that reaches the books and GST. An independent review of the payroll part found five things, all fixed before merge; the worst was older than this change: the year-end pay report with no client chosen sent every client's staff pay to anybody with payroll access, whatever they were assigned to. Checked: the web and backend suites, eight browser scenarios (four for My practice), the layout measurement of the changed screens, and negative controls for the door rule and the review's fixes. Not done, each on its own line: the firm's own payroll dates are not on the compliance calendar and three firm-level payroll screens do not list the practice (POST-A-534); the year-end report reads its runs without paging (POST-A-535); Profitability does not yet count fees billed the new way, and the rest of the fee readers (POST-B-193). Nothing was tried on the live site.

- **POST-A-529, a signed-in tab is told that a newer version of the site is live, and loads it when the person next moves to another screen** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — Every merge republishes the site, and a tab opened before it kept the old pages until something failed. Each build now carries an id and the live site serves the same id as a small file; an open tab compares the two a while after it opens, every few minutes while it is in front and when the person comes back to it. When a newer version is live a small card says so, once: "A newer version of PracticeSync is ready". The new version loads by itself the next time the person moves to another screen, or when they press "Load it now", and never on a timer, so nothing typed into a form is lost. A read that fails says nothing. If a cache still serves the old version after the load, the tab does not reload again at every move. Checked: the rule's own tests, a test of the wiring, the web suite, and two browser scenarios (told once and only by a real answer; loads once at the next move). Not done: whether the live site serves the id file can only be checked after this is deployed; the client and employee portals and the sign-in pages are not told, because they load fresh each visit. The working rule that goes with it is on the owner's list: no merge to the live site from the evening before the demo until it is over, except an urgent fix.

- **POST-A-533, the first-day card walks a Partner through the firm's own practice, and sign-up asks the firm's legal form** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The card's four steps were all about clients, so a new CA could finish it without learning that the firm's own books exist. A Partner's card now has a second list, "Your own firm": check the firm's own details, start its own books, import its own bank statement, raise a first fee bill. Each ticks itself from the firm's own records and opens the right screen of My practice; a Manager's card is unchanged, because the firm's own practice is the Partner's alone. The four client steps no longer count the firm's own fee bill or bank statement as a client's. Sign-up's firm profile now asks "Your firm's legal form" (it was recorded as a Partnership for every firm until somebody corrected it) and says what the PAN is for: the firm's own books are set up from it. Both stay optional, so a CA without the PAN to hand can still finish sign-up. Checked: the server's rule and facts (16 new tests), the browser's reader, and a browser scenario of the card. Not done: the sign-up form itself was not driven in a browser; nothing was tried on the live site.

- **POST-A-535, the payroll reports read every run and every payslip, however many there are** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The year-end pay report, its list of years and the table of payroll runs each read their rows in one request, and the database answers at most a thousand rows to a request without saying there are more. Past that the year-end report added up part of the year, the list of years lost years and the table lost months, with nothing on the screen to say so; a firm with a hundred clients reaches that in under a year. The ids of the runs also went into one filter, which fails outright once there are a few hundred. All of these reads now go through the shared pager, the list of runs is sent in pieces, and a rule holds that the report service and the route that lists the runs send no request of their own. Eight of the ten new tests fail on the previous code. Not done and in the ledger: the sums are still added up in the API from the year's payslips, so a large firm's report is slow though whole (POST-A-536).

- **POST-A-537, the People screens open inside My practice for a Partner, the firm menu lists less, and a click on an area opens it** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The owner walked the live Test Firm the morning My practice went live and found four things. Clicking Team inside My practice threw him out into the firm's navy frame; that was a choice of the first build (a Manager uses those screens too), and it was wrong for the person My practice was built for. The firm menu showed thirteen areas at once. A click on an area in the menu did nothing. And the first row of My practice's list appeared late, so the list jumped. Decided with the owner: the firm is three rooms (Clients, My practice, People); work is delegated from the Partner to the Manager and from the Manager to the team, so Managers keep the team screens and never see collections or the firm's profit. Built: for a Partner, Team, Assignments, Approvals, Workload and Login History are a People group inside My practice and open in its frame, with no separate Team entry on the outer menu; a Manager sees the same screens from the Team entry as before; the outer menu lists Home, Clients, Deadlines, Work, Team or My practice, and AI, and puts Accounting, Payroll, Relationships, Health, Knowledge and Engagements under "More" with a line about each; a click on an area opens it; the row for the firm's books keeps its place while it loads; Time Tracking is in Work and no longer listed in My practice. Nothing was removed and no address changed; who may open what is still decided by the server. Checked by tests that work out each role's menu from the server's own permission table and prove no role lost anything, and by three new browser scenarios (a Partner on Team, a Manager on Team, the menu). Not done: the AI guide that replaces the tips (POST-A-538).

- **POST-A-539, the professional-tax page says what it is for and states nothing nobody has confirmed** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The owner was shown Settings, Tax and statutory values on the live Test Firm and read it as a backend screen a CA can do nothing with. Two of its sentences also stated things nobody had confirmed: that twenty-two states levy professional tax (the list is the product's own and probably one or two too long) and that Article 276 makes the employer liable (a legal reading never checked against the text). The second also appeared in the note on a payroll run for an employee in a state with no slabs. The page now opens with what it is for (a state that is not built in deducts nothing, and the run says so, until the firm records that state's slabs here), counts only the states the product itself holds, and says the list of states is the product's own and unconfirmed. The run's note says nothing was deducted, that this is a gap and not a finding that nothing is due, and where to record the slabs. The server no longer calls the built-in states "verified against the state Act". No figure, slab or deduction changed: this is wording. A test holds the rule over the page, the note and the server's sentence, and over every screen (none cites Article 276); 22 of its 24 tests fail on the old wording. The three questions this leaves are on the owner's list under S9, for a practising CA.

- **POST-A-538, a "Where is…?" guide replaces the guided tips** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The owner tried the guided tips on the live Test Firm and asked for a guide a person can ask. A "Where is…?" button now sits on the top bar of every screen: a person types what they are looking for and is shown up to three screens, each with a sentence saying where it is on their own menu and a button that opens it. The AI chooses only from the list of real screens the person's role is offered and writes nothing itself, so it cannot point at a screen that does not exist, describe a feature that does not exist, or send a Manager to My practice. Before the question is sent the box says which provider reads it and that it leaves India; a PAN or GSTIN typed into it is replaced first; nothing from the firm's records is sent. When the AI is not set up, over the firm's allowance, rate limited or down, the same box does a plain word search and says so. The guided tips, their switch in Settings and their pop-ups are removed in the same change. Verified by tests on both sides and in a real browser against a stand-in server; it has not yet been asked a question by the real AI, so how well it picks is for the owner to try on the live site. The two unused columns the tips left behind are POST-A-541.

- **POST-A-542, what the role walk found on the Team screens** (opened and closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — The product was walked in a browser as a Partner, a Manager, an Executive and a Reviewer, each with that role's own permissions. The menus were right for every role. Four things were wrong and are fixed: the Team screen offered "Invite Member" to a Manager, who cannot invite; when the list of members could not be read the screen showed four zeros and "No team members yet" beside the error; an Executive or a Reviewer who typed the address of Team, Assignments, Approvals, Workload or Login History was shown the screen drawn around a refusal, and is now told in words that the screen is withheld; and the notice a non-Partner sees on My practice still used the area's old names. Not a blanket guard: an Executive's menus hide the firm-level tax desks although the server lets them read their own clients there, and whether to show or refuse those is a product question left open. The browser harness can now sign in as any staff role with that role's real permissions. Verified with stand-in data only; nothing was run against the real server.

- **POST-A-541, the two columns the guided tips used are dropped** (closed 21-11-2026; migration 775) — Migration 774 added two fields to each person's record for the guided tips: whether they wanted tips and which they had closed. The tips were replaced by the "Where is…?" guide the same day (POST-A-538), which removed everything that read or wrote the two fields, and the owner asked for them to be removed. Migration 775 drops both and checks its own result; its rollback puts them back empty. What is lost is each person's tips choice, which means nothing without the tips; no firm's or client's data was in either field. A test holds the premise the removal stands on: no module the server runs names either field. Merged only after the guide's change was live, because a server still offering the old tips against a database without the fields would have answered that screen with an error.

- **POST-A-490, an invoice line can be entered from a price including GST** (closed 21-11-2026; no migration) — A tester asked for it and the owner said to build it. On a sales invoice, a small calculator beside each line's rate opens a box: the person types the price including GST, for the whole line or for one unit, and the server works out the unit rate before tax from the line's quantity, GST rate and discount. The box shows the server's own sentence and figures, and nothing changes until the person presses "Use this rate"; the line is then an ordinary line, so the tax, the books, GSTR-1 and the PDF are untouched. Where no rate reaches the typed figure exactly (a rate is a whole number of paise and the tax on a line is rounded down) the box says "Nearest rate, not exact" and by how much; the difference is never hidden. A line with compensation cess is refused in a sentence. Verified by comparing the answer with what the real invoice route stores, over a grid of prices, quantities, rates and discounts, within a State and across States, and in a real browser against a stand-in server. Not done: purchase bills, credit and debit notes, quotations and recurring invoices do not offer it yet.
